# azapptoolkit: comprehensive project review

*Repository tiredithumans/azapptoolkit at commit 74ad3e4 (v0.30.2), reviewed 2026-09-26.*

## How to read this report

The review ran 26 first-pass slices that together covered every source, documentation and CI file in the repository, then a completeness critic named six lenses the slices had not applied and six gap slices swept those. Every finding cites a `file:line` with quoted evidence. Every finding was adversarially verified by an independent agent that re-read the code, and the high-severity bug and security items were re-verified a second time. Two judges then ranked the survivors, one for operator risk and value, one for engineering leverage, and the lead session spot-checked 12 of the 14 high items directly in the code (see Appendix C and the self-verified notes). Environment limits: the desktop (Tauri) crate could not be compiled in the review container because WebKitGTK is absent, so its roughly 300 inline tests were reviewed statically; the shared crates' tests (591), the frontend host tests (200) and the wasm type-check all pass. Verdict legend: **confirmed** means the claim holds as stated; **partially** means the claim is true but overstated or mis-cited, and the entry carries the verifier's correction on its verdict line. Ids F001 to F491 are stable and searchable across every section; a merged entry names its sibling ids on its verdict line.

Findings produced: 491 · surviving verification: 490 · confirmed: 359 · partially confirmed (with corrections): 131 · refuted: 1

## Executive summary

azapptoolkit is a mature, unusually well-documented codebase with an explicit safety contract (never under-report, never cache a degraded run, coverage is never overstated, a partial success is a real write) and a source-scanning invariants suite that pins many of those rules. Its strengths are real and consistent across slices: the pure decision cores (Exchange `verdict`/`targets`/`aap`, the LRU+TTL cache with its store-after-invalidate guard, the resource-aware scoping gates, the audit scorer) are fail-closed by construction and heavily table-tested; the Graph transport has one retrying core with verb-derived retry classes and same-origin guards; auth hardening (PKCE, state, nonce, tid/oid re-binding, chunked keyring, AADSTS redaction) is careful; IPC parity is exact at 186 commands; and the frontend has a single-writer open-items design, strong a11y foundations and a well-reasoned GUI harness. The dominant problem is results that mislead in the reassuring direction: a whole family of RBAC-scopable mailbox permissions scores as healthy, the 5000-site sweep and a swallowed SP lookup are cached as complete, backup Pass 3 thins silently, partial AAP migrations and vetoed one-click fixes toast success, and a restore that duplicates the estate is offered on screen as the re-run remedy. Second come documented controls that do nothing: the auto-update opt-out is never read, the install format is never checked, and the Linux artifact cannot start on Ubuntu 22.04, Debian 12 or RHEL 9. Third are session and auth edges: a domain-form tenant id the config screen accepts can never sign in, `interaction_required` purges the whole session, sign-out fails open on a keyring error, and CAE-ness is missing from the token cache key. Fourth are engineering-debt clusters: three cancel flags shared across run kinds that the keep-alive UI lets overlap, frontend primitives bypassed at dozens of sites, invariant rules that pass vacuously, the Exchange transport outside the shared retry class and error taxonomy, and an orchestration layer with almost no handler-level tests. Finally, several load-bearing sentences in AGENTS.md and `docs/architecture/` now instruct contributors to do the wrong thing. Almost every fix has a precedent in the same file or a sibling module, and roughly half of the top items are small. Verification was strict: 131 claims were narrowed with a recorded correction and one was refuted. Start with the mailbox scoping tables and the dropped coverage signals (F151, F178, F023, F039), then the updater gate and the Linux runner pin, then the shared cancel flags.

## Top priorities

Items 1 to 5 were ranked by both judges; items 6 to 12 alternate the operator-risk and engineering-leverage lenses.

**1. Tenant-wide mailbox permissions invisible to the audit, and the EWS scope never gets a verdict** (F151, F251, F054) · high · S for the tables, M for the resolver. Both judges ranked this. `mailbox_named` at `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/scoping.rs:195` covers only `Mail.`, `MailboxSettings.`, `Calendars.` and `Contacts.`, so an app holding `Mail-Advanced.ReadWrite.All` or `MailboxItem.ImportExport.All` gets a healthy row, no Scope fix and no high-risk points; this is the exact class the audit exists to catch. In parallel, `resolve_mail_scopes` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/exchange/mail_scopes.rs:108` hardcodes the Microsoft Graph resource, so EWS `full_access_as_app` shows Unknown forever and a correctly RBAC-scoped EWS app still scores org-wide. Start by extending `mailbox_named` with the `MailboxItem.`/`MailboxFolder.`/`MailboxConfigItem.`/`Mail-Advanced.` prefixes plus entries in `HIGH_RISK_APP_PERMISSIONS` at `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/permissions.rs:67`, with a table test citing Microsoft's RBAC-for-Applications roles; then make the resolver accept `(resource, value)` pairs and add the joined resolver test that F456 names at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/exchange/tests.rs:200`.

**2. Capped and degraded reads drop their signal and are cached as complete** (F178, F059, F452, F023, F002, F039, F040) · high · M. Both judges ranked this. `list_all_sites` at `crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/sharepoint.rs:105` truncates at 5000 sites and returns no flag, so `is_complete()` is true, the per-app panel says the app reaches no site, and the result is cached for 60 minutes; `score_one` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/audit.rs:1489` swallows a failed SP lookup and the run caches as a clean scan; `permissions_resolve.rs:277` caches a degraded detail that shows granted permissions as not granted; backup Pass 3 at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/backup.rs:675` has no session latch and records nothing in `skipped`. The fix shape already exists in each file: return `(rows, truncated)` as `collect_all_pages_capped` does and fold it into `is_complete()` (the DTO at `crates/azapptoolkit-dto/src/sharepoint.rs:130` needs a serde-defaulted `truncated`), return `Err` instead of `None` so the existing Transient classification marks the run degraded, skip the cache put on a degraded resolution, and thread `SessionDead` plus `note_graph_failures` into Pass 3 as Passes 1 and 2 already do.

**3. The documented update opt-out is inert, MSI and .deb installs get the wrong payload, and Linux builds cannot start on current LTS distros** (F036, F139, F487, F488) · high · S. Both judges ranked this. `UserSettings::load`, the only reader of `AZAPPTOOLKIT_AUTO_UPDATE`, has no production caller, so every launch egresses to GitHub against the README's MDM promise; `check_for_update` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/updater.rs:26` never consults `tauri::utils::platform::bundle_type()`, so an MSI operator who clicks Update gets a second per-user NSIS install and a .deb install downloads the AppImage and fails with a raw plugin string. The Linux release leg at `.github/workflows/release.yml:105` runs on floating `ubuntu-latest`, so the AppImage and .deb import `GLIBC_2.38` and fail at exec on Ubuntu 22.04, Debian 12 and RHEL 9. Add a pure `update_policy(auto_update, bundle_type)` function with a unit test, make the DTO tri-state so `shell.rs` can hide or relabel the menu item, pin the Linux runner to `ubuntu-22.04` and add an `objdump -T` guard step that fails when the max `GLIBC_` version exceeds 2.35.

**4. One Cancel button stops every long run of every kind, and concurrent fan-outs clobber each other's throttle** (F440, F441, F442, F443, F444) · medium · M. Both judges ranked this. `audit_cancel` is claimed by the read-only audit, all 11 `bulk_*` writes and the AAP migration; `sweep_cancel` by the site, Key Vault and mailbox sweeps; `dr_cancel` by backup and restore. Every top-level view is keep-alive and `CancelFlag::cancel` stamps every older generation, so the docstring at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/bulk.rs:127` ("never run at once") is a false premise and cancelling a scan can halt a bulk delete mid-selection. `GraphClient` has a single `throttle_observer` slot (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/throttle.rs:141`), so whichever run drops first clears the survivor's adaptive back-off. Add `bulk_cancel` and `migration_cancel` to `AppState`, split `sweep_cancel` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:443`, pin command-to-flag in `repo_invariants/cancel.rs`, and make `clear_throttle_observer` compare-and-clear; every frontend Cancel handler must then call its own command.

**5. The Exchange transport sits outside the shared retry class and error taxonomy, and the invariant that should catch it passes vacuously** (F140, F164, F166, F167, F168, F215) · medium · M. Both judges ranked this. `send_core` at `crates/azapptoolkit-exchange/src/client/transport.rs:265` open-codes its retry loop, so a `New-ManagementRoleAssignment` that committed before a 502 is replayed, fails as a duplicate and `grants.rs` then keeps the org-wide grant with "scoping is NOT effective"; `repo_invariants/trust.rs:163` skips any file without `with_retries(`. The verifier refuted the earlier "duplicate assignment survives removal" headline, so the accurate framing is false failures, not duplicates. Two smaller siblings ride along: `rbac.rs:220` lists references through `invoke_optional`, so a misclassified 400 can unlock an irreversible group delete, and `targets.rs:716` compares DNs case-sensitively while the post-write proof folds case. Port `send_core` onto `with_retries` with a `retry_class_for(cmdlet)` helper, make the trust rule fail any crate that references `MAX_RETRIES` or `next_backoff_ms` directly, and add an Exchange `error_conformance.rs` mirroring ARM's.

**6. Restore is not idempotent while the DR view and the restore report both tell the operator to re-run it** (F424, F037, F038) · high · M. Operator-risk lens. In the one scenario DR exists for, following the on-screen remedy at `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/dr.rs:502` ("recreates only what is missing") creates a second copy of every already-restored app with new appIds, paired SPs, fresh 180-day secrets and re-granted admin consent, and the Restore button stays live after a successful run. `restore.rs:751` repeats the re-run instruction and `restore.rs:734` reports failed Graph reads as runbook facts. Reword the callout and clear the loaded plan after `report.set`, stop the report saying "re-run the restore", then make Pass 1 (`restore.rs:197`) adoption-aware by display name plus a source marker.

**7. Give the orchestration layer a test seam** (F459, F457, F455, F010, F451, F450) · medium · M. Engineering-leverage lens. Roughly twenty confirmed bugs (F001, F004, F023, F037, F038, F039, F054, F070 to F075, F460) live in `State`- or `AppHandle`-bound bodies that no test can construct; only `credentials.rs` and `backup.rs` have wiremock handler tests, and `bulk.rs` proved the `ProgressSink` seam works then kept it private. Lift the trait from `bulk.rs:46` into `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/progress.rs:21` as `pub(crate)` with a test `Recorder`, replace the closure at `backup.rs:364`, add `commands/test_support.rs` with one mock base-URL convention and shared builders, then write the three F455 tests against `permissions.rs:618` and `permissions.rs:389`, which already take `&GraphClient` and need no refactor.

**8. The SSO claims editor detaches a live policy from an empty editor and writes transformation claims that can never be emitted** (F371, F071, F070, F447) · high · M. Operator-risk lens. A first-time operator without `Policy.ReadWrite` consent has the claims read swallowed to `None` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:510`, the tab at `sso_tab.rs:543` renders an empty editor, and one Save detaches the configured policy; each save also orphans a tenant-level `claimsMappingPolicy` because `set_claims_mapping` (`mod.rs:1643`) unassigns and recreates instead of PATCHing. The codec at `sso/claims.rs:48` emits `TransformationID` instead of `ID` and conflates both on read, and the round-trip test at `claims.rs:442` pins the wrong shape. Add a `claims_read_failed` discriminator to `SsoConfigDto`, disable Save until a successful read with a Load-claims consent button, add `transformation_id` to the schema entry DTO, and replace the masking test with Microsoft's documented fixture.

**9. Pin the IPC contract mechanically and stop a non-UiError rejection from aborting the WASM app** (F088, F425, F019, F091, F368) · medium · M. Engineering-leverage lens. Command, arg-struct and return-type parity is exact today (186 = 186 = 186) but is checked only by `.claude/hooks/command-parity-check.sh:15`, an advisory hook that always exits 0 and needs jq; `invoke_result` at `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/bindings/mod.rs:17` unwraps the error deserialization, so any string rejection (invalid args, ACL) panics the whole frontend. Add `repo_invariants/ipc.rs` reusing `sources::commands()` with a vacuity floor, wrap `invoke_result` to deserialize through `serde_json::Value` and map a non-UiError rejection to `UiError::new("ipc", ..)`, and move `DirectoryIndexStatus` (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/applications/mod.rs:86`) into the DTO crate.

**10. Audit-surface fixes report success while doing nothing or dropping warnings** (F394, F022, F403, F333) · high · S. Operator-risk lens. The Permissions-tab AAP migration gate at `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/exchange_scoping_section.rs:324` checks only `failures`, so a `partial` report (legacy policy kept, fail-closed) toasts "Migrated" while the sibling audit dialog gates correctly; the redundant-permissions Fix at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/remediation.rs:257` returns empty `removed` and `skipped` when every value is vetoed and the UI hides the button; `scope_remediation.rs:111` hard-codes zero warnings so "groups were NOT applied" reads as clean; `permissions_tab.rs:116` sets its outcome note right before `on_changed` unmounts it. Add `AapMigrationReport::is_clean()` on the DTO (`crates/azapptoolkit-dto/src/exchange.rs:174`) and use it on both surfaces, push vetoed values into `skipped`, gate `on_done` on a non-empty `removed`, read `res.warnings.len()`, and toast failures before `on_changed`.

**11. Error codes are produced consistently but consumed inconsistently** (F110, F122, F462, F466, F467, F353, F465) · medium · S. Engineering-leverage lens. `consent_required` is flattened to `token_error` inside every client call (`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/http_error.rs:129`), so the shared consent fallback never fires for scoped calls; the throttled message at `http_error.rs:76` reads "retry after Some(30)s"; `.toast__message` at `apps/desktop/web-rs/styles.css:3513` collapses the appended hint; only Graph has a connect timeout, so ARM, Key Vault and Exchange spin about four minutes against a firewalled host (`crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/client.rs:38`); the inline-error path at `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/hooks/use_command.rs:85` (24 call sites) never offers Re-authenticate; and a 401 renders as bare text with hints that say "sign out" against the in-place re-auth design. Each is a one-file change on a shared producer or sink and reaches every surface at once.

**12. The permission tester reports a definitive no_access when its reads failed, and never sees EWS-only holders** (F055, F056, F057, F449, F058) · medium · M. Operator-risk lens. `orgwide_mailbox_grant` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:63` folds a failed Graph read into `EntraReach::NotHeld`; `test_site_access` at `:1092` prints "holds no organization-wide SharePoint grant" when the grants read failed; `find_mailbox_reachers` at `:466` discovers candidates from the Graph resource only, so a principal with `full_access_as_app` over every mailbox is reported as no_access. An admin uses these answers to decide who can read a mailbox. Return a tri-state (Held / None / Unreadable), score Unreadable as unknown in `synthesize`, page `appRoleAssignedTo` on both mailbox resources, and let `exchange_available` reflect the real degraded mode so the documented partial-coverage caveat can fire.

## Quick wins

Small-effort items with real payoff that are not already covered above, drawn from both judges.

- **F141 · settings.json corruption is overwritten with defaults**: distinguish NotFound from a parse or IO failure in `UserSettings::mutate` (`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/settings.rs:121`) so a hand-edit typo cannot wipe vault bindings and tenant defaults.
- **F426 · Key Vault browser survives a tenant switch**: one Effect on `tenant.get()` clearing the listed secrets, vault name and `loaded` (`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/key_vault_view.rs:42`) closes the only tenant-scoped surface that ignores the #1 footgun rule.
- **F338 · "Rotate & remove existing" deletes every client secret unconfirmed**: a count in the label and a `ConfirmDialog` at `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/credentials_tab.rs:1115`, matching the confirm-gated siblings.
- **F195 · ARM assignments duplicated per subscription**: dedupe by assignment id at `crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/client.rs:55` so a management-group role is not shown N times and does not inflate high-privilege counts.
- **F478 · unwritable log dir panics the process before any subscriber exists**: fall back to stderr-only logging instead of `.expect()` on the appender at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:353`; the judge confirmed this directly.
- **F295 · `just setup` panics on a fresh clone**: run `just check` after `trunk build --locked` in `scripts/setup.sh:132` and `scripts/setup.ps1` so the first documented command succeeds on all three OSes.
- **F296 · docs-only CI skips the tests that pin AGENTS.md and CHANGELOG.md**: one `case` arm in `.github/workflows/ci.yml:75` classifying AGENTS.md, CHANGELOG.md and `.claude/hooks/*` as code.
- **F218 · `just check` skips the GUI harness**: add `--all-targets --features test-support` to the web-rs check at `justfile:251` so the inner loop compiles what CI compiles.
- **F020 · adaptive throttle halves only once**: separate `last_halved` from `last_throttle` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/throttle.rs:102` so the cap keeps degrading under a sustained 429 storm; a paused-clock test fits.
- **F026 · bulk credential sweep busts both tenant-wide indexes**: loop `invalidate_app_credentials` instead of `invalidate_app_lists` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/bulk.rs:283`, avoiding a full app and SP re-enumeration on large tenants.
- **F460 · four SSO detail writes bust both tenant-wide indexes**: the same mis-tier at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:1728` and three siblings; use `invalidate_app_details`.
- **F003 · cache-only search answers without proving the session**: add `prove_tenant_session` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/search.rs:302` and its two siblings, and drop `graph_for(` from the invariant's `SESSION_PROOFS` so the rule stops passing vacuously.
- **F029 · the Audit cache guard is unpinned**: a source-scan rule that every `.put(CacheKind::Audit` at `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/audit.rs:445` sits inside `if run_is_cacheable(` turns an AGENTS.md sentence into a failing test.
- **F148 · a new `CacheKind` variant panics at runtime**: an exhaustive match in `CacheKind::idx` plus a const assert at `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/cache.rs:45` makes it a compile error.
- **F212 and F213 · six unused dependencies and no gate**: remove `anyhow` and `thiserror` from `apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml`, `tracing` from arm, keyvault and web-rs, `url` from `crates/azapptoolkit-exchange/Cargo.toml:18`, then add a `cargo machete` recipe (`justfile:329`) with the thiserror macro ignore.
- **F239 · two stale rationales in auth-and-consent.md**: `docs/architecture/auth-and-consent.md:82` still says the BearerProvider boundary flattens to `String` and tells agents to extend two `matches!` sets that no longer exist; rewrite to `TokenError` with code and `core::reauth::REAUTH_FATAL_CODES`.

## Strategic projects

- **Posture snapshot and drift report (#109)** (F267). Every seam the owner's design names exists and is unblocked; the project turns the audit from a point-in-time screen into "N new high-risk grants since the last snapshot", the question a security committee actually asks. Effort L, no new scopes or persistence policy.
- **Audit signal expansion** (F278, F161, F264, F276, F268, F078, F259, F274). The workbench never reads federated identity credentials (the class `federation.rs` calls the worst case), `disabledByMicrosoftStatus`, Identity Protection risk detections, workload-identity Conditional Access or per-credential sign-in activity. Each is one `$select` field or one v1.0 read plus a table-tested rule; together they change what the audit can claim.
- **Idempotent, destination-aware DR restore** (F424, F037, F042, F428, F090, F152, F150, F038). Resolve each manifest app in the destination before creating it, make `plan_restore` look at the destination and the schema, rewrite every `api://` identifier-URI form, model flexible FICs, and thread read failures into `ManualItem` reasons. This is the project that makes DR trustworthy rather than one-shot.
- **Run identity for cancellation, progress and throttling** (F440, F441, F442, F443, F444, F035). Beyond the per-kind flags in priority 4, have `claim()` return a generation that rides the first progress event as `run_id`, add a `cancel_run(run_id)` command, filter listeners by run id, and give the throttle a multi-observer slot. That also fixes interleaved bulk-progress rows across mounted `BulkActionBar`s and gives the AAP migration a real Cancel.
- **Detail-pane and launch-time state architecture** (F336, F357, F372, F471, F472, F333, F340). Every mutation rebuilds the whole detail pane and remounts every visited tab, which is the root cause of the deferred-reload workarounds, the torn-down certificate reveal, lost scroll and stale-snapshot replays; at launch every restored dock pane fires its full detail fetch hidden and Home ships the whole audit run over IPC for a few counts. A long-lived `RwSignal<Arc<ApplicationDetail>>` outside the Suspend, first-show gating of restored panes and a `get_cached_audit_summary` command form one coherent project.
- **Behavioural GUI coverage of the mutation-heavy surfaces and degraded audit states** (F343, F410, F430, F411, F400, F329, F414). The browser tests are the frontend's only behavioural gate, yet Owners, Expose an API, Permissions actions, rotate and federated flows, the SSO wizard, create app and enterprise Access have zero coverage, no test renders a cancelled or truncated run or asserts an ARIA state value, and 13 demo reads are unregistered. A shared helper module plus one shard per surface would have caught F333, F340, F371, F372 and F394 before shipping.
- **Capabilities catalog completion, with readiness derived from it** (F258, F021, F024, F163, F255, F391, F233, F262, F271). Readiness cannot recognise the custom Entra role the repo itself recommends, keeps a hand-maintained CAE list that has drifted, and has no entries for claims mapping or provisioning, so those 403s carry no remediation and the provisioning pane cannot offer consent. Adding the entries and deriving the probe list from the catalog restores the one-definition rule across the three auth planes.
- **One HTTP transport core and an observability baseline** (F166, F201, F467, F463, F290, F476, F479, F480, F481, F482, F478, F477, F484, F485). ARM and Key Vault duplicate the status-to-variant closure, Exchange keeps a hand-rolled enum, only Graph has a connect timeout, error bodies flow raw and network errors drop their cause; no client sends a `client-request-id` or captures `request-id`, write commands log no failures, webview panics never reach the backend log, and there is no "Open log folder" or diagnostics bundle. A `core::http_client` builder, a shared transport generic over the macro-generated error enums, one `warn!` hook at the UiError boundary and a small diagnostics command remove four copies of every future policy change and make failures diagnosable.

## Changes that must ship together

Backend and frontend halves that mislead if landed alone, from both judges.

- **F178, F059, F452**: the Graph client returns the truncation flag, the DTO gains `truncated` and `is_complete()` changes, and `sites.rs` plus `app_site_access_panel.rs` render the `IndexCapNotice`-style caveat; the regression test cannot be written until the DTO field exists.
- **F371, F070, F447**: the `SsoConfigDto` discriminator and the codec's second id field must land with the `sso_tab` Callout and Save gate and the `claims_editor` field; the pinned round-trip test currently asserts the wrong wire shape.
- **F036, F139, F487, F477**: the backend policy and format gate needs the tri-state DTO; `shell.rs` and `update_splash.rs` must consume it to hide or relabel "Check for updates"; log the discarded check error while there.
- **F027, F058**: a `mailbox_scoping_resolved` flag on the audit run result and export coverage needs the coverage sentence plus a Callout on the org-wide mailbox group; `exchange_available` on the tester result has the same backend-then-UI shape.
- **F127, F402, F393**: the core marker stability test and the frontend `GROUP_CATALOG` and posture count must agree, with `UNSCOPABLE_LEGACY_MAILBOX` advice kept separate from `UNCONFINABLE_MAILBOX`; the truncated-run caveat belongs on the strip in the same change.
- **F002**: a serde-defaulted degraded flag on `ApplicationDetail` must pair with a `permissions_tab` Callout, or the operator still sees "Not granted" with no explanation.
- **F055, F056, F449**: tri-state `EntraReach` and `HeldSharePointGrants` in the backend, unknown rendering in `permission_tester_view`, and fixtures that can express a failed read.
- **F394, F022**: `is_clean()` belongs on the DTO crate and is consumed by two frontend surfaces; the remediation report's `skipped` semantics and the `row.rs` `on_done` gate change together.
- **F440, F442, F443**: new per-kind flags or run ids on `AppState` require each frontend Cancel handler (security strip, `BulkProgressRow`, three Resource Access panels, the app site panel, `dr.rs`) to call its own command; one side alone changes nothing.
- **F054**: carrying the resource in `resolve_mail_scopes` changes the verdict map keys the Permissions tab and `score_application` read; confirm both consume the EWS key before declaring done.
- **F075**: `warnings` on `SamlSsoSummary` is a DTO change that only helps once `components/sso_summary.rs` renders a Callout and a fixture carries a warning.
- **F150**: `Option<String>` subject on the core model, `FederatedCredentialDto`, the Federated tab row and the restore warning all change together.
- **F376, F377**: the enterprise Credentials expiry badge and Access-tab windowing need DTO changes (a shared expiry helper, `principal_id` on the assignment DTO) alongside the frontend rendering.
- **F094, F073**: sub-site revoke commands and bindings exist with no UI caller, while the UI carries a dead "(capped)" branch for a constant backend field; each needs a decision on both sides.
- **F353, F465, F110, F405**: the re-auth affordance on the inline path and an `unauthorized` branch depend on core not flattening `consent_required` inside client calls; a `UiError::is_consent_required()` helper then replaces the hand-rolled inline callouts.
- **F088, F425, F019, F091, F368**: the IPC invariant test, the safe `invoke_result` wrapper and moving `DirectoryIndexStatus` (with `app_index_cap`) into the DTO crate touch backend, DTO and bindings together; land as one PR so the parity scan has one convention to pin.
- **F472**: a `get_cached_audit_summary` command plus `posture_counts` in core must ship with `home_dashboard.rs` switched to it and the demo fixture registered.
- **F462, F466**: the new throttled message text should replace the GUI fixtures' "Too many requests" strings so the rendering, including the pre-wrap hint, is pinned end to end.
- **F035, F444**: terminal bulk-progress semantics and a Cancel affordance for the AAP migration are a backend event contract plus frontend row rendering; change them together.

## Scorecard by area

### Shared crates (114 findings, 8 slices)

The pure decision cores are the strongest code in the repository: the Exchange `targets`/`verdict`/`aap`/`references` modules are fail-closed by construction with every past regression pinned by a named test, the core cache's store-after-invalidate guard is exhaustively tested, the scoping gates and Selected-scope levels match Microsoft's model, the federation and redirect validators close real userinfo-host tricks, and every role GUID in the capabilities catalog is correct. The Graph transport has one retrying core with verb-derived retry classes, a same-origin guard on every `nextLink`, and tenant-prefixed self-invalidating caches. The weaknesses sit where Microsoft moved after the code was written or where one path escaped the shared rule: the mailbox advisory misses the RBAC-era permission families, the flexible-FIC model cannot deserialize a null subject, `list_all_sites` is the one capped scan without a truncation flag, the Exchange transport open-codes its retry loop and error enum, the ARM `atScope()` semantics label inherited assignments as direct, and Log Analytics accepts a `PartialError` 200 as complete. Auth is careful about PKCE, nonce and keyring handling but treats `interaction_required` as a dead refresh token, omits CAE-ness from the cache key, and accepts a domain-form tenant id it can never sign in with. All 591 tests pass and the crate-level conformance tests exist for ARM and Key Vault but not for Graph or Exchange.

### Tauri backend (104 findings, 6 slices, plus most of the 52 gap-slice findings)

The command layer implements its documented contracts faithfully where they are pinned: tenant-scoped keys, tiered invalidation, generation watches on the pinned indexes, the full-collection PATCH contract, `run_is_cacheable`, `run_bulk_seq`, generation-based cancellation, atomic owner-only file writes, and exact IPC parity across 186 commands. The invariants suite derives its subjects from a source walk with vacuity floors and self-tests. The weak spots are the paths those pins do not reach: three shared cores return `Err` after a real Graph write so invalidation is skipped, degraded resolutions and swallowed SP lookups are cached, backup Pass 3 lacks the latch its siblings have, the restore is not idempotent while telling the operator to re-run, the claims codec mis-models Graph's schema entry, the permission tester and site sweep report unreadable or truncated data as clean, and the resolver hardcodes the Graph resource. Cross-cutting: three cancel flags and one throttle observer are shared across run kinds, only Graph has a connect timeout, no client sends a `client-request-id`, write commands log no failures, and handler-level tests exist only in `credentials.rs` and `backup.rs`. Three files (`audit.rs`, `permissions.rs`, `sso/mod.rs`) exceed 1400 lines and split along seams the repo has already used twice.

### Leptos frontend (128 findings, 7 slices)

Structure is sound: `TenantScopedUi::reset()` covers every field and is pinned, the open-items workspace has one write path with cross-tenant persistence tested, the error sink is coherent, every dialog wires a focus trap and Escape, `TabBar` implements the full WAI-ARIA tabs pattern, colour tokens clear WCAG AA, and the GUI harness (orphan-mount sweep, loud-reject unmocked policy, typed fixtures) is well reasoned. The recurring weakness is drift from the documented primitives: `Badge` is bypassed at 45 sites, 11 raw tables lack `DataTable` keynav, about 10 load failures skip `DetailLoadError`, both Owners tabs hand-roll `DirectorySearch` and reintroduce the "No matches under an untouched box" bug, and 66 form-error sites have no live role. Behavioural gaps follow the same shape: the SSO tab renders an unreadable policy as empty and Save detaches it, partial AAP migrations toast success, the whole detail pane remounts on every mutation, `invoke_result` panics on a non-UiError rejection, and the Key Vault browser survives a tenant switch. 125 of 186 command literals are never referenced by a GUI test and the untested set is exactly the mutation-heavy surfaces; the demo's ids and dates have drifted from its own catalog.

### Cross-cutting: tooling, docs, security, dependencies, API currency, product (92 findings, 6 slices)

Tooling is well reasoned and its rationales are pinned by tests, the security posture of the app's own code is strong (loopback PKCE with state and nonce, redacted `Debug` on secret types, `same_origin` on every follow, consistent OData and OPATH escaping, CSV and HTML escaping in exports, SHA-pinned actions, cargo-deny and cargo-audit on both lockfiles), dependency holds are still valid against today's registry, and every Graph call is on v1.0 except the one beta-only report. The edges are sharp: `just setup` panics on a fresh clone, the docs-only detector skips the tests that pin AGENTS.md and CHANGELOG.md, the Linux release runner floats, the updater signing key is in the environment of the whole build, six declared dependencies are unused with no gate, and the webview CSP and plugin capabilities grant more than the frontend uses. Documentation is rich and mostly accurate, but two architecture-doc rationales contradict AGENTS.md and the code, the operator RBAC role and README omit shipped features, five docs carry stale module paths, and the SSO wizard hardcodes the commercial cloud despite a sovereign-cloud abstraction. The product surface is unusually complete for its niche; the real gaps are Entra's 30-day soft delete being neither surfaced nor acknowledged, workload-identity Conditional Access, and tenant app-management policies.

## Contents

1. **Bugs and security**: 113 findings (12 high, 58 medium, 43 low) in 103 entries after nine merges.
2. **Cleanup, refactors, performance and dependencies**: 114 findings (14 medium, 100 low) in 106 entries after eight merges.
3. **Enhancements and feature opportunities**: 108 findings (30 medium, 78 low) in 106 entries after two merges.
4. **Documentation, tests, CI/tooling and accessibility**: 103 findings (31 medium, 72 low) in 101 entries after two merges.
5. **Second-pass findings (gap slices)**: 52 findings (2 high, 28 medium, 22 low) from the six completeness-critic lenses.
6. **Appendix A**: the one refuted claim and the notable downgrades. **Appendix B**: 302 minor nits grouped by slice. **Appendix C**: coverage and method notes for every slice (26 finders, the toolchain runner and 6 gap slices), the toolchain baseline and the critic's assessment.

---

## Bugs and security

This bucket holds the 113 verified defects and security findings from the review: 12 high, 58 medium and 43 low (92 tagged bug, 21 tagged security). Nine pairs or triples were reported independently at the same file and are merged below, so the section carries 103 entries; a merged entry names its other ids on the verdict line. Sub-themes are ordered by operator impact: wrong audit results first, then tenant mutations with the wrong outcome, backup and restore, auth and session, configuration, security hardening, caches, robustness, and finally tooling. Within each sub-theme entries run high, medium, low. Twenty-six findings carry a "partially" verdict; each entry states what the verifier corrected.

### Audit scoring and coverage accuracy

#### F151 · high · effort S · Mailbox advisory's name-shaped fallback misses MailboxItem.*/MailboxFolder.*/Mail-Advanced.*: org-wide read/write of every mailbox item scores as all-clear
`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/scoping.rs:195` · confirmed, re-verified · also reported as F251 (`scoping.rs:75`, partially: the `graph_mail_role` omission is documented in exchange-scoping.md:46-50, but that rationale covers AAP-migration parity, not advisory membership)
**Problem.** `mailbox_named` tests only the prefixes `Mail.`, `MailboxSettings.`, `Calendars.` and `Contacts.`, so `MailboxItem.*`, `MailboxFolder.*` and `Mail-Advanced.*` never satisfy `is_mailbox_reaching_permission`. Microsoft's RBAC-for-Applications table lists all of them as Graph application permissions with scoped roles. An org-wide grant of `MailboxItem.ReadWrite.All` or `Mail-Advanced.ReadWrite.All` therefore yields no mailbox finding, no high-risk points and no Scope fix; the app ranks healthy. `HIGH_RISK_APP_PERMISSIONS` lists none of them either.
```
fn mailbox_named(value: &str) -> bool {
    ["Mail.", "MailboxSettings.", "Calendars.", "Contacts."]
```
**Proposal.** Extend `mailbox_named` with `MailboxFolder.`, `MailboxItem.`, `MailboxConfigItem.` and `Mail-Advanced.` and add a table test over Microsoft's supported-roles list; add the write/export variants to HIGH_RISK and the read variants to MEDIUM. Either add them to `graph_mail_role` with the CHANGELOG note exchange-scoping.md requires, or reword the UNCONFINABLE_MAILBOX recommendation, which currently claims RBAC has no role for them.

#### F054 · high · effort M · resolve_mail_scopes hardcodes Microsoft Graph, so the EWS full_access_as_app scope never gets a verdict
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/exchange/mail_scopes.rs:108` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** All three resolver paths (`:111`, `:242`, `:341`) call `exchange_role_for_resource_permission(MICROSOFT_GRAPH_APP_ID, ..)`, and `graph_mail_role("full_access_as_app")` is `None`, so the EWS value is dropped. The Permissions tab shows the broadest mailbox grant as Unknown and caches that until `invalidate_app_details`. The audit's lean resolver drops it too, so an RBAC-scoped `Application EWS.AccessAsApp` grant still scores at org-wide weight. The mod.rs:32-35 comment justifying Graph-only is contradicted by scoping.rs:106-116 and the repo's own tests.
**Proposal.** Pass the role alongside the value (`&[(String, &'static str)]` or `&[ExchangeTarget]`) into `resolve_mail_scopes` and `resolve_mail_scopes_audit_cached`, fix the `:341` filter to read the role from the target, update the audit caller and correct the mod.rs comment. Add a unit test that an EWS-only input reaches the probe and maps to `Application EWS.AccessAsApp`.

#### F178 · high · effort M · list_all_sites drops its truncation signal, so a >5000-site tenant's Sites.Selected sweep is reported and cached as complete
`crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/sharepoint.rs:105` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** `list_all_sites(max)` loops `while out.len() < max`, then `out.truncate(max); Ok(out)` with no truncation flag, unlike `collect_all_pages_capped`. The sweep sets `total_sites = sites.len()`, `is_complete()` returns true, the result is cached for the audit TTL, and the per-app panel affirms "no per-site grants" for grants on site 5001 and beyond. No `MAX_SITES` check exists in the command or in web-rs.
**Proposal.** Return `Result<(Vec<Site>, bool)>` with the bool set when the cap stopped the walk; add `truncated` to `SiteSweepResult`/`AppSiteAccessDto`, fold it into `is_complete()`, skip caching (or cache with the flag), render the existing `IndexCapNotice` pattern in the Sites tab, and add a wiremock test where page 1 exceeds `max`.

#### F059 · medium · effort M · Site sweep silently truncates at MAX_SITES_PER_SWEEP and caches the result as complete; is_complete() has no truncation dimension
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sharepoint.rs:843` · confirmed
**Problem.** The command side of the F178 defect: `MAX_SITES_PER_SWEEP = 5_000` is passed to `list_all_sites`, the sweep caches on `!cancelled && sites_failed == 0`, and `SiteSweepResult::is_complete` ignores truncation while the audit sibling (`dto/audit.rs:125-127`) includes `!self.truncated`. The UI summary reads "scanned 5000 of 5000 sites" and the CSV claims full coverage, so the "Raise if a user legitimately hits it" comment can never be acted on.
**Proposal.** Add `truncated: bool` to `SiteSweepResult`/`AppSiteAccessDto`, include `!self.truncated` in `is_complete()`, prefer caching with the flag so a large tenant is not re-swept every time, and render "more than 5 000 sites, coverage is partial" in the summary and export; add a DTO test mirroring the audit's truncated case.

#### F023 · medium · effort S · score_one swallows a failed SP lookup, skips the org-wide reconciliation, and the run still caches as a clean complete scan
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/audit.rs:1489` · confirmed
**Problem.** A failed `get_service_principal_by_app_id_lean` is mapped to `None` with a warn. With `sp = None`, `orgwide` is empty, so `resolve_mail_scopes_audit_cached` can return a `Scoped { Rbac }` verdict that is never reconciled against a surviving org-wide grant, the under-report `AuditCoverageGap::GraphAppRoleAssignments` documents. Nothing is pushed to `degraded`, so `run_is_cacheable` passes and the run is served as authoritative. Rare in practice because the SP index seeds most lookups.
**Proposal.** Either return `Err(err.into())` from `score_one` (classified Transient, landing in `unscored` and `PerPrincipalScoring`), or add an `sp_unresolved` flag to `ScoreCtx` and a new `AuditCoverageGap::ServicePrincipalLookup`; in both cases skip the Exchange probe when `sp` is `None`.

#### F002 · medium · effort S · get_application_detail caches a degraded permission resolution for the 60-minute TTL
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/applications/permissions_resolve.rs:277` · confirmed
**Problem.** `client.resolve_resource_sp(&id).await.ok().flatten()` collapses a 429/5xx into the same `None` as "resource SP genuinely absent". Both runtime closures then return `None`, every permission of that resource renders as Not granted and offers to grant, and the detail is stored under `app_detail_key` unconditionally. The graph layer caches only `Ok`; the command layer undoes that. A dead token fails earlier, so the realistic trigger is retry-budget exhaustion under throttling.
**Proposal.** Return `(Vec<ResolvedPermission>, bool /*degraded*/)` from `resolve_required_resource_access`, skip `state.cache.put` when degraded, and add `#[serde(default)] resolution_degraded: bool` to `ApplicationDetail` so the tab shows a `Callout` inviting Refresh; unit-test one `Err` resource.

#### F022 · medium · effort S · Redundant-permissions Fix silently drops values whose covering permission is vetoed; the UI then clears the Fix button
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/remediation.rs:257` · confirmed
**Problem.** When every covering permission is vetoed (`Err(_) => HashMap::new()` for a non-Exchange-admin, or a transient Exchange failure via `.unwrap_or_default()`), the planner hits `continue` without `skipped.push`, contradicting the handler's own comment that the value "shows up in `skipped`". The command returns `Ok { removed: [], skipped: [] }`; `RedundantPermsAction` toasts "Removed 0 redundant permissions" and `on_done` removes the Fix button while the finding and the live grant remain.
**Proposal.** In `plan_redundant_removals`, collect pre-veto broaders first and push the value into `skipped` (or a new `vetoed` list with a reason) when the post-veto set is empty; in the UI fire `on_done` only when `removed` is non-empty and render the veto reason. Add a planner test for the all-vetoed case.

#### F123 · medium · effort S · Rule 13 emits the broad-prefix delegated advisory for declared scopes regardless of consent
`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/scoring.rs:483` · partially (for SP rows `scope_values` is already the AllPrincipals grant set, so the blurb is right there; the rule is advisory only, no score impact)
**Problem.** `rule_high_risk_delegated` filters `perms.scope_values` with `is_risky_delegated_scope` and never reads `has_admin_consent`; for app-registration rows `scope_values` is the declared manifest. Every app that merely declares a user-consent `Mail.Read` or `Files.Read` lands in a group whose blurb says "Admin-consented delegated scopes with broad reach", burying the genuinely admin-consented cases. The only Rule 13 test covers the two exact entries plus `User.Read`.
**Proposal.** Thread the per-client AllPrincipals scope set the runner already keeps (`delegated_scopes_by_client`) into app rows so the rule can tell consented from declared; keep the two PS entries unconditional and emit the broad-prefix half only for consented scopes, or word "declared" and "admin-consented" separately. Do not gate on the coarse `has_admin_consent` flag alone, since the grants read is best-effort. Fix the groups.rs:113 blurb.

#### F393 · medium · effort S · Truncated runs with findings show no coverage caveat on screen
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/security_view.rs:224` · partially (the degraded lede is rendered unconditionally on the Findings pane; only truncated-with-findings and the All-apps pane lack caveats, and truncation needs more than 10 000 apps)
**Problem.** The posture strip filters on `r.cancelled` only, and the truncated callout lives inside the `(!any_findings)` branch of findings.rs. A truncated run that does have findings renders groups, counts and every "Fix all N" over an arbitrary prefix with no notice; the All-apps pane renders none of the three caveats. The export's `coverage_sentences` is more honest than the screen.
**Proposal.** Move the truncated (and optionally degraded) caveat into `PostureStrip`, driven by `r.coverage()` / `!is_complete()`, so both audit panes see it; add a GUI case with `truncated: true` and populated items.

#### F056 · medium · effort S · orgwide_mailbox_grant folds a failed Graph read into EntraReach::NotHeld
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:63` · confirmed
**Problem.** `orgwide_mailbox_grant` returns `None` for an SP lookup `Err`, a `mailbox_resource_roles` `Err` and a `list_app_role_assignments` `Err`, and `test_mailbox_access` maps `None` straight to `EntraReach::NotHeld`. Combined with `RbacReach::None{had_assignments:false}` (the managed-identity case) the verdict is a definitive `no_access` stating "No organization-wide Graph mailbox permission is granted in Entra ID." The SharePoint sibling distinguishes unreadable from none.
**Proposal.** Return a tri-state (Held / None / Unreadable) and add `EntraReach::Unreadable` scored as unknown with detail "Entra app-role assignments couldn't be read"; keep `Ok(None)` from the SP lookup as NotHeld. Add a synthesize test: Unreadable plus `RbacReach::None` gives unknown, not no_access.

#### F057 · medium · effort M · find_mailbox_reachers discovers candidates from the Microsoft Graph resource only
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:466` · confirmed
**Problem.** The reverse lookup reads `appRoleAssignedTo` on the Graph SP only and filters with `Some(MICROSOFT_GRAPH_APP_ID)`. A principal whose sole mailbox grant is org-wide EWS `full_access_as_app` is either absent or, if it is in Exchange's SP store, enters with empty held permissions and reads `no_access`. The single-app tester (`:71-79`) and the audit's `prefetch_ews_full_access_grants` already read the Office 365 Exchange Online SP, and the architecture doc claims the probe uses the same two-layer union.
**Proposal.** Page `list_app_role_assigned_to` on both mailbox resources via `mailbox_resource_roles`, filtering with `is_scopable_exchange_resource_permission(Some(resource.app_id), value)`, or reuse the `prefetch_ews_full_access_grants` shape to add EWS holders with held `["full_access_as_app"]`; extend the merge test with an EWS-only candidate.

#### F055 · medium · effort S · test_site_access claims "holds no organization-wide SharePoint grant" when the grants read actually failed
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:1092` · confirmed
**Problem.** `sharepoint_grants_held` documents that `None` must be reported as unknown, and sharepoint-selected.md says a failed read reports `unknown`, never "no access". Yet when `held` is `None` path 1 is skipped and, with no permission entry on the chain, the command returns a definitive `no_access` whose detail asserts a fact it never established. Only the entry-found branch handles `None`. The trigger needs a Graph failure on the SP reads while the site resolve succeeds, hence medium.
**Proposal.** Extract a pure `site_verdict(held, hit, label)` and return `unknown` when grants could not be read and no entry exists; make `sharepoint_grants_held` a tri-state so a genuinely absent SP (`Ok(None)`) still yields `no_access` while an `Err` yields `unknown`.

#### F193 · medium · effort S · Log Analytics `PartialError` responses (HTTP 200 plus `error`) are treated as complete results
`crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/models.rs:44` · confirmed
**Problem.** `LogsQueryResponse` models only `tables`; Microsoft documents that a non-fatal query error returns 200 with `tables` plus an `error` of code `PartialError`, and Kusto guidance says to ignore the whole result. `LogAnalyticsClient::query` returns `tables[0]` unconditionally, and `get_app_graph_usage` builds `GraphUsageResult` with only `truncated` as its coverage channel. A truncated 90-day summarize shows fewer call patterns than exist, and the operator may strip a permission the app uses.
**Proposal.** Add `#[serde(default)] error: Option<LogsErrorInfo>` to `LogsQueryResponse`; return `ArmError::Protocol("partial result: ...")` when present, or thread a `partial` flag beside `truncated` so the panel warns. Add a wiremock test with a `PartialError` body.

#### F192 · medium · effort S · `atScope()` is documented as excluding inherited assignments; Azure defines it as "at or above the scope"
`crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/client.rs:90` · confirmed
**Problem.** The client doc, the sweep module doc, the DTO doc and two UI strings all say `atScope()` returns only direct vault grants; Microsoft documents it as "at or above the scope", so every inherited RG, subscription or MG Owner is emitted as a per-vault row labelled direct. The panel table never shows the row's real `scope` (only the CSV does), and the empty-state text claims inherited-only vaults will not appear. The verifier kept medium because the listed principals genuinely hold the role; the provenance label is what is wrong.
**Proposal.** Keep the `atScope()` query but compare `props.scope` to the vault `id` case-insensitively and either drop non-matching rows or add `inherited: bool` to `KeyVaultAccessRow` with an "inherited from <scope level>" badge. Rewrite the doc comment, `keyvault_rbac.rs:5`, `dto/keyvault.rs:16` and the two UI strings; add a wiremock test with a subscription-scoped assignment.

#### F195 · medium · effort S · `principalId eq` returns assignments "at, above or below" each subscription, so a management-group assignment renders as N duplicate rows
`crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/client.rs:55` · confirmed
**Problem.** The client doc says "at or below", but Microsoft's filter returns assignments above the subscription too. `list_managed_identity_azure_roles` fans out per subscription and flattens with no dedupe by `RoleAssignment.id`, each copy labelled with a different subscription name, inflating the RBAC footprint and the high-privilege count. `readiness.rs` is unaffected because it collects into a `HashSet`.
**Proposal.** Fix the doc comment to "at, above or below", dedupe in the consumer by `RoleAssignment.id` before `flat`, and label above-subscription rows with the scope rather than the subscription that surfaced them; add a unit test with two subscriptions returning the same MG-scoped id.

#### F072 · medium · effort S · Managed-identity Azure RBAC view duplicates management-group-inherited assignments once per subscription
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/managed_identity.rs:251` · confirmed
**Problem.** The consumer side of F195: `per_sub` lists are flattened via `flat_map` with no `HashSet` on assignment id, so a managed identity holding Reader at a management group over N subscriptions renders N identical rows. `scope_level` explicitly classifies "Management group" scopes, so these rows are expected output and wrong by a factor of N.
**Proposal.** Extract a pure `dedupe_assignments(Vec<(String, RoleAssignment)>)` keyed by `RoleAssignment.id` (fallback `(scope, role_definition_id, principal_id)` because `id` is `Option<String>`), label above-subscription rows "(inherited)" or with the MG name, and unit-test one MG assignment returned by two subscriptions.

#### F376 · medium · effort M · Enterprise Credentials expiry badge truncates days ("0d left" for an expired credential) and is the fourth independent expiry helper
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/mod.rs:69` · confirmed
**Problem.** `cred_status` uses `chrono::Duration::num_days()`, which truncates toward zero, so a credential expired in the last 24 h yields `days == 0`, misses the `< 0` arm and renders "0d left". auth-and-consent.md:181-183 documents that the SSO path was fixed with `div_euclid` for exactly this misread. The verifier found the scope is wider: `core::audit::credentials.rs:24,40` also uses `num_days()` and maps 0 to ExpiringSoon, so the backend-fed dashboards misreport too. Thresholds are re-declared in three files.
**Proposal.** Floor in core (`summarize_credentials` and `ListCredentialStatus`, with an `EXPIRY_CRITICAL_DAYS` constant next to `EXPIRY_WARNING_DAYS`), then add one frontend `days_until`/`expiry_badge` helper and replace the five copies; unit-test that an `end` 12 h in the past renders "Expired".

- **F124 · `Calendars.ReadWrite` keeps its PS-ported medium weight while the net-new `Contacts.ReadWrite` is high** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/permissions.rs:145` · partially (the medium tier mirrors Constants.ps1:123-130 on purpose, so this is a risk-model call rather than a defect). Org-wide calendar write scores 5 and ranks Low while the same reach on contacts scores 10. Either promote it with a CHANGELOG note or add a comment stating the PS-parity exception, pinned with the file's `INTENTIONALLY_*` pattern.
- **F126 · Rule 7 labels multi-year certificates as "Long-lived secrets" and the dated-certificate path is unpinned** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/scoring.rs:931` · partially (nothing shows the legacy rule excluded certificates; the certain defect is the misnomer plus the missing test). `is_long_lived` is kind-agnostic, so a 2-year certificate scores +3 under "Long-lived secrets", in tension with Rule 17's advice to prefer certificates. Word the issue by kind and add table rows for a 400-day certificate and secret; treat exempting certificates as an explicit risk-model decision.
- **F136 · Rule 19 wording claims Entra-tenant reach for a personal-accounts-only audience** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/scoring.rs:598` · confirmed. `PersonalMicrosoftAccount` is folded into `(true, true)` and reported as "any Entra tenant and personal Microsoft accounts", which is wrong for an MSA-only app; the test table has no such row. Give the value its own arm with reach text "personal Microsoft accounts" and add the row.
- **F128 · Bare-value `least_privilege_alternative` assumes Graph, and the permission picker calls it while holding the resource id** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/permissions.rs:240` · confirmed. An operator selecting the Office 365 Exchange Online `Mail.Read` appRole is told to scope it via Exchange RBAC, which `is_scopable_exchange_resource_permission` says cannot apply there. Change `scope_hint` to take the resource and call `least_privilege_alternative_for`, and add the bare name to the repo_invariants GONE list.
- **F334 · SharePoint sites section is gated on the bare `Sites.*` value instead of the resource-aware helper** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/permissions_tab.rs:808` · partially (the core classifier `is_sharepoint_orgwide_permission` already accepts both Graph and SPO, so a Graph-only gate would be wrong; the realistic false positive is a third-party API exposing a `Sites.`-prefixed role). Gate both call sites (`permissions_tab.rs:808-814`, `enterprise_application_detail_pane/permissions.rs:253-258`) on the resource-aware classifier and decide explicitly whether SPO-resource apps keep the section.
- **F155 · 'Log Analytics Reader' satisfaction set omits Reader/Contributor/Owner** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/azure_roles.rs:43` · partially (readiness never returns Missing for the Azure plane, so a Reader-only operator sees Unknown, not a false "not held"). The catalog and OPERATOR-ROLES.md say "(or Reader)" and Microsoft confirms `*/read` covers query. Add `READER, CONTRIBUTOR, OWNER` to the arm and a test mirroring `control_plane_supersets_satisfy_reader_but_not_kv_data_plane`.

### Remediation and tenant mutations with the wrong outcome

#### F070 · high · effort M · Claims codec mis-models transformation-sourced schema entries: emits TransformationID instead of ID and conflates both on read
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/claims.rs:48` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** Graph's transformation-sourced schema entry carries both `ID` (joined by `OutputClaims[].ClaimTypeReferenceId`) and `TransformationID`. The DTO has one `id`; the writer emits it under `TransformationID` and omits `ID`, so every transformation claim this editor writes has nothing to join to and is never emitted. `parse_schema_entry` reads `ID` first, so Microsoft's own example parses with the transformation reference lost, and a no-op Save rewrites it pointing at a transformation that does not exist. The round-trip test masks this by using one string for all three ids.
```
let key = if e.source.as_deref() == Some("transformation") {
    "TransformationID"
```
**Proposal.** Add `transformation_id: Option<String>` to `ClaimSchemaEntryDto`; always emit `ID` from `id` and `TransformationID` from `transformation_id`; read both (also the `TransformationId` casing) into their own fields; add `data_type` to `TransformParamDto`. Replace the masking test with Graph's documented shape and expose the field in the ClaimsEditor.

#### F371 · high · effort M · SSO tab renders an unreadable claims policy as an empty editor; Save then detaches the real policy
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/sso_tab.rs:543` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** `get_sso_config` collapses any error from `list_assigned_claims_mapping_policies` (which needs the `Policy.ReadWrite.ApplicationConfiguration` write token even for the read) into `(None, None)`, and `SsoConfigDto.claims_policy: None` means either "no policy" or "read failed". The tab seeds `ClaimsEditorState::from_dto(&..unwrap_or_default())`, an empty editor with no warning. First Save triggers consent; `grant_consent` deliberately does not reload; second Save runs `set_claims_mapping`, which detaches every assigned policy and creates none for an empty editor. Live custom claims are silently detached.
**Proposal.** Add a discriminator to `SsoConfigDto` (`claims_policy_unavailable` or `claims_read_error`) set in the `Err` arm; in `SsoEditor` render a warn `Callout` with a "Load claims" button that requests `policy_write` consent and bumps `reload`, and disable "Save claims" until a successful read. Also bump `reload` after successful consent. Add a GUI test asserting Save is disabled and no `set_claims_mapping` call happens.

#### F394 · high · effort S · Exchange scoping section toasts "Migrated" on a `partial` AAP report; the audit dialog gates correctly
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/exchange_scoping_section.rs:324` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** The gate is `dry_run || !r.failures.is_empty()`, but the backend sets `status = "partial"` inside a successful item (policy kept because a grant is still org-wide) and pushes to `warnings`, and a cancelled or dead-session run arrives with empty `items`, non-empty `unattempted` and `incomplete: true`, all with `failures` empty. The section toasts "Migrated N policy(ies)", fires `on_changed`, and never mounts `AapMigrationReportView`, whose stated job is that partial never reads as success. The toast noun is also wrong; items are per app.
```
if dry_run || !r.failures.is_empty() {
```
**Proposal.** Add `AapMigrationReport::is_clean()` (`!dry_run && failures.is_empty() && !incomplete && unattempted.is_empty() && !items.is_empty() && all(status == "migrated")`), use it in both `do_migrate` and `migrate_legacy_scope.rs`, keep `mig_result` inline unless clean, toast "Migrated N app(s) / removed M policy(ies)", and unit-test the three non-clean shapes.

#### F001 · medium · effort M · create_application returns Err after the app registration was already created, so no cache is invalidated and a retry creates a duplicate
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/applications/mod.rs:322` · confirmed
**Problem.** `POST /applications` lands, then `ensure_service_principal(..).await?` or `add_password(..).await?` propagates and the command returns `Err` without `invalidate_app_lists`. `apps_pairing`, `app_name_index` and `search_corpus` are pinned with a 60-minute TTL, so the new app is invisible and the natural retry creates a second registration. `ensure_service_principal` has no replication retry, so this is reachable beyond 403/429. The verifier downgraded to medium: the operator sees an error and the list Refresh busts the cache.
**Proposal.** Give `create_application_core` the `(result, Option<UiError>)` shape `downgrade_application_permission_core` already has, collecting SP/secret failures once the registration exists; call `invalidate_app_lists` whenever `application` exists and then surface the error. Update the `bulk.rs` and `restore.rs` callers and add a wiremock test (201 then 403).

#### F004 · medium · effort M · grant_single_permission_core and grant_admin_consent_core return Err after real writes so the command layer never invalidates
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permissions.rs:648` · confirmed
**Problem.** In the single-grant core the `requiredResourceAccess` PATCH lands, then `ensure_service_principal` or `resolve_resource_sp` fails with `?`; in the consent core the SP is created and then the idempotency `try_join` fails (a 403 on `oauth2PermissionGrants` is realistic). The command receives `Err` and busts nothing, so the cached detail shows the old manifest and a new SP is missing from `sp_index`, enterprise and search for the TTL. `bulk.rs:470-482` and `restore.rs:274` share the gap. The repo solved this shape at `:887-890`.
**Proposal.** Adopt the `(outcome, Option<UiError>)` return in both grant cores, collect later errors once the PATCH or SP creation has landed, and have `grant_single_permission` / `grant_admin_consent` (and the bulk and restore callers) invalidate on `manifest_changed || sp_created` before surfacing the error; mirror the two `downgrade` wiremock tests.

#### F071 · medium · effort M · set_claims_mapping leaks one orphan claimsMappingPolicy per save and swallows the listing error
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:1643` · partially (one policy may be assigned to several SPs, so an in-place PATCH needs an `appliesTo` guard the original proposal lacked)
**Problem.** `remove_claims_mapping_policy` is a `$ref` DELETE that unassigns but never deletes the tenant-level policy, and `apply_claims_policy` POSTs a fresh one on every save, so the tenant accumulates one orphan under `/policies/claimsMappingPolicies` per "Save claims". The comment's premise "effectively replace-only" is wrong; Graph documents Update and Delete. `if let Ok(existing)` turns a transient listing failure into an attempt to assign a second policy, which Graph rejects with an opaque 400.
**Proposal.** Add `update_claims_mapping_policy` (PATCH) and `delete_claims_mapping_policy` to `policies.rs`; propagate the listing error with `?`; when exactly one policy is assigned and this SP is its sole `appliesTo` subject, PATCH in place, otherwise unassign, create and assign; when the new policy is empty, unassign then DELETE only if `appliesTo` is empty. Add a CHANGELOG entry.

#### F074 · medium · effort S · OIDC create leaves the client-secret lifetime unbounded, unlike the SAML cert lifetime and the rotation path
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:356` · confirmed
**Problem.** `input.secret_lifetime_days.unwrap_or(180)` reaches `add_password` unchecked, while the SAML path bounds `cert_lifetime_days` before instantiate and `keyvault.rs:85` clamps 1..=730. The wizard field is free text. `0` mints an already-expired secret; the verifier found that `u32::MAX` days overflows chrono's date range inside `add_password` and panics after `instantiate_application_template` has already created the app and SP.
**Proposal.** Add `resolve_secret_lifetime_days(Option<u32>) -> Result<u32, UiError>` with bounds 1..=730 shared as a `core` constant with `keyvault.rs`, call it before `instantiate_application_template` and reuse it in `configure_oidc`; add a table test mirroring the certificate-lifetime one.

#### F340 · medium · effort M · Identifier-URI add/remove replays the tab's stale snapshot as a full replace (lost-update), contradicting the module doc
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/expose_api_tab.rs:123` · confirmed
**Problem.** `uris` is a `StoredValue` snapshotted at mount; Add and Remove mutate it and call `set_identifier_uris(&list)`, and the backend PATCHes the client-supplied list with no live read, whereas `upsert_api_scope` re-reads live. If another admin or the SSO wizard (which also writes `identifier_uris`) added a URI after the tab loaded, it is silently dropped. The module doc claims the backend re-reads live state before every write.
**Proposal.** Add `add_identifier_uri` / `remove_identifier_uri` backend commands that GET the live list, apply the delta, validate and PATCH (like `upsert_api_scope`), or call `get_expose_api` immediately before computing the new list; update the module doc.

#### F403 · medium · effort S · ScopeMailboxButton's SP path discards `ExchangeAccessResult.warnings` (hard-coded 0)
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/dialogs/scope_remediation.rs:111` · confirmed
**Problem.** The AppReg arm maps `(removed, res.warnings.len())`; the SP arm maps `(removed, 0)` although both bindings return the same DTO. The Exchange section documents that the most common warning ("a management scope already exists with a different group set") means the requested groups were NOT applied. On the audit Fix for an enterprise app or managed identity that warning is dropped, the Fix disappears via `on_done` and the toast says "Scoped mailbox access".
**Proposal.** Use `res.warnings.len()` on the SP arm; better, return the `Vec<String>` from both arms and keep the modal open listing warnings, calling `on_done` only when they are empty or after the operator dismisses them.

#### F333 · medium · effort S · Permissions tab consent/downgrade outcome notes are set right before `on_changed` unmounts the tab
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/permissions_tab.rs:116` · confirmed
**Problem.** `on_changed` re-runs the pane's `LocalResource` and the `Suspend` rebuilds the subtree, so the `consent_result` and `scope_note` Callouts flash at most for the refetch. The `consent_result` Callout is the only place `failures.len()` appears, and the success toast omits failures, so a partial consent (`GrantResult.failures`, collected rather than errored by the backend) reads as an unqualified success. The comment at `:205-206` is stale. Medium because the reloaded table shows live per-row state.
**Proposal.** Follow the credentials tab's documented pattern and report via the session toast stack before `on_changed`: `toast_error` naming the failure count (and first message) when `!r.failures.is_empty()`, `toast_success` otherwise; `toast_success(note)` for the downgrade; drop the two Callouts and fix the stale comment.

#### F396 · medium · effort S · ScopeWizard preseed uses `scope_kind_for` while `mechanism` uses `cart_mechanism`; a held Sites.* row on Office 365 SharePoint Online reaches a scoped mode with a silent no-op Grant button
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/scope_wizard.rs:484` · confirmed
**Problem.** `scope_mode` and `mechanism` are independent signals set from predicates of different strictness. The verifier found this reachable today: `held_permissions_panel.rs:43-44` gates the Scope button with value-only `is_sharepoint_orgwide`, so `Sites.Read.All` held on the SPO resource seeds the wizard, `scope_kind_for(Some(SPO), ..)` is `None`, and the wizard sits in `ScopeMode::Managed` with `mechanism == None`. Step 3 claims a scoped grant and "Grant access" returns silently at `let Some(kind) = mechanism.get_untracked() else { return; }`.
**Proposal.** Derive `effective_mode` (OrgWide whenever `mechanism` is `None`) and read it in `run_apply`, `review_line`, `review_targets`, `strip_warning`, `targets_label`, `consent_and_retry` and the radios; use `cart_mechanism` in the preseed; replace the two silent returns with an error message; unit-test a delegated preseed.

#### F395 · medium · effort S · ScopeWizard: selecting "Org-wide" on a SharePoint or SharePoint-item cart is a one-way door
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/scope_wizard.rs:819` · confirmed
**Problem.** For SharePoint and SharePointItem carts the scoped panel is shown only while `scope_mode` is Sites or Items, and the sole radio sets OrgWide; nothing on step 2 sets the mode back, unlike the Exchange block with its Managed/Existing radios. Recovery is Back to step 1 and toggling a permission out and in. The GUI test `orgwide_option_grants_without_scoping` covers Exchange only.
**Proposal.** Render a "Specific sites (recommended)" / "Specific libraries, folders & files (recommended)" radio above each panel, or drive all radios from a `mode_options(kind)` table; add a GUI test that returns from org-wide to Sites mode.

#### F339 · medium · effort S · Lifetime/validity inputs are silently coerced (garbage to default, out of range clamped) instead of validated
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/credentials_tab.rs:507` · confirmed
**Problem.** The rotate and generate-certificate dialogs do `parse::<u32>().unwrap_or(180).clamp(1, 730)` and `unwrap_or(365).clamp(1, 1095)` on free-text inputs with no error path, while the new-secret dialog's `resolve_expiry_fields` returns `Err`. "abc", "0", "3650" or "18" (a typo for 180) mint a credential with a lifetime the operator did not type. `generate_self_signed_certificate` has no backend cap, so the frontend clamp is the only one.
**Proposal.** Extract `parse_days(raw, min, max) -> Result<u32, String>`, surface the message via the shared `error` signal before dispatch, add table tests, and consider `<Select>` presets matching the new-secret dialog.

#### F372 · medium · effort S · "Rotate and activate immediately" reveals the show-once certificate, then the reload it triggers tears the reveal down
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/sso_tab.rs:622` · confirmed
**Problem.** `rotate_cert` sets `rotated_cert` then bumps the outer `reload`; the `SsoContent` Suspense drops the current `SsoEditor`, where `rotated_cert` lives, so the `<pre class="secret-reveal">` is gone before the operator can copy it. The staged path is immune because `staged_pem` lives outside that Suspense. Medium because the public SAML certificate is recoverable from the federation metadata URL.
**Proposal.** Lift `rotated_cert` (and `cert_subject`) to `SsoContent`, or stop bumping the outer `reload` on rotate and bump a rollover-panel reload instead; render through the shared `CopyBlock`; add a GUI test in `sso_rollover.rs`.

#### F164 · medium · effort M · Exchange transport replays non-idempotent cmdlets (New-/Remove-) on 5xx and network errors
`crates/azapptoolkit-exchange/src/client/transport.rs:263` · confirmed · also reported as F140 (`transport.rs:265`, partially: the "duplicate role assignment survives removal" harm is refuted, since Exchange rejects the duplicate and removal sweeps every assignment)
**Problem.** `send_core` open-codes attempt, delay and `MAX_RETRIES` and retries every POST on a reqwest error and on 429/5xx with no `RetryClass`; the exchange crate never adopted `with_retries`, so `trust.rs` (which skips files without `with_retries(`) is vacuous for it while `http_retry.rs` claims Exchange is unified. A replayed `New-ManagementRoleAssignment` after a committed write fails as a duplicate, which `grants.rs:118` records as `failed to assign`, keeping the org-wide grant; a replayed `Remove-ApplicationAccessPolicy` reports `partial` while the policy is gone.
**Proposal.** Port `send_core` onto `with_retries(label, class, ..)` with a `retry_class_for(cmdlet)` helper (named exactly so `trust.rs:167` matches): `Get-`, `Test-`, `Search-` and paging Idempotent, `New-`, `Remove-`, `Set-`, `Update-` NonIdempotent; extend `trust.rs` to flag a transport that calls `.send()` without `with_retries(`; add wiremock tests for a 502 on `New-` (not resent) and `Get-` (resent); fix the http_retry.rs doc.

#### F167 · medium · effort S · Tenant-wide list reads that gate the irreversible group delete use invoke_optional, so a misclassified 400 reads as 'no references'
`crates/azapptoolkit-exchange/src/client/rbac.rs:220` · confirmed
**Problem.** `list_management_scopes` and `get_application_access_policies` are identity-less list-alls routed through `invoke_optional`, whose `is_not_found_body` matches any body containing "not found". A 400 with that text becomes `Ok(vec![])`; `retired_scope_groups` then reports `reference_check_complete: true` and `delete_exchange_scope_group` finds zero references before `Remove-DistributionGroup`. `list_service_principals` already uses `invoke_command`. The trigger is hypothetical but the consequence is irreversible.
**Proposal.** Switch both list-alls to `invoke_command` (keep the `-Identity` getters on `invoke_optional`); add a wiremock test that a 400 "not found" on `Get-ManagementScope {}` is `Err`; optionally tighten `is_not_found_body` to cmdlet-shaped phrasings.

#### F168 · medium · effort S · DN set comparisons are case-sensitive in the consolidation path but case-folded in the post-write proof
`crates/azapptoolkit-exchange/src/targets.rs:716` · confirmed
**Problem.** The crate documents (targets.rs:480-486) that Exchange echoes DNs in its own casing and folds the post-write proof (`rbac.rs:197`), yet `targets.rs:716`, `scope_group.rs:540` and `scope_group.rs:744` compare raw strings, each pairing an echoed RecipientFilter DN with a group-object DN. `reconcile_scope_filter` can pass the folded proof then fail `scope_filter_agrees` with `scope_filter_mismatch`, blocking a correct migration; `move_exchange_scope_to_managed_group` can re-repoint and list the app's own managed group as a retired candidate. The failure direction is safe.
**Proposal.** Add `ScopeGroups::same_groups_as` and `same_dn(a, b)` helpers (folded) and use them at the three sites; extend `consolidation_is_a_no_op_when_the_scope_is_already_managed` with a case-variant DN; consider `to_lowercase()` over `to_ascii_lowercase()` in `folded_dns`.

- **F061 · grant_exchange_mailbox_access creates the Entra service principal before validating that anything is scopable** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/exchange/grants.rs:332` · confirmed. `ensure_service_principal` runs before `require_scopable_targets`, so a "nothing to scope" error leaves a new SP behind and the list caches are busted only on success; the "(line ~450)" comment is stale. Reorder to validate first and fix the comment.
- **F008 · Certificate upload does not parse the pasted PEM/DER; a private-key paste is forwarded to Graph** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/applications/credentials.rs:256` · partially (the dialog sends `end_date_time: None`, so no expiry mismatch exists today, and a cert-plus-key bundle passes base64 only when the DER length is a multiple of 3). `normalize_cert_blob` strips every `-----` line regardless of label and checks only base64. Reject non-`CERTIFICATE` labels, parse the DER with `x509_parser` (already in the lock) and show the thumbprint before upload.
- **F347 · SecretRevealDialog reports 'Copied' even when the clipboard write fails, and a reflex Escape dismisses the one-time secret** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/dialogs/secret_reveal_dialog.rs:21` · confirmed. `let _ = JsFuture::from(promise).await; copied.set(true)` ignores rejection and `use_escape(|| true, ..)` closes unconditionally while `on_close` releases the reload. Set `copied` only on `Ok`, show a Callout on `Err`, and gate Escape or require the Done button for both one-time reveals.

### Backup and restore

#### F039 · high · effort S · Backup Pass 3 (managed identities) is not session-latched and swallows per-MI read failures without recording them in `skipped`
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/backup.rs:675` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** `backup_managed_identities` takes no `SessionDead`; per-item and whole-batch failures both become empty Vecs via `Result::unwrap_or_default` / `.unwrap_or_default()`, and the caller returns `Ok(TenantBackup{..})` with no `session.is_dead()` check, unlike after passes 1 and 2. A refresh-token death or exhausted retry budget during Pass 3 yields an Ok manifest whose managed identities all carry `held_app_roles: []`; restore then rebinds nothing and reports success. `SkippedObject.kind` reserves `managedIdentity` but no code path ever emits it.
```
.map(|v| v.into_iter().map(Result::unwrap_or_default).collect()),
```
**Proposal.** Thread `session: &SessionDead` in, keep the per-item `Result`, call `note_graph_failures` on it, push a `SkippedObject::new("managedIdentity", ..)` per `Err` while still emitting the MI with empty roles, then mirror passes 1-2 with `if session.is_dead() { return Err(..) }`; return `(Vec<ManagedIdentityBackup>, Vec<SkippedObject>)`. Extend the wiremock degrade test with a 500 on `appRoleAssignments`.

#### F424 · high · effort M · DR view tells the operator a re-run restore "recreates only what is missing"; the backend creates every app unconditionally and the Restore button stays live after a run
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/dr.rs:502` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** `restore_tenant` Pass 1 calls `create_application_core` (a bare POST) for every manifest app with no destination lookup, and Entra does not enforce displayName uniqueness. The session-expired path returns `Ok(report)` with `session_expired = true`, and the callout tells the operator to "run the restore again"; doing so duplicates every already-restored app with a fresh appId, paired SP, regenerated secret and re-granted consent. `plan`/`loaded` are never cleared after `report.set`, so the primary Restore button reappears and a second click re-runs on the same manifest. The MI runbook item (restore.rs:750-752) gives the same instruction.
**Proposal.** Short term: reword the callout to say a re-run re-creates the apps listed above, and clear `loaded`/`plan` after a report renders.
