**Problem.** The `None` arm calls the client directly under `try_join`, so `state.single_flight(sp_index_key)` never sees it, and nothing gates `apps_pairing_key` either:
```rust
let (apps, sps) = futures::future::try_join(
    client.list_applications_all(query, Some(APPS_MAX)),
    client.list_service_principals_index(),
```
cache.rs documents that the sibling `indexes_cached` was rewritten to avoid exactly this, and `sp_index_watch` exists only to serve the bypass. On a cold tenant, Home mounting plus a click on App Registrations runs two `/applications` and two `/servicePrincipals` scans. Any concurrent gated reader adds a third SP scan.
**Proposal.** Replace the arm with `try_join(client.list_applications_all(..), cache::sp_index_cached(&state, &client, &tenant_id))`, which keeps concurrency and inherits the gate and the correctly keyed watch. Wrap the miss path in `state.single_flight(&cache_key)` with a post-lock re-check as `search_corpus` does; `sp_index_watch` and the `Some(cached_index)` arm then become unnecessary.

#### F025 · medium · effort M · bulk_remove_expired_credentials walks the whole tenant even for an explicit selection and hardcodes 10_000 instead of APPS_MAX
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/bulk.rs:163` · partially (the `ids.contains` cost is sub-millisecond, and a selected app beyond the cap cannot exist because the list and audit truncate at the same `APPS_MAX`)
**Problem.** The only frontend caller always passes `Some(&ids)`, yet the command walks every `/applications` page to the 10 000 cap before `retain`. Every "Fix all N" therefore pays a full tenant walk that the repo's own cache comments call "tens of seconds". The literal `10_000` duplicates `applications::APPS_MAX`, which audit.rs, credentials.rs and backup.rs use by name.
**Proposal.** When `object_ids` is `Some`, fetch exactly those apps via `batch_get_json::<Application>` on `/applications/{id}?$select=...` with a per-id fallback on a whole-batch failure, and keep the walk only for the `None` tenant sweep. Replace the literal with `super::applications::APPS_MAX`.

#### F026 · medium · effort S · Bulk expired-credential sweep busts both tenant-wide indexes (invalidate_app_lists) for a credential-only mutation; the per-app fix uses the tiered invalidation
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/bulk.rs:283` · confirmed
**Problem.** The sweep's only mutation is `client.remove_password`, yet on success it drops the SP pairing index, the app-name index, the Enterprise Apps list and every mailbox-scope verdict:
```rust
if any_removed {
    super::applications::invalidate_app_lists(&state.cache, &tenant_id);
}
```
applications/cache.rs:134-147 explains why credential mutations must use `invalidate_app_credentials`, and the per-app `remediate_remove_expired_credentials` follows that rule. After every sweep the operator pays a full app plus SP re-enumeration for a change that touched neither.
**Proposal.** Loop over the summaries with removed keys calling `invalidate_app_credentials(&cache, &tenant_id, &object_id)`, or add an `invalidate_app_credentials_many` variant. Extend `repo_invariants/cache.rs` to pin that no credential-only command calls `invalidate_app_lists`.

#### F375 · medium · effort M · Opening the SSO tab runs the SP→app→app-SSO Graph chain twice and reads the SP a third time
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:1745` · confirmed
**Problem.** `get_sso_summary` calls `get_sso_config` again (SP fields, claims policies, `find_application_by_app_id`, app SSO fields, none cached). It only wraps the same fields plus URL formulas that are pure functions of tenant and app id. The tab fires the summary serially after config resolves, and `SigningCertRolloverPanel` reads the SP a third time. Each open therefore costs about eight Graph requests for data the tab already holds. The summary also crosses IPC as an untagged `serde_json::Value` that the frontend re-parses and swallows as "Summary unavailable." on mismatch.
**Proposal.** Either return the summary (or the rollover DTO) as fields of `SsoConfigDto` so one command feeds the tab, or build the summary client-side from `SsoConfigDto` plus URL helpers moved into `azapptoolkit-dto`. Delete `get_sso_summary`, or type it as a `#[serde(tag = "protocol")]` enum (see F081).

#### F336 · medium · effort L · Every mutation rebuilds the whole detail pane and remounts all visited tabs (root cause of the deferred-reload workarounds)
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/application_detail_pane.rs:136` · confirmed
**Problem.** `bump_reload` re-runs the detail resource, and the `Suspend` closure yields a brand-new header, `TabBar` and every keep-alive tab. With `Suspense` (no `Transition` exists in web-rs) the skeleton flashes first. The keep-alive latch preserves state across tab switches only. Removing one owner therefore re-runs every visited tab's IPC resources (federated credentials, expose API, authentication, activity, CA, mail scopes) and discards half-typed forms. It also forces the credentials tab's three "defer on_changed until the dialog is dismissed" dances, which a browser test pins and any refactor must adapt.
**Proposal.** Keep a long-lived `RwSignal<Option<Arc<ApplicationDetail>>>` on the pane, update it from the resource, and mount header, tab bar and tabs once outside the `Suspend`, showing `DetailSkeleton` only while it is `None`. As a cheap interim step, switch `Suspense` to `Transition` to remove the flash.

#### F111 · medium · effort M · Token endpoint calls have no retry/backoff; the readiness probe fans out six refreshes at once and a 429/5xx surfaces as a terminal `token_exchange`
`crates/azapptoolkit-auth/src/service/mod.rs:154` · confirmed
**Problem.** `post_token` is a single `send()`; a 429 with `Retry-After` or a transient 5xx becomes `TokenExchange("HTTP 429")`. The Graph transport fetches the bearer once outside `with_retries`, so no client retry budget covers it. The readiness checklist fans about six token requests out concurrently. The verifier adds that `restore_session` at launch on a network blip returns `Ok(None)`, forcing a full browser sign-in although the keyring token is fine.
**Proposal.** Wrap the send in `core::http_retry::with_retries`, classifying 429, 5xx and transport errors as `Retry`. Retry `refresh_token` grants freely (they are idempotent); for the single-use `authorization_code` exchange retry only transport errors before a response arrived. Add a wiremock test: 429 with `Retry-After: 1`, then 200, exactly two requests.

#### F179 · medium · effort S · Grant read-through cache is attached to the per-SP appRoleAssignments read; the tenant-wide appRoleAssignedTo read it describes is uncached
`crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/roles_grants.rs:41` · confirmed
**Problem.** `list_app_role_assignments` carries the read-through cache, key `assigned_to:{sp}`, and a comment describing the audit's `prefetch_graph_app_roles` and the consent lens. Both of those callers, plus permission_tester.rs and enterprise_application.rs, call `list_app_role_assigned_to`, which ends in a bare `collect_all_pages` with no cache. caching-and-search.md names that read as the one dominating the audit and consent view, so the intended win is not realised. Meanwhile 18 per-SP pre-write `existing` reads get a 60-minute staleness window for nothing.
**Proposal.** Move the read-through block to `list_app_role_assigned_to`, drop or re-key the cache on `list_app_role_assignments`, and fix the comments; mutators already sweep the `grants:` prefix so invalidation is unchanged. Add a wiremock test with `.expect(1)` on `appRoleAssignedTo` called twice.

- **F364 · `use_debounced` leaks one `Closure::once_into_js` per cancelled timer (every keystroke that resets the debounce)** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/hooks/use_debounced.rs:35` · confirmed. wasm-bindgen documents that an uncalled `FnOnce` closure leaks, and every keystroke inside the 300 ms window cancels one; toast.rs and the two blur timers leak the same way at lower frequency. Own the `Closure` in a `StoredValue` and drop it on cancel, or use one persistent closure per hook.
- **F130 · `deduped()` clones grants, scopes and `mail_scopes` per scored principal, measurable but negligible at current throughput** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/types.rs:315` · partially (at 193k apps/s scoring takes about 50 ms for 10k apps and `format!`-built issue strings dominate, so "the one avoidable allocation cluster" is overstated). Use borrowed keys for the dedup sets or take `AppPermissions` by value when the code is next touched.
- **F188 · list_conditional_access_policies pages without $top, contrary to the 'every paged read sends $top' invariant** — `crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/directory.rs:80` · confirmed. The read follows nextLink over the one-shot, unretried `scoped_get` with the default page of 100, and Graph supports `$top` on this endpoint. Add `?$top={MAX_PAGE_SIZE}` and extend the paging test to match it on page 1.
- **F232 · reqwest http2 is off, so every Graph/Exchange fan-out multiplexes over HTTP/1.1 connections** — `Cargo.toml:29` · confirmed. `default-features = false` drops `http2` and nothing re-enables it, so N in-flight requests hold N TLS connections per host; nothing records this as deliberate, and the auth crate special-cases TLS-intercepting proxies whose behaviour ALPN can change. Add `"http2"`, benchmark a fan-out before and after, and record the decision next to the opt-level bench.

### Dependency hygiene

One medium finding (a hard API-version retirement date) and nine low ones about manifests: declared-but-unused crates, inline requirements that belong in `[workspace.dependencies]`, feature sets wider than any consumer needs, and one alias cargo cannot resolve.

#### F257 · medium · effort S · Key Vault control-plane api-version 2023-07-01 is retired on 2027-02-27; other ARM/KV versions are merely behind
`crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/client.rs:19` · partially (the premise was understated and the original target 2024-11-01 would itself be retired; the record's proposal is corrected to 2026-02-01)
**Problem.** `KEYVAULT_API = "2023-07-01"` drives the vault listing, and Microsoft states that all Key Vault control-plane versions before 2026-02-01 retire on 27 February 2027 with no exception. `LOG_ANALYTICS_WORKSPACES_API` is four stable releases behind, the data-plane `7.4` predates date-based versions but is explicitly unaffected, and `AUTHORIZATION_API`/`SUBSCRIPTIONS_API` are current. No constant carries a source link or review date, and no repo doc records an api-version review policy.
**Proposal.** Bump `KEYVAULT_API` to `2026-02-01` and confirm the read-only list response is unchanged; optionally bump the Log Analytics and data-plane versions. Add a `// Source: <url>, reviewed YYYY-MM` comment per constant and note the 2027-02-27 date in DEVELOPMENT.md; the wiremock tests reference the constants and follow automatically.

- **F219 · proc-macro-error2 2.0.1 triggers a future-incompatibility hard error (E0365) in the wasm build; the pinned toolchain hides it until the next MSRV bump** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/Cargo.toml:22` · confirmed. `cargo report future-incompatibilities` shows `pub use proc_macro;` in a transitive dependency of `leptos_macro`, and no bump checklist or CI step surfaces it (the anchor is really line 28, the `leptos` dependency). Add the report to the MSRV-bump checklist and a non-gating CI print; track leptos 0.9, with a `[patch]` as fallback.
- **F214 · http_error_enum! consumers must carry an unnamed `thiserror` dependency, document it at the macro and in the manifests rather than trying to re-export it** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/http_error.rs:65` · partially; also reported as F226 (partially). Both original proposals to re-export via `$crate` were refuted: thiserror-impl hard-codes `::thiserror::__private` paths, so arm, graph and keyvault must keep their own dependency, and core itself does not depend on thiserror. Add a sentence to the macro doc and a `# needed by core::http_error_enum!` comment beside each manifest line, plus `cargo-machete` ignore metadata if that tool is adopted.
- **F173 · Cargo.toml declares unused `url` and `tokio` dependencies and a per-crate `bytes` instead of a workspace dependency** — `crates/azapptoolkit-exchange/Cargo.toml:13` · confirmed; also reported as F212, the cross-manifest sweep (arm and keyvault `tracing`, exchange `url`, desktop `anyhow` plus `thiserror`, web-rs `tracing`; the `thiserror` machete hits on arm, graph and keyvault are false positives per F214). Nothing in exchange/src names `url` or `tokio` outside tests, and tokio is already a dev-dependency. Remove the six dead declarations, regenerate both lockfiles in the same commit, and consider a `cargo machete` step in `just verify-full`. Overlaps F223 and F209.
- **F227 · bytes declared in four crates outside [workspace.dependencies]** — `crates/azapptoolkit-graph/Cargo.toml:14` · confirmed. graph, exchange, keyvault and arm each pin `bytes = "1"` inline against the documented workspace-dependency pattern. Add `bytes` under `# HTTP` in the root table and switch the four manifests; `webbrowser` is optional uniformity, not a rule violation.
- **F209 · Both crates declare `tracing` but never use it, list `tokio` as a runtime dependency used only by `#[tokio::test]`, and pin `bytes = "1"` outside `[workspace.dependencies]`** — `crates/azapptoolkit-keyvault/Cargo.toml:13` · confirmed; also reported as F224 (partially: the claim that keyvault and arm "emit nothing on 429/5xx" is wrong, since `core::http_retry::with_retries` logs centrally, so no per-crate logging should be added). Delete `tracing` from keyvault, arm and web-rs (where no subscriber exists), move `tokio` to dev-dependencies, and hoist `bytes` (F227).
- **F231 · The futures facade's default `executor` feature pulls futures-executor into Windows/macOS builds for nothing** — `crates/azapptoolkit-graph/Cargo.toml:23` · partially (rewriting paths to `futures_util` is needless churn; the facade's own feature flags achieve the drop). All 25 use sites are futures-util items. Set the workspace entry to `default-features = false, features = ["std", "async-await"]` with a comment, as the repo already does for oauth2 and reqwest.
- **F230 · Workspace tokio feature set is the union; library crates inherit net/io-util/rt-multi-thread/macros they don't use** — `Cargo.toml:26` · partially (the original trim omitted `rt`, which auth's `spawn_blocking` needs, and auth's missing tokio dev-dependency; as first written it would break auth). Only auth opens raw sockets and no crate builds a runtime. Trim the workspace entry to `sync` and `time`, give auth `net`, `io-util` and `rt` plus a dev-dep with `macros` and `rt`, and give src-tauri `macros` and `rt-multi-thread`.
- **F221 · [workspace.dependencies] keyring-core alias names a package cargo cannot resolve, and no member uses it** — `Cargo.toml:43` · confirmed. `package = "keyring_core"` fails resolution (cargo does not normalise underscores) and auth declares its own `keyring-core = "1.0"`, so the entry is dead until someone follows the workspace rule and hits a confusing error. Drop the alias and switch auth to `workspace = true`; no lockfile change.
- **F223 · src-tauri declares anyhow and thiserror but uses neither; anyhow is then unused workspace-wide** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml:42` · confirmed. No file under src-tauri names either crate and it does not invoke `http_error_enum!`; `desktop` is the only workspace consumer of anyhow. Delete both lines and `anyhow = "1"` from the root table; no lockfile change.

---

## Enhancements and feature opportunities

This bucket holds the 108 verified findings that propose an improvement or a new capability rather than report a defect. None is high severity; 30 are medium and 78 are low, with 58 small, 45 medium and 5 large efforts. Two pairs describe the same gap at the same file and are merged below (F042 with F428, F264 with F276), giving 106 entries. Of the 108, 66 were confirmed as stated and 42 were confirmed with a correction; each correction is carried into its entry. The findings are grouped by product area, and within each area high, then medium, then low.

### Audit and remediation

#### F027 · medium · effort M · Exchange mailbox-scoping unavailability (no Exchange client, or breaker tripped) is invisible in the run result, the workbench and every export
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/audit.rs:899` · confirmed
**Problem.** `audit_exchange_client` logs Exchange unavailability at `info!` and returns `None`; `score_one` flips `exo_tripped` at :1569 with another `info!`. Neither reaches `AuditRunResult`, `AuditExportCoverage` or `coverage_sentences`, which already carries the analogous sign-in caveat at :636-642. For a non-Exchange-admin, apps already confined via Exchange RBAC are listed as Critical "Org-wide mailbox access" with a Scope Fix that needs the same client and will fail, and the run is cached and exported as complete.
```rust
Err(err) => {
    tracing::info!(?err, "audit: Exchange scoping unavailable");
    None
```
**Proposal.** Add a serde-defaulted `mailbox_scoping_resolved: bool` to `AuditRunResult`, `AuditExportCoverage` and `CachedAuditRun`, set false when `exo` is `None` or `exo_tripped` is set at the end of the run. Add a `coverage_sentences` line and a Callout in the Org-wide mailbox finding group, and keep the flag out of `degraded` so the run stays cacheable, matching the sign-in precedent.

#### F127 · medium · effort M · Three emitted issue markers have no finding group or posture count, so unconfinable org-wide reach is scored but invisible in the workbench
`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/types.rs:564` · confirmed
**Problem.** `UNSCOPABLE_LEGACY_MAILBOX`, `UNCONFINABLE_MAILBOX` and `UNCONFINABLE_SHAREPOINT` are emitted by the scorer, but `issue_marker` (filter.rs:61-95) and `GROUP_CATALOG` (groups.rs:47-136) have no key for them, posture.rs has no count, and row.rs tab routing is the only consumer. An app holding `Contacts.Read` on Office 365 Exchange Online or `Sites.Manage.All` on Office 365 SharePoint Online reaches every mailbox or site but appears in no group, count or filter. The verifier notes the Permissions tab and held-permissions panel do show a legacy-grant callout, but nothing in the workbench findings or posture does, and the catalog's own principle (groups.rs:43-45) favours advisory groups over hiding a scored finding.
**Proposal.** Add an advisory group (key `unconfinable_reach`, title "Org-wide reach the toolkit cannot confine") in `GROUP_CATALOG` and `issue_marker`, matching the three markers with `starts_with`, no remediation kinds, tab "permissions", counted in `posture_counts`. Add the three markers to `emitted_issue_markers_are_stable`. F402 constrains the wording.

#### F402 · medium · effort S · Neither `UNCONFINABLE_MAILBOX`/`UNCONFINABLE_SHAREPOINT` nor `UNSCOPABLE_LEGACY_MAILBOX` belongs to a finding group, so the findings-first pane hides them
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/audit_view/filter.rs:60` · partially (the example was wrong: a legacy Exchange Online `Mail.Read` grant yields `UNSCOPABLE_LEGACY_MAILBOX`, not `UNCONFINABLE_MAILBOX`, and the two carry deliberately different recommendations)
**Problem.** The `issue_marker` arms cover high_risk_perms, high_risk_delegated, external_exposure, orgwide_mailbox, scoped_mailbox, legacy_mailbox_scope, orgwide_sharepoint, redundant_perms, scoped_sites and ownership, then `_ => return None`; only row.rs:62-66 knows the three markers. The scorer deliberately splits confinable org-wide reach (Fix) from unconfinable reach (remove the grant), but the default findings-first pane has a group only for the first. A principal whose sole mailbox problem is an unconfinable grant is invisible there unless another rule fires. No doc says these markers should stay ungrouped.
**Proposal.** Add advisory Actionable group(s) with no bulk action, keeping the legacy-resource advice separate from the UNCONFINABLE one: either two groups (`legacy_orgwide_grants` pointing at `LegacyExchangeGrantsCallout`, `unconfinable_orgwide` advising review or re-declaration on Graph) or one group whose Detail column quotes the matched line. Add them to `PostureCounts` and to `issue_marker_findings_match_exactly_their_finding`. Same gap as F127.

#### F125 · medium · effort S · Read halves of several high-weighted families score zero (Contacts.Read, MailboxSettings.Read, Device.Read.All, Notes.Read.All, Application.Read.All)
`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/permissions.rs:319` · confirmed
**Problem.** The MEDIUM table (permissions.rs:131-157) holds `Mail.Read`, `Files.Read.All`, `Sites.Read.All`, `Chat.Read.All` and `Calendars.Read` but not the five named values, which appear only on the narrower side of the SUBSUMED table. `Contacts.Read` is mapped by `graph_mail_role` (scoping.rs:87), so Rule 11 raises "Organization-wide mailbox access" for it while it contributes 0 points against 5 for `Calendars.Read`. `MailboxSettings.Read` reads every mailbox's forwarding settings, and `Device.Read.All`/`Application.Read.All` are tenant-wide reads on par with `Group.Read.All`. The guard test `every_broader_subsuming_permission_carries_a_risk_weight` walks only the broader side, so the omissions are invisible.
**Proposal.** Add `Contacts.Read`, `MailboxSettings.Read`, `Device.Read.All` and `Notes.Read.All` (and decide on `Application.Read.All`) to `MEDIUM_RISK_APP_PERMISSIONS` with a CHANGELOG note. Extend the guard test with a narrower-side walk for the mailbox family, every value `graph_mail_role` maps being HIGH, MEDIUM, or listed in an `INTENTIONALLY_UNSCORED` table with a reason (`Mail.ReadBasic*`, `Calendars.ReadBasic`).

- **F138 · `Files.*.All` org-wide reach gets no scoping advisory or least-privilege pointer although the Selected alternative and item wizard exist** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/permissions.rs:261` · confirmed. `least_privilege_alternative_for` points only `Sites.*` and Exchange-scopable values, so `Files.ReadWrite.All` (HIGH, +10) gets only the still org-wide `Files.Read.All` pointer although `Files.SelectedOperations.Selected` and `ScopeKind::SharePointItem` exist; sharepoint-selected.md:45-48 lists this as future work. Extend the helper with a Files arm and add an advisory Rule 12 marker (e.g. `ORG_WIDE_FILES`, no remediation) so the picker hint, audit recommendation and item wizard agree.
- **F034 · During the tenant-wide prefetch the audit progress block reads '0 / 0 apps' with no phase label** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/audit.rs:173` · partially (the controller already seeds `progress`, so the block renders "0 / 0 apps" with a 0% bar throughout the prefetch; what is missing is a phase label, plus a view tweak for `total == 0`). The first `audit-progress` emit is at :280, after the six-way `join!` the code itself calls the longest phase of a large run. Emit one preparatory `AuditProgress` with `current_app: Some("Reading tenant-wide directory data…")` before the `join!`, and render the fraction only when `total > 0`.
- **F035 · Terminal bulk-progress events report done == total even for a cancelled or halted run; the credential sweep emits no terminal event** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/bulk.rs:1014` · partially (this is a documented and tested contract with at most a one-frame UI effect, since `BulkProgressRow` is unmounted as soon as the command returns). `run_bulk_seq`, `bulk_delete_applications` and `bulk_grant_permissions` emit `done: total` regardless of cancellation, while `bulk_remove_expired_credentials` emits no terminal event. Either harmonise to `done: outcomes.len()` and update `a_cancel_before_the_first_item_processes_nothing`, or leave as-is and only add the missing terminal event to the sweep.
- **F129 · Covered narrower permissions keep their own weight (surface-area model), pinned by test but not stated in the architecture doc** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/scoring.rs:83` · partially (the behaviour is pinned by tests.rs:1551-1565 and half-documented as "the broader permission already carries the risk weight"; only the explicit sentence is missing, and the `deduped()` analogy concerns identical pairs). `Mail.ReadWrite` plus `Mail.Read` scores 15 (High) though its reach equals `Mail.ReadWrite` alone (10, Medium). Add one sentence to the Rule 18 section of audit-findings-and-remediation.md stating a covered narrower grant keeps its Rule 1/2 weight; scoring as reach would break PowerShell parity and the characterization snapshot.
- **F133 · `AuditItem.publisher` is overloaded: publisher domain for app rows, home-tenant GUID for SP rows** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/scoring.rs:1093` · confirmed. SP rows set `publisher: sp.app_owner_organization_id.clone()` while app rows use `publisher_domain`, so the CSV `Publisher` column mixes `contoso.com` with tenant GUIDs; the overload is deliberate, pinned and export-only. Add `#[serde(default)] pub home_tenant_id: Option<String>`, leave `publisher` `None` for SP rows, add a `HomeTenantId` CSV/HTML column and extend `principal_kind_is_additive_on_the_wire`.

### Scoping (Exchange and SharePoint) and resource access

#### F094 · medium · effort M · Sub-site Selected grants made in the app cannot be revoked in the app; the list and revoke commands and bindings exist but have no UI caller
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/bindings/sharepoint.rs:223` · partially (the "never listed" half is overstated: the permission tester already answers whether THIS app reaches a given library, folder or file URL; what is missing is a per-resource view of all app grants and any in-app revoke)
**Problem.** `list_selected_item_permissions` (:204) and `remove_selected_item_permission` (:223) are implemented, registered at lib.rs:216 and typed, but a grep outside bindings, ipc_mock and demo finds no caller; only `grant_selected_item_access` (scope_wizard.rs:206) is used. AGENTS.md calls sub-site Selected scopes a second, non-enumerable mechanism, so a folder or list grant made through the wizard can be undone only from Graph Explorer or PowerShell. sharepoint-selected.md:53 anticipates a panel rather than ruling one out.
**Proposal.** Add the revoke path where the grant is verified: extend the permission tester's SharePoint result (or a "Selected item permissions" section on the SharePoint panel) with a `list_selected_item_permissions` table for the resolved URL and a per-row Revoke behind a confirm. Carry the doc's caveat that an empty result means "no grants on this resource", never "no item-level access for this app"; reuse `ItemSelectionPanel`'s URL resolution and add a GUI shard test.

#### F058 · medium · effort S · MailboxReachersResult.exchange_available can never reflect the realistic degraded mode (missing Exchange consent / 403), so the summary's partial-coverage caveat never fires
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:496` · confirmed
**Problem.** `exchange_client` only checks tenant_context and username and never touches a token (`exchange_client_checked` is the pre-acquiring variant), so the flag is always true for any signed-in operator with a UPN. A missing Exchange.Manage consent or an admin-API 403 surfaces only as dropped errors: the AAP read falls to `None` and the SP list is info-logged, silently removing the Exchange-SP-store candidate source. The frontend caveat (mailboxes.rs:54-57) and the doc sentence are therefore unreachable; the per-row unknown count is the only hint, and nothing says Exchange-only principals were not enumerated.
```
let exo = exchange_client(&state, &tenant_id).ok();
let exchange_available = exo.is_some();
```
**Proposal.** Pre-acquire best-effort with `state.ensure_exchange_token(&tenant_id).await` and set `exchange_available = false` (keeping `exo = None`) on failure. Add `exchange_sp_store_read: bool` from the `list_service_principals` outcome so the summary and CSV coverage block can say "Exchange-registered apps without an Entra grant could not be enumerated"; update the doc sentence.

- **F069 · Office 365 SharePoint Online is the documented seam: the tester ignores org-wide Sites.* grants on that resource and the converter cannot strip them** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:884` · confirmed. `sharepoint_grants_held` resolves roles via `graph_role_index` (Microsoft Graph SP only) and classifies with the name-shaped `is_sharepoint_orgwide`, so an org-wide Sites.* grant on Office 365 SharePoint Online is never seen and the app can read `no_access`. Step 1 (S): resolve both SP resources like `mailbox_resource_roles` and classify with `is_sharepoint_orgwide_permission(Some(resource), value)`, treating a tenant with no SPO SP as an empty set; step 2 (M): teach `convert_site_access_to_selected` to strip SPO grants and widen `is_scopable_sharepoint_resource_permission` with a CHANGELOG note.
- **F060 · apply_exchange_mailbox_scope's warnings name each failed strip/assignment but never summarize which permissions remain org-wide, unlike migrate_one** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/exchange/grants.rs:276` · partially (the Exchange scoping section renders every warning verbatim under a warn Callout, so only the ScopeWizard's count-only summary hides the consequence). `migrate_one` (aap_migration.rs:392-398) states "still granted organization-wide" while the sibling path emits only per-item failures. After the strip, push one consolidated warning through a helper shared with aap_migration when `remove_unscoped` is set and targets remain; optionally an additive `effective: bool` on `ExchangeAccessResult` for the wizard summary.
- **F174 · No cleanup path for an app's orphaned management scope / SP pointer / managed group after decommissioning** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/exchange/grants.rs:472` · partially (leaving them is a deliberate user-facing decision "so the scoping can be re-applied", and a later re-grant is unblocked in-app via "Move to managed group", so only the decommission-cleanup gap stands). No `Remove-ManagementScope` or `Remove-ServicePrincipal` exists anywhere. Offer an opt-in, separately confirmed teardown that proves zero role assignments reference the scope and zero assignments remain for the pointer, modelled on `delete_exchange_scope_group`'s live re-checks.
- **F263 · Exchange role-assignment list renders administrative-unit-scoped assignments as '(org-wide)'** — `crates/azapptoolkit-exchange/src/models.rs:36` · partially (AU scoping is a documented read-only limitation, effective-scope verdicts stay correct, and the cited rbac.rs docstring describes the write parameter, not the read model). `ExoRoleAssignment` carries only `CustomResourceScope` and the UI renders `unwrap_or_else(|| "(org-wide)")`, so an AU-scoped assignment is literally shown as org-wide. Add `RecipientAdministrativeUnitScope` after confirming the wire key from a live envelope, carry it through `ExchangeRoleAssignmentDto`, render "Administrative unit <id>", and keep `roles_already_scoped` keyed on `custom_resource_scope`.
- **F406 · held_permissions_panel's `is_held_scopable` is value-only, so a Sites.* held on Office 365 SharePoint Online gets a Scope… button the wizard cannot honour** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/held_permissions_panel.rs:43` · partially (the main premise is false: `row_scope_kind` returns only SharePoint kinds and the app-reg tab has no per-row mail Scope either, so the mail exclusion is parity by design; only the value-only point survives). `is_held_scopable` uses `is_sharepoint_orgwide` with no resource check, against AGENTS.md's "carry the resource, never the bare value", and the comment names a non-existent `existing_scope_kind_for`. Make it `is_held_scopable(resource, value)` using the same predicate as `row_scope_kind`, keep mail excluded, fix the comment.
- **F407 · SharePoint site section renders "Grant write" as Primary, contradicting the read-is-primary rule the audit dialog documents** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/sharepoint_sites_section.rs:204` · confirmed. Both "Grant read" and "Grant write" use `ButtonAppearance::Primary`, while scope_remediation.rs:366-377 documents read-as-Primary as the least-privilege convention shared by three surfaces. Make "Grant write" Secondary and order read last to match the dialog.
- **F436 · Permission tester: the seeded principal shows as a bare GUID in the Identity field (and fires a search for it); mailbox/site URL survive a tenant switch** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/permission_tester_view.rs:93` · confirmed. The seed Effect sets `app_query` to the raw GUID, the debounced `global_search` returns the display name via the exact-lookup path, and the results Effect discards it; the tenant-reset Effect clears identity but not `mailbox`, `site_url` or `resource_tab`. Substitute the display name when the query still equals the seeded id, and reset the extra fields or move them to `TenantScopedUi`.
- **F439 · Mailboxes panel keeps the previous mailbox's verdict table on screen while a new probe runs and after a failed probe** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/resource_access/mailboxes.rs:162` · confirmed. `do_probe` sets `probing`, clears `error` and seeds `progress` but never clears `result`, so the old verdicts sit under the progress bar and beneath any error banner for a different target; the tenant-reset Effect at :152-159 does clear it. Call `result.set(None)` when the target mailbox differs, or render the stale table `aria-busy` with a "showing results for <old>" line.
- **F197 · `KeyVaultResource` drops `properties.enableRbacAuthorization`, so the RBAC sweep cannot tell a legacy access-policy vault from one with no assignments** — `crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/models.rs:101` · confirmed. The sweep counts access-policy vaults as scanned-and-empty; the verifier adds that the UI empty state already names the limitation, and that RBAC assignments on such vaults still produce rows that confer no data-plane access, so the flag is needed to annotate rows as well. Add `properties.enable_rbac_authorization`, count `vaults_access_policy_mode` in `KeyVaultSweepResult`, show it beside `vaults_failed`, optionally a Security-tab finding; wiremock test.

### Credentials and SSO

#### F075 · medium · effort S · SAML create silently drops a failed claims policy or notification-email step; the wizard shows an unqualified success
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:264` · confirmed
**Problem.** Notification-email (mod.rs:240-244) and claims-policy (254-267) failures are log-and-continue; `SamlSsoSummary` has no `warnings` field and `components/sso_summary.rs` never reads `claims_policy_id`. An operator who filled in the Attributes & claims step sees the same success screen as one who did not and discovers the missing claims at the first federated sign-in. Degrading is documented as deliberate; its invisibility is not, and `RotateCredentialResult.warnings` is the established pattern.
```
tracing::warn!(?err, "claims-mapping policy failed; SSO app created without it");
None
```
**Proposal.** Add `#[serde(default)] pub warnings: Vec<String>` to `SamlSsoSummary`; in `configure_saml` push a human message per degraded step ("Custom claims were not applied: <err>; open the SSO tab → Save claims to retry"). Render a warn `Callout` in `sso_summary.rs` when non-empty and add a fixture with a warning so the GUI test covers it.

#### F385 · medium · effort M · Claims editor has no advisory validation for edits Graph rejects or that silently strip the default claims
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/claims_editor.rs:329` · confirmed
**Problem.** `schema_row_to_dto` drops only fully-empty rows, so attribute-only rows, transformation rows whose id matches no transform (:565) and duplicate SamlClaimType rows from repeated "Edit" clicks (:459-461) all reach `set_claims_mapping`. That command detaches the old policy (sso/mod.rs:1643-1655) before applying the new one, so a Graph rejection leaves the SP with no claims policy at all. Unchecking "Include the basic claim set" with an empty schema removes name, email, givenname and surname from every assertion with no warning. `UriListState`'s opt-in advisory validation is the precedent.
**Proposal.** Add `ClaimsEditorState::problems() -> Vec<String>` covering rows with neither claim type, unknown transformation ids, duplicate claim types, `!include_basic && schema.is_empty()` and transforms with no output claim, rendered in a `Callout tone="warn"` above Save, advisory only. Reorder `set_claims_mapping` to create the new policy, then detach, then assign, with orphan cleanup if detach fails; unit-test `problems()`.

#### F382 · medium · effort M · SSO wizard: numeric lifetimes silently fall back to server defaults on a typo, the review step omits most inputs, and three Textareas remain the documented last UriListEditor migration
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/dialogs/sso_wizard_dialog.rs:166` · confirmed
**Problem.** `cert_lifetime_days: cert_days.get_untracked().trim().parse().ok()` (and `secret_lifetime_days` at :195) turns a typo like `36o` into `None`, so the backend mints a 365-day certificate with no feedback. The review step (:325-343) shows only Protocol, Display name, Entity ID and Reply URL, omitting lifetimes, logout URL, redirect URIs, notification emails and the claims count, before creating an app that is never auto-deleted on partial failure. Three Textareas (:297-314) remain the migration frontend-workspace.md:53-55 already lists as outstanding, and the email rule is written inline here while sso_tab.rs:535 applies none. The verifier notes the Textarea part is a known TODO; the parse and review gaps are new.
**Proposal.** Parse both numbers with `parse::<u32>()` and set `error` on `Err` or out of range (1..=1095 for certificates, 1..=730 for secrets), disabling Next/Create while invalid. Add a shared `notification_email_reason` validator used through `UriListState::validated` by the wizard and the SSO tab, migrate the URI lists to `UriListEditor`, render every field in the review `<dl>`, and drop the "one remaining migration" sentence from the doc.

#### F381 · medium · effort S · Gallery create and the SSO wizard discard the created service-principal id; no post-create navigation to the SSO tab the hint promises
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/dialogs/gallery_dialog.rs:107` · confirmed
**Problem.** `on_ok` discards the `GalleryAppSummary` (which carries `service_principal_id`) and closes, while the dialog's own hint reads "Finish single sign-on on its SSO tab afterward"; the operator must find the new row in a list that may be thousands long, after `bump_enterprise_apps` refetches. Wizard step 3 offers only "Done" although `SamlSsoSummary.service_principal_id` is in hand. `session.open_enterprise_on_tab` (navigation.rs:117) already exists and is used by sso_certificates_dashboard.rs:271.
```
move |_| {
    on_created.run(());
    on_close.run(());
```
**Proposal.** In the gallery `on_ok`, toast success and call `session.open_enterprise_on_tab(s.service_principal_id, "sso")` before `on_close`. In wizard step 3 add an "Open application" primary button opening "sso" (SAML) or "overview" (OIDC) then closing; extend the gallery GUI test to assert `open_items` contains the new SP.

#### F374 · medium · effort M · Certificate reveals are a bare <pre> with no copy/save; the existing CopyBlock is private and hand-rolls the clipboard call
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/sso_tab.rs:400` · confirmed
**Problem.** The staged reveal (:399-403) and the rotate reveal (:770-772) render `<pre class="secret-reveal">` with no copy button, and the rotate one has no label or hint, although the text asks the operator to "Upload this to the application before activating." `sso_summary::CopyBlock` is the codebase primitive for label + hint + pre + Copy but is a private `fn` and re-implements `navigator.clipboard().write_text`, which `util::copy_text` claims to centralise. The verifier corrects the evidence: `save_generated_certificate_pfx` exists for app-registration certificates, but nothing saves the SAML signing public certificate.
**Proposal.** Make `CopyBlock` `pub` and use it for both reveals with a "New signing certificate (Base64)" label. Route its copy through `util::copy_text` extended with a completion callback so the "Copied" state still works, and add a backend `save_certificate_to_file(base64, suggested_name)` writing 64-column PEM via the OS save dialog as a "Save as .cer" button.

#### F373 · medium · effort S · Rotate-and-activate and Retire run on one click while every other destructive action in the pane is staged behind ConfirmDialog
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/sso_tab.rs:759` · confirmed
**Problem.** "Rotate and activate immediately" (:759-765) and Retire (:473-479) fire directly via `cmd.run_toast_err`; `grep -c ConfirmDialog sso_tab.rs` is 0, while delete SP, remove assignment, remove owner and revoke in the same pane all stage a `ConfirmDialog`. The backend doc calls rotate a big-bang that stops sign-in for any app holding a static certificate, and retire is the action that ends the rollback path. The verifier notes the cited docs concern danger styling and explicit buttons, not confirmation; the finding stands on in-pane inconsistency and outage risk.
**Proposal.** Stage `pending_rotate: RwSignal<bool>` and `pending_retire: RwSignal<Option<(thumbprint, key_id)>>` behind `ConfirmDialog` with the app name or thumbprint as subject and the outage risk in the body, considering `require_keyword="ROTATE"` for the big-bang path. Keep Remove-expired one-click and update the sso_rollover GUI tests to click through the dialog.

#### F338 · medium · effort S · 'Rotate & remove existing' deletes every client secret with no confirmation and no count
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/credentials_tab.rs:1115` · confirmed
**Problem.** The `button--danger` Primary calls `do_rotate(true)` directly, and :512-516 collects every `key_id` in `secrets`, active ones included. The dialog body says only "optionally removes the existing secret(s)" and the label carries no count, while per-row removals (:1130-1160) and the expired sweep (:1162-1177) go through `ConfirmDialog` with a named subject. Rotation warnings ("failed to remove {key_id}") reach the UI only as a count with "see the log", and no log viewer exists in the app.
**Proposal.** Label the button with the live count ("Rotate & remove {n} existing") and route `do_rotate(true)` through a `ConfirmDialog` naming the count, or require `require_keyword` when `n > 1`. Surface `r.warnings` text in the toast or an expandable Callout.

- **F085 · with_replication_retry skips steps 4 and 5 of the SAML create flow** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:221` · confirmed. Steps 2, 3 and 5b are wrapped, but `add_token_signing_certificate` (:221-223) and the activation PATCH (:226-234) against the same fresh SP are not, so a lagging replica leaves the half-configured app the retry exists to prevent. Wrap both; the closures are `FnMut() -> Fut` over borrowed data and retrying a POST on NotFound is safe because nothing was created.
- **F392 · No way to install an operator-supplied SAML signing certificate (PFX), only self-signed minting** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/sso_tab.rs:385` · confirmed. `stage_saml_signing_certificate` (self-signed via `addTokenSigningCertificate`) is the only staging path, while regulated tenants require an internal-CA certificate. Add `import_saml_signing_certificate(tenant, sp, pfx_base64, password)` parsing with the already-pinned `p12-keystore`, deriving the thumbprint via `core::thumbprint::canonical`, PATCHing Sign+Verify `keyCredentials` plus a `passwordCredentials` entry, leaving the preferred thumbprint untouched (an older-dated import reads Superseded, not Staged), zeroizing the password, and extending the trust invariant tests.
- **F190 · remove_key_credential / remove_service_principal_key_credential PATCH the unchanged array and report success when the key id is absent** — `crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/credentials.rs:198` · partially (callers do not map NotFound today, so returning an error alone would turn a concurrently-removed certificate into a hard remediation failure; the caller change is required). Both removers filter `live_key_credentials` and PATCH whatever remains, so `remediate_remove_expired_credentials` counts a removal that did not happen and busts caches. Return `GraphError::NotFound` without the PATCH, treat NotFound from either remover as already-removed in the remediation, let it propagate in `remove_certificate_credential` and the SSO retire path; wiremock test asserting no PATCH.
- **F079 · Key Vault rotation writes secret versions with no ownership tags and no collision check** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/keyvault.rs:120` · partially (the hazard needs an operator to type another app's secret name, tagging alone is cheap and safe, the KV client has no versions call and `RotateCredentialInput` has no `force`). `SecretSetRequest` is sent with `tags: None, content_type: None`, so a vault reader cannot tell which app or `keyId` a version was minted for. Populate `azapptoolkit-app-id`, `azapptoolkit-object-id` and `azapptoolkit-key-id` tags via a unit-tested builder, add a metadata-only `list_secret_versions(name)` and return `secret_owned_by_other_app` on a tag mismatch.
- **F196 · `SecretItem`/`KvSecretItemDto` drop Key Vault's `managed` attribute, so certificate-backed secrets are indistinguishable in the secret browser** — `crates/azapptoolkit-keyvault/src/models.rs:6` · partially (there is no rotation picker to filter since the name is free text, and a managed-secret rejection is already rolled back and typed as `vault_error`). Every vault certificate materialises a `managed: true` secret that the browser lists like any other. Add `#[serde(default)] managed: Option<bool>` to `SecretItem` and `KvSecretItemDto`, map it in `kv_list_secrets`, badge "certificate-backed" in `key_vault_view.rs`, and optionally pre-check the typed rotation name or map the rejection to an actionable UiError.
- **F200 · Residue of the deliberately removed `kv_set_secret` command (DTOs, binding args, phantom `delete_secret`) and an over-granting operator role doc remain** — `crates/azapptoolkit-keyvault/src/lib.rs:9` · partially (CHANGELOG-archive.md:1883-1890 records the deliberate removal, so implementing the command contradicts a documented decision; only the cleanup stands). `KvSetSecretInput`/`KvSecretMetadataDto`, a `SetArgs` binding struct hidden by `#![allow(dead_code)]`, a lib.rs doc naming a `delete_secret` that exists nowhere, and a custom role granting `deleteSecret` remain. Delete the residue, fix lib.rs:10 to list `list_secrets`, `get_secret`, `set_secret`, reword OPERATOR-ROLES.md:31 to "list/get/set (rotation)", and drop `Microsoft.KeyVault/vaults/secrets/deleteSecret/action` from azure-custom-role.json.

### App registration editing: authentication, Expose an API, federation

- **F017 · set_application_authentication validates every redirect URI but passes logout_url to Graph unchecked** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/applications/authentication.rs:311` · confirmed. The three redirect lists run through `validate_redirect_uris`, but `logout_url: Some(input.logout_url.unwrap_or_default())` goes straight to Graph, so a wildcard or `javascript:` value surfaces as a raw 400 instead of the typed `invalid_redirect_uri`. Validate a non-empty logout URL with a variant that additionally requires https, since `validate_redirect_uri` admits custom schemes; add one test.
- **F158 · redirect validator accepts `http://[::1]` and unbounded length, both of which Entra rejects** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/redirect.rs:355` · confirmed (the line citations were wrong, the loopback set is at redirect.rs:46 and the `[::1]` assertion at :73/:115; the substance is exact). Microsoft states the IPv6 loopback is not supported and each redirect URI is capped at 256 characters, so the inline row hint misses both and the three-platform PATCH fails with a generic 400. Drop `"::1"` with a comment citing Microsoft, reject URIs over 256 characters, update the pinned tests; the validator also serves restore, SSO and authentication paths.
- **F162 · federation: audiences accepts more than one value although the code comment and Graph say exactly one** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/federation.rs:51` · confirmed. The comment says "Entra accepts exactly one value" but the check is `audiences.is_empty()`; the UI always sends one, so only the untrusted restore manifest can carry more and is caught by Graph after the app shell exists. Change the check to `audiences.len() != 1` and add a two-audience test beside `rejects_missing_values`.
- **F342 · Authentication form has no Reset or dirty state; Overview Save round-trips an empty PATCH (and busts list caches) when nothing changed** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/authentication_tab.rs:184` · partially (the Overview edit does have a Cancel, and the pane-level Refresh is an existing if heavy way back, so "no Cancel" applies to the Authentication tab only). The Authentication tab renders only Save with no dirty indication, and Overview computes its diff inside the async closure, so a clean form still dispatches `{}` and `update_application` then calls `invalidate_app_lists`. Derive a `dirty` signal for each form, disable Save when clean, add a Reset that rebuilds the `UriListState`s, and short-circuit an empty Overview patch with a toast.
- **F350 · Pre-authorized client add is a GUID-only field with no directory lookup, and existing rows show the bare GUID with no display name** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/expose_api_tab.rs:289` · partially (the backend rejects a non-GUID with `invalid_client_app_id` before Graph, so only a well-formed but wrong GUID reaches Graph or silently pre-authorizes the wrong app). The add path is a free-text `<Input>` (:741-746) and rows render `<td class="mono">{p.app_id}</td>` (:567) with no name. Add a typeahead over the tenant's app registrations and service principals, and resolve display names for existing rows from the SP index with a GUID fallback.

### Enterprise applications and managed identities

- **F377 · Access tab renders every assignment unwindowed with no filter, and the DTO drops principal_id so the search cannot exclude already-assigned principals** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/access.rs:137` · partially (`DataTable` is documented as intentionally uncapped pending a real-tenant stall, and Graph allows one principal several roles, so exclusion must key on the (principal, selected role) pair). `AppAssignmentDto` lacks `principal_id` though `AppRoleAssignment.principal_id` exists, so `DirectorySearch`'s `exclude` prop goes unused and a duplicate assignment fails with a generic Graph error. Add `principal_id`, derive `exclude` from assignments holding the selected role, add a client-side filter Input, and revisit `ShowMore` only with evidence of a stall.
- **F378 · Access tab role picker offers app roles whose allowedMemberTypes exclude users/groups** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/access.rs:212` · confirmed. `role_options` filters only on `is_enabled`, so Application-only roles are offered and Graph rejects the assignment with an opaque 400 after the confirm; app_roles.rs:30-38 already labels the member types. Filter to roles whose `allowed_member_types` contains "User", render the others as `<option disabled>` "(applications only)", unit-test the predicate.
- **F391 · Provisioning tab tells the operator to grant Synchronization.Read.All but offers no consent button because no capability entry exists for it** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_detail_pane/panels.rs:282` · partially (the Callout is at panels.rs:39-46, and the "sync" consent feature plus the sync `ScopedTokenAdapter` already exist, so `request_scope_consent(.., "sync")` works today; missing are an `ensure_sync_token` pre-acquisition, a catalog row and the button). Every other consent-gated surface in the pane turns `consent_required` into a one-click grant; Provisioning names the scope and stops. Add `ensure_sync_token` mirroring `ensure_group_member_token` and call it in `get_enterprise_app_provisioning`, add a `synchronization_read` capability, and give the Callout a "Grant consent & retry" button that bumps `reload`. See F163 and F024.
- **F389 · MI Azure RBAC assign form: eight hardcoded roles only, unvalidated free-text scope, and a consent button labelled the same as the grant wizard** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/managed_identity_detail_pane.rs:527` · partially (the backend already validates the scope shape via `subscription_from_scope` and returns `invalid_scope` before any ARM call, so only typos inside a valid subscription prefix reach ARM). `COMMON_AZURE_ROLES` offers eight roles with no custom role-definition entry, and the ARM consent button reuses the wizard's "Grant access" label in the same pane. Add a "Custom role definition id…" option validated as a GUID and relabel the consent button "Grant consent to Azure"; client-side scope validation is optional polish mirroring the backend check.
- **F206 · ARM 409 `RoleAssignmentExists` on `create_role_assignment` reaches the operator as a raw `arm error (409): {json}`** — `crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/transport.rs:87` · confirmed. Every non-429 4xx maps to `ArmError::Api { status, body }` and `assign_managed_identity_azure_role` specialises only `forbidden`, so the most common slip (re-assigning a held role) shows a JSON blob under `arm_error`. Match `status: 409` with `RoleAssignmentExists` in the body and return `UiError::validation("already_assigned", …)` or treat it as success and refresh; unit-test the mapping and optionally parse `error.code` for all 4xx.

### Operator tooling: search, DR, settings, readiness

#### F040 · medium · effort S · Enterprise-app assignee/group read failures degrade to empty with no `skipped` record; the manifest silently under-reports access
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/backup.rs:555` · confirmed
**Problem.** A per-SP failure of `appRoleAssignedTo` or `memberOf` produces an `EnterpriseAppBackup` with zero assignees and groups and no `SkippedObject`; only the whole-SP failure at :566-576 is recorded. The session latch is honoured via `note_graph_failures`, so the gap is limited to transient per-item failures, but on restore such an app gets no assignments re-applied and the report shows nothing amiss. The `skipped` doc (dto/backup.rs:59-66) calls a DR backup that silently drops what it could not read the most dangerous artifact the app produces.
```
let assigned_i = match &assigned[i] {
    Ok(v) => v.clone(),
    Err(_) => Vec::new(),
```
**Proposal.** When `assigned[i]` or `groups[i]` is `Err(e)`, push `SkippedObject::new("enterpriseAppAssignments" / "enterpriseAppGroups", &sp.id, Some(sp.display_name.clone()), format!("... could not be read: {e}"))` alongside the partial entry. Update the `SkippedObject.kind` doc list and the backup-and-restore.md sentence; the DR view already renders `skipped` generically.

#### F042 · medium · effort M · `plan_restore` dry-run skips the schema check and omits enterprise/MI/skipped counts the restore will act on
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/restore.rs:87` · confirmed · also reported as F428 (`restore.rs:87`, low, partially: the schema check fires before any write, so the cost is a wasted confirm click and a late error, UX not safety)
**Problem.** `plan_restore` calls only `build_restore_plan`; `check_manifest_schema` runs solely inside `restore_tenant` (:155), so a too-new manifest passes the dry-run and is refused only after Confirm. `RestorePlan` (dto/backup.rs:351-368) lists app-registration work only, although Passes 4-5 re-apply SP settings, role assignments, group memberships and MI role re-binds that the later report has whole sections for. The only tenant note is `<Show when=move || plan.tenant_changed>` (dr.rs:444), so restoring into the source tenant, which duplicates the entire estate, shows no note at all.
**Proposal.** Call `check_manifest_schema` in `plan_restore` and surface it as a blocker like `cloud_mismatch` (generalise `cloud_blocked()` to `plan_blocked()` in dr.rs). Extend `RestorePlan` with `serde(default)` fields `enterprise_apps_to_reapply`, `enterprise_apps_manual`, `managed_identities_to_rebind` and `skipped_in_backup`, render them in `RestorePlanView`, and warn explicitly when `!tenant_changed` ("restoring will create duplicates of every app"); extend the pure unit test at restore.rs:1049.

#### F258 · medium · effort L · Readiness checklist cannot recognise the custom Entra role the repo itself recommends
`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/capabilities.rs:428` · confirmed
**Problem.** `matched_directory_role` (:428-451) matches only catalog `roleTemplateId`s (the TID_* built-ins) or catalog display names, and `/me/transitiveMemberOf/microsoft.graph.directoryRole` is the only read. An operator holding the custom Entra role OPERATOR-ROLES.md:19 recommends ("two custom roles + one built-in role", shipped as `entra-custom-role.ps1`) therefore reads "Role missing" on every DirectoryRole-detected capability, and neither the doc nor readiness.rs warns of it. Learn confirms custom template ids are tenant-defined and recommends the unified RBAC API over `directoryRoles`.
**Proposal.** Add an optional `required_actions: &[&str]` per capability (e.g. `microsoft.directory/applications/credentials/update`) and a second detection path: either `roleManagement/directory/roleAssignments?$filter=principalId eq '{me}'&$expand=roleDefinition`, or, avoiding a new scope, resolve unknown template ids through `roleManagement/directory/roleDefinitions/{id}` and compare `allowedResourceActions`, reporting "have (custom role X)". Surface app-scoped role assignments next to Owners as a separate item, and document the limitation in OPERATOR-ROLES.md meanwhile. Related: F163, F024, F262.

- **F009 · GUID search swallows probe failures, so a throttled or dead-session lookup renders as "No matches"** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/search.rs:272` · confirmed. All four probes are collapsed with `.ok()`, so a 429 past the retry budget or a 403 is indistinguishable from a genuine miss, and `corpus_truncated: false` is hardcoded at :289, against the module doc's "never silently omit" contract. Distinguish `GraphError::NotFound`, add `#[serde(default)] pub lookup_degraded: bool` to `GlobalSearchResults` rendered through the existing `cap_notice` slot, and unit-test `assemble_guid_hits` with an `Err(Throttled)` input.
- **F012 · Search corpus reports SP-index truncation but discards the app-registration index's own truncation** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/search.rs:198` · confirmed. `sp_index_truncated: sps.len() >= SP_INDEX_MAX` ignores the app index's `APPS_MAX` cap, whose `_truncated` is dropped in `list_application_index_named`, so a registration-heavy tenant sees "No matches" for an existing app; caching-and-search.md says both caps "must not drift". Compute `sps.len() >= SP_INDEX_MAX || apps.len() >= APPS_MAX`, or carry the bool through `app_name_index_cached`.
- **F049 · CSV exports are written without a UTF-8 BOM; non-ASCII display names open garbled in Excel on Windows** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/export.rs:141` · confirmed. `write_via_dialog` writes `content.into_bytes()` raw and is the single sink for every CSV, although the formula-injection guard names Excel as the consumer, so `Zürich Finanz` renders as mojibake. Prefix `\u{FEFF}` in one `csv_bytes` helper used by both CSV arms, test the first three bytes and that `csv_columns` on the header is unaffected; note base R needs `fileEncoding="UTF-8-BOM"`.
- **F053 · AuthConfigStatus lacks the resolution source; the Tenant-connection hint is generic rather than reflecting whether an env/baked override is active** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/config.rs:18` · partially (settings_view.rs:335-339 already warns that an env var can override; what is missing is saying one is active, and the baked tier is not mentioned at all). `resolve` lets an env var win over settings.json and `AuthConfigStatus` has no source field, so an in-app reconfigure silently reverts after restart. Add `ConfigSource { Env, Settings, Baked, Unset }` fields (serde default) and a one-line note when Env or Baked, and emit `cargo:warning=` from build.rs when a `.env` value is empty or not a GUID.
- **F271 · Readiness reports a PIM-eligible role as 'Missing' with no way to tell it from 'not assigned', and no activation link** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/readiness.rs:147` · partially (no portal deep-link pattern exists and `CloudEnvironment` has no portal host helper, so the link is not an S; the eligibility probe needs a new consent scope). 'Missing' conflates eligible-but-inactive (activate in minutes) with unassigned (request in days), and "Activate one of" is shown even when the operator cannot. Add a sovereign-aware `portal_root()` PIM activation link, and a `pim` optional scope feature reading `roleEligibilityScheduleInstances/filterByCurrentUser` for a `Verdict::Eligible` state that degrades to today's behaviour when unconsented.
- **F024 · Claims-mapping (Policy.ReadWrite.ApplicationConfiguration) and provisioning read (Synchronization.Read.All) have no capabilities-catalog entry, so readiness never lists them and SSO 403s carry no role remediation** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/readiness.rs:278` · partially (the consent half is already surfaced because both SSO paths pre-acquire via `ensure_policy_write_token`; the gap is the readiness row and the 403 role remediation). `consent_scopes_for` accepts `policy_write` and `sync`, yet no `Capability` carries either `scope_feature` and sso/mod.rs has no `forbidden_remediation` splice. Add `sso_claims_mapping` and `provisioning_read` entries, splice `forbidden_remediation` into the two `ensure_policy_write_token` paths, and test that the `consent_scopes_for` keys equal the catalog `scope_feature`s. Same gap as F163.
- **F163 · Consent features 'sync' and 'policy_write' exist but have no Capability, so the readiness checklist and 403 hints cannot cover the SCIM tab or custom-claims writes** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/capabilities.rs:125` · confirmed (the verifier notes readiness derives its feature set from CAPABILITIES, so the rows are simply absent and adding them suffices). No entry has `scope_feature: Some("sync")` or `Some("policy_write")` although both are real consent features with token adapters and UI consent requests. Add `provisioning_read` and `claims_policy_write` rows (checking the role lists against Learn's least-privileged tables), splice `forbidden_remediation(.., "claims_policy_write")` into the SSO claims step, and update OPERATOR-ROLES.md.
- **F262 · `admin_consent` capability omits Application Administrator / Cloud Application Administrator, which Learn lists as supported for non-Graph app-role grants** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/capabilities.rs:181` · confirmed. The row lists only Privileged Role Administrator and Global Administrator, so the readiness checklist and the Declared-permissions header label over-report "Missing" for a Cloud Application Administrator who can legitimately grant SharePoint, Exchange or tenant-API app roles. Add the two roles and reword the remediation to scope the PRA/GA requirement to Microsoft Graph and AAD Graph app roles; optionally a resource-aware hint in the consent command.
- **F157 · Capability scope strings hardcode commercial-cloud hosts; the readiness checklist prints them verbatim in sovereign builds** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/capabilities.rs:322` · confirmed. `https://vault.azure.net/.default` and three siblings are printed by readiness as "Not consented: …" while the probe uses `self.auth.cloud().*_resource()`, so a GCC High operator reads the wrong audience. Keep strings but with `{keyvault}`/`{arm}`/`{exchange}`/`{loganalytics}` placeholders expanded in `scope_detail_text`, and test that no literal `https://` host remains in `CAPABILITIES[*].scopes`.
- **F153 · AZAPPTOOLKIT_CLOUD cannot be baked like the client/tenant ids, so a sovereign-cloud team build still needs a per-workstation env var** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/cloud.rs:67` · partially (a minority deployment with a documented env-var workaround, and `cargo:rustc-env` from src-tauri's build.rs is visible only to src-tauri, so the fallback must live in state.rs and be injected into `EntraAuthService::new`). build.rs bakes only CLIENT_ID and TENANT_ID, contradicting README's "no per-workstation configuration" promise for GCC High, DoD and 21Vianet teams. Bake `AZAPPTOOLKIT_BUILD_CLOUD`, read it beside `BUILD_CLIENT_ID` and pass the resolved cloud into the auth service; document in README, DEVELOPMENT.md and `.env.example`.
- **F149 · mutate's process-wide lock does not cover a second app instance; std's File::lock would close the gap without a dependency** — `crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/settings.rs:116` · confirmed. `SETTINGS_LOCK` is a static Mutex and no single-instance plugin is registered, so two instances can interleave read-modify-write and one silently drops the other's field (e.g. a vault binding). Open `<config_dir>/settings.lock` and call `File::lock` (stable since 1.89, MSRV 1.98) inside the existing mutex for the read-modify-write scope; it composes with the atomic rename.
- **F369 · Saved views snapshot only facet + search; the drawer's date range is neither saved nor cleared on apply** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/saved_views.rs:12` · confirmed. `SavedView { name, facet, search }` ignores the created-on range hosted in the same drawer, so "Disabled, created this quarter" cannot be saved and applying a view leaves the current range active with fewer rows and no visible reason. Extend `SavedView` with `#[serde(default)] after/before: Option<NaiveDate>`, thread the two signals from `ListScaffold`, and set them (including to `None`) on apply.
- **F390 · Creation-date range filter on the Enterprise (and App Registrations) list is local state and survives a tenant switch** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/enterprise_application_list.rs:54` · confirmed. `created_after`/`created_before` are view-local signals and `keep_alive` keeps the mounted view across `set_active_tenant`, so tenant A's range filters tenant B's list behind a collapsed drawer. Add both pairs to `TenantScopedUi` with `reset()` lines and the pinning-test assertion, or document the exception beside the credential-facet one.
- **F432 · Cache diagnostics offers per-kind Clear for only Lists/All although the DTO and backend support all four kinds; the toggle button does not say what it will do** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/dialogs/cache_diagnostics_dialog.rs:197` · confirmed. `CacheKindDto` and `clear_cache` support ServicePrincipal, Permissions, Audit, Lists and All, but the dialog offers only "Clear lists" and "Clear all", so dropping a stale audit run means rebuilding the tenant-wide indexes; "Toggle enabled" forces reading the table first. Render a Clear per kind row, keep Clear all, make the label reactive ("Disable cache"/"Enable cache"); the demo fixtures already exist.
- **F434 · Home dashboard's 'With secrets'/'With certs' are inert metrics with no matching App Registrations facet to drill into** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/home_dashboard.rs:123` · partially (inert and linked metrics are visually distinct at rest, and state/mod.rs:129-131 documents the absence as deliberate). Every other number drills, and the credential facets partition by status, not kind, so "apps holding a secret" has no destination before a secret-to-certificate migration. Add `secrets`/`certs` facets, an `open_apps_with_facet` mirroring `open_enterprise_with_facet`, switch the two metrics to `metric_link`, and update the comment.

### Auth, sign-in and network resilience

#### F110 · medium · effort S · `consent_required` is flattened to `token_error` inside every client call, so the shared UI consent fallback never fires for scoped calls; docs still blame a `String` boundary that no longer exists
`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/http_error.rs:129` · confirmed
**Problem.** `passthrough_code` returns only `REAUTH_FATAL_CODES`, so the `TokenError { code: "consent_required" }` that `token_adapter::token_error` now carries across the boundary collapses to `token_error` in every `ui_code()`, and `Session::report_consent_required` (keyed on `e.code == "consent_required"`) cannot fire for a scoped Graph, SharePoint or policy call. The verifier adds that no `ensure_write_token` exists, so the documented default `consent_feature = "write"` fallback cannot fire for a first Graph write in an un-consented tenant. auth-and-consent.md:82-83, `.claude/rules/auth.md` and several state.rs comments still attribute the loss to a `String` boundary that `TokenError{code}` replaced. Passing the code through is safe: it is non-retryable, asserted non-fatal and treated per-item in bulk.
```
$name::Token(t) => {
    $crate::reauth::passthrough_code(&t.code).unwrap_or("token_error")
}
```
**Proposal.** Introduce `core::reauth::PASSTHROUGH_CODES = REAUTH_FATAL_CODES ∪ {"consent_required"}` so every client `ui_code()` emits `consent_required`, keeping `is_reauth_fatal` separate. Rewrite the doc paragraph, the rule file and the state.rs comments to say the `ensure_*` probes exist to surface consent before side-effecting work and to bind a `consent_feature`; add a dto test on `UiError::from(GraphError::Token(..)).code`.

#### F112 · medium · effort M · On Linux without a Secret Service the sign-in hard-fails after the browser round trip; the requirement is undocumented and the `keyring` hint is misleading
`crates/azapptoolkit-auth/src/token_cache.rs:65` · partially (the in-memory refresh-token fallback the finding presented as the fix is a product and security decision for the team; documentation plus a precise hint is the uncontroversial part)
**Problem.** On Linux the only store is `zbus_secret_service_keyring_store`; `ensure_keyring_store` memoises a registration failure forever (:26-42), and `store_token_outcome` (service/mod.rs:455-459) fails after a successful code exchange, discarding a good access token. On a box without a session bus or gnome-keyring/kwallet (SSH with X forwarding, WSLg, minimal WMs, containers) the hint "unlock your keychain/credential manager, then retry" cannot be followed and every retry repeats the browser round trip. No doc states Secret Service as a hard requirement; README:326 still says libsecret.
**Proposal.** Document the Linux Secret Service (zbus) requirement in README and DEVELOPMENT.md, and make `ensure_keyring_store` failures distinguishable from a locked store so sign_in.rs:156-158 can say "no credential store available". Treat a session-only in-memory mode as a team decision (access tokens are already memory-only), surfacing a `keyring_unavailable` notice if adopted.

#### F181 · medium · effort S · Scoped writes (group membership, site/list/item grants, claims policies) bypass the retry loop entirely, so a 429 with Retry-After fails immediately where the identical unscoped write would be replayed
`crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/transport.rs:120` · confirmed
**Problem.** `scoped_send_core` does a single `req.send()` and maps the status; its rationale ("one-shot configuration writes, not high-volume reads", :78-82) predates the loops that now call it: `add_group_member` per restored SP (restore.rs:709-716) and `grant_list_permission`/`grant_list_item_permission` inside the multi-target grant loop (sharepoint.rs:566-600). `core::http_retry` documents `Throttled` as safe to replay for any verb, and `send_core_url_with` already takes an explicit provider and class, so the scoped writes bypass the one retry policy AGENTS.md mandates and never notify the throttle observer. Only the scoped GET is pinned one-shot.
**Proposal.** Reimplement `scoped_send_core` on `send_core_url_with(token, retry_class_for(&method), method, url, ..)`, deriving the class from the verb so scoped DELETEs stay idempotent-replayable and POST/PATCH replay only after a 429. Keep `scoped_get` one-shot, update the doc comments at :78-82, and add two wiremock tests (429 with Retry-After then 201 succeeds; a 502 on a scoped POST hits `.expect(1)`).

#### F183 · medium · effort S · Inner $batch 5xx sub-responses are terminal while the same GET alone is retried; one transient 503 marks the whole site sweep incomplete and strands a DR backup object
`crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/batch.rs:281` · partially (Microsoft's batching guidance speaks only to retrying 429 sub-requests, and the "audit prewarm cold lean-SP entry" example is stale; the real consumers are the DR backup, where the error becomes a recorded `SkippedObject`, and the site sweep, where a sub-error makes the whole sweep incomplete and uncached)
**Problem.** The re-batch predicate is `sub.status == 429 && budget.may_retry()`, and `map_batch_response` maps any 5xx straight to `GraphError::Server`, while the single-request transport retries every non-4xx. The same object read therefore has two retry policies depending on whether it travelled batched or alone; one transient 503 sub-response turns a 5000-site sweep into an incomplete, uncached result and a backup object into a skipped one until re-run. Both paths degrade honestly, so this is an enhancement rather than a correctness bug.
**Proposal.** Extend the predicate to `matches!(sub.status, 429 | 502 | 503 | 504) && budget.may_retry()`, notifying the throttle observer for 429 only; `budget.wait(max_retry_after)` already falls back to jittered exponential backoff. Add a wiremock test mirroring `batch_get_json_retries_inner_429_then_surfaces_throttled` with an inner 503 that succeeds on the second POST, and one where an exhausted budget still surfaces `Server`.

#### F427 · medium · effort S · Dead-session recovery convention (`report_if_session_dead`) is applied in only 2 of the 9 error surfaces in this slice
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/permission_tester_view.rs:231` · confirmed
**Problem.** The sink's own doc (state/errors.rs:45-48) says surfaces with an inline banner call it first, yet grep finds calls only in dr.rs (6) and bulk_action_bar.rs (1). permission_tester_view.rs:231-235, resource_access sites.rs, keyvault.rs and mailboxes.rs, and key_vault_view.rs render `Err(e) => error.set(Some(e.message))`, and global_search.rs maps the error to a `String` inside its `LocalResource`, re-rendering "Search failed: …" on every settled keystroke. A `refresh_missing`/`not_signed_in` failure therefore shows a raw message with no Re-authenticate action. The verifier widens the gap: `use_command::run` calls only `report_consent_required`.
**Proposal.** At each listed `Err(e)` arm call `session.report_if_session_dead(&e)` first and fall through to the banner only when it returns false. Carry the `UiError` in the search resource, render a single "Session expired, re-authenticate" row and report once per failure (guard on `is_reauth_fatal()` plus a `reported` flag); consider a repo_invariants scan that any `Err(e) => error.set(Some(e.message))` in web-rs views is preceded by the sink call.

- **F114 · Browser launch failure is only logged; the authorize URL is never surfaced, so the operator waits out the full 300 s with no way to proceed** — `crates/azapptoolkit-auth/src/service/mod.rs:263` · confirmed. `open_system_browser` failure is warned ("user must open URL manually") and the flow waits for the timeout, but the URL is stripped from the log and never returned, so on a snap-confined `xdg-open` or a blocked handler there is nothing to open. Pass an `on_open_failed` callback (or return `AuthError::BrowserLaunch(url)` while the listener stays alive) and forward it via a Tauri event to a "Couldn't open your browser, copy this link" button; the URL is single-use and PKCE-bound.
- **F113 · `AuthError::Cancelled` is never constructed; the two real cancel paths (300 s timeout, `access_denied`) surface as `loopback`/`authorization` with misleading hints** — `crates/azapptoolkit-auth/src/service/mod.rs:273` · confirmed (the `cancelled` code the finding cites lives in audit.rs, not a `dr.rs`). Closing the tab yields `Loopback("timed out…")` and "Check your network", while Cancel on the consent screen yields `Authorization("access_denied")` and "An administrator may need to grant consent"; the frontend keeps a `cancelled` hint nothing can reach. Map the timeout and a code-less `access_denied` to `Cancelled` (or a dedicated Timeout code), update the `From<AuthError> for UiError` table, or remove the variant and the dead hint together.
- **F354 · Sign-in recovery hints point at 'Settings → Tenant connection', which the file itself says is unreachable before sign-in** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/sign_in.rs:191` · confirmed. The 90002/900023 and 700016/700054 hints send the operator to Settings, which the same file's `on_reconfigure` doc says is behind the sign-in that cannot complete; the card already renders "Signing in to {tenant} · Change". Reword both hints to point at Change, assert in `an_aadsts_code_beats_the_generic_code_hint` that no hint mentions Settings, and consider a `where_to_fix` parameter for post-sign-in reuse.
- **F018 · Per-item loops (remove_expired_passwords, set_application_owners, initial owners) continue after a re-auth-fatal error and drop the UiError code** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/applications/credentials.rs:293` · confirmed. `KeyFailure`/`OwnerChangeFailure` carry `err.to_string()` only, so the frontend cannot recognise a dead session and trigger in-place `reauthenticate`, and the operator sees N identical failure strings; these loops are one app's items, so cancellation is not needed. Convert with `UiError::from(err)`, carry `code` as an additive `#[serde(default)]` field, and `break` on a re-auth-fatal code, flagging the result.

### Frontend consistency and polish

#### F341 · medium · effort S · Tab-level load failures render red text with no Retry instead of the DetailLoadError primitive
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/expose_api_tab.rs:81` · confirmed
**Problem.** The hand-rolled branch below appears verbatim in authentication_tab.rs:66-69, federated_tab.rs:557-559 and permission_picker.rs:243-248, and the verifier adds sharepoint_sites_section.rs:319, enterprise app_roles.rs:263 and permissions.rs:222. A transient 429 on `get_expose_api` or `list_federated_credentials` leaves a dead red line whose only recovery is the pane-level Refresh, while the Activity and Conditional Access tabs already use `DetailLoadError` with `on_retry`. frontend-workspace.md and `.claude/rules/frontend.md` name `DetailLoadError` the single load-failure primitive, so this is drift from a documented invariant.
```
Err(e) => {
    view! { <Body1 class="form-error">{e.message}</Body1> }.into_any()
}
```
**Proposal.** Replace each branch with `<DetailLoadError error=e on_retry=Callback::new(move |_| reload.update(|n| *n += 1)) />` (the picker can re-set `resource_app_id` to retrigger its resource). No GUI test references these strings, so the strings-check hook stays quiet.
