Introduce `components::ui::FormError` (`<p class="form-error" role="alert">`) and migrate the `Body1 class="form-error"` sites starting with ConfirmDialog and the dialogs; give the Copied span `role="status"` (the GUI test queries only the class, so it is safe); document that `tone="danger"` Callouts default to `role=alert`.

#### F313 · medium · effort S · Date-range filter labels are not associated with their <input type="date">, so the two date pickers have no accessible name
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/date_range_filter.rs:63` · confirmed, also reported as F362
**Problem.** The `<label class="date-range-field__label">` is a sibling of the `<input type="date">` with no `for`/`id` and does not wrap it, so "Created before" and "Created after" are announced as two unnamed date fields on both list views' filter drawers. Every other native input in the tree (26 checked) is wrapped in a label or carries `aria-label`. F362 additionally flags the `saved_views.rs:113-115` name input, which relies on its placeholder alone (see F328 for the fix).
**Proposal.** Give the input `aria-label=label` (the true one-liner; wrapping in the `<label>` would change the `.date-range-field` block structure), add `aria-label="View name"` to the saved-views input, and add a GUI assertion in `tests/gui/application_list.rs` that the date input's accessible name is "Created before".

#### F324 · medium · effort M · Repeated per-row Remove/Revoke/Delete controls share one accessible name; thaw Buttons never receive a row-specific aria-label
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/tabs/permissions_tab.rs:923` · confirmed
**Problem.** Every Application-permission Revoke button carries the static `aria_label="Revoke application permission"` although `revoke_subject` is already in scope at :919, and `expose_api_tab.rs:389-398` renders per-row thaw `<Button>`s whose only name is "Remove"; the same bare label recurs in owners, federated, sso, settings, claims, app-roles and access views, and `attr:aria-label` appears nowhere in src. The dock already diagnosed this failure (`open_items_dock.rs:112-116`, "six identical Closes") and fixed it with a formatted label.
**Proposal.** Thread the row identity into `aria_label` for IconButton rows (`format!("Revoke {value} on {resource}")`). For thaw Buttons use `attr:aria-label=format!("Remove {uri}")` (the root is a `<button>`) or swap to the project's `IconButton(Trash)`, starting with permissions, expose-api, credentials and the enterprise access/owners tabs.

#### F314 · medium · effort S · Findings accordion header exposes no expanded state and reads its chevron glyph aloud
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/audit_view/findings.rs:321` · confirmed
**Problem.** The group header `<button class="finding-group__header" on:click=toggle>` has no `aria-expanded` or `aria-controls`, and its chevron is a literal "▾"/"▸" text node with no `aria-hidden`, so every header name is read with "black down-pointing small triangle" appended. The same shape recurs for the Healthy section header at :161-170, and `aria-expanded` appears nowhere under `audit_view/`. The comment at :283-289 shows the team already reasons about this header's accessible name, so this is an oversight.
**Proposal.** Add `aria-expanded=move || is_open().to_string()` and `aria-controls=<group-body-id>` to both header buttons, and either `aria-hidden="true"` on the chevron span or `Icon::ChevronDown/Right` as FilterToggle already uses. Consider a shared `DisclosureHeader` primitive with FilterToggle and `changelog_notes.rs:62`.

#### F323 · medium · effort M · Two role="menu" disclosures (account menu, Export) lack the ARIA menu keyboard contract
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/shell.rs:391` · confirmed
**Problem.** `shell.rs:391` (`role="menu"`, `menuitem` rows) and `export_menu.rs:68` promise the WAI-ARIA menu pattern, but the only wiring is an outside-mousedown listener and `use_escape`; grep for `keydown` or `focus()` in either file returns nothing and neither uses `use_focus_trap`. Focus stays on the trigger and Tab is the only way in, and the Export thaw Button exposes no `aria-haspopup`/`aria-expanded` at all. Both are used on every list view and the Security tab. F363 reports the same defect from the ExportMenu side.
**Proposal.** Either implement the pattern once via a `use_menu_keynav(container_ref)` hook (Arrow/Home/End over `[role=menuitem]`, focus first item on mount, restore to trigger on close) shared by both files, setting `attr:aria-haspopup`/`attr:aria-expanded` on the Export button; or drop `role="menu"`/`menuitem` and expose honest disclosure lists. The first is the better UX; the second is an hour.

- **F316 · Eight empty action-column <th></th> headers (DataTable bakes the gap in) and no visually-hidden utility** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/ui/data_table.rs:47` · partially, the `scope="col"` half is polish since a full `<thead>` row of `<th>` is resolved as column headers by every mainstream screen reader. `DataTable` tells callers to pass `""` for action columns and renders `<th>{h}</th>`, eight raw tables carry `<th></th>`, and `styles.css` has no `.sr-only` rule, which is why nothing in the app can be labelled invisibly. Render `<th scope="col"><span class="sr-only">Actions</span></th>` when the header is empty, add the one `.sr-only` rule, and apply the same to `audit_dashboard.rs:305` and the eight raw tables.
- **F328 · Placeholder-only inputs with no label in the claims editor (15 inputs), saved-view naming and the retired-group typed confirm** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/claims_editor.rs:583` · partially, `attr:aria-label` on a thaw `<Input>` lands on the wrapper span, so the working fix is `id` plus `<label for>` or a `<Field label>` wrapper. `claims_editor.rs` has 15 `<Input>`, 14 placeholders and 0 `<Field>`, `saved_views.rs:113-115` is a raw placeholder-only `<input>`, and `retired_scope_groups.rs:195` re-implements ConfirmDialog's typed confirm without its `<label>`. Pair each claims input with a label via `id`/`for` or `<Field>`, wrap the saved-view input in a `<label>`, and switch the retired-group delete to `<ConfirmDialog require_keyword=… subject=…>`.
- **F386 · Claims editor sub-row remove buttons are a bare "✕" glyph with no accessible name** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/claims_editor.rs:718` · confirmed. Three thaw Buttons (:712-719, :738-745, :767-774) contain only U+2715, announced as "multiplication x" or nothing, so a transformation with several inputs presents a run of identical unnamed buttons. Replace them with `<IconButton icon=IconName::Close aria_label=format!("Remove input claim {}", …)>` (the primitive requires `aria_label`) and consider naming the row-level Remove buttons by their SAML URI.
- **F349 · Permission picker checkboxes share one aria-label and clone the counts map per option** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/permission_picker.rs:474` · confirmed. Lines :421 and :474 give every option checkbox `aria-label="Select permission"` (Graph alone lists about 400), while `application_list.rs:703` already shows the descriptive pattern; `counts.get()` inside the per-option `.map` also clones the whole `HashMap` per option per render. Use `aria-label=format!("Select {}", r.value)` and read counts with `counts.with(|c| c.get(&r.app_id).copied())`.
- **F363 · ExportMenu (and the account menu) claim `role="menu"` without the menu keyboard contract** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/export_menu.rs:68` · confirmed. `export_menu.rs:67-83` renders `role="menu"`/`menuitem` with no `on:keydown` and no focus move on open; the module doc explains the plain-DOM disclosure as a WebView2 overlay workaround, not a reason to drop the keyboard contract. Same remediation as F323: drop the menu roles and keep `aria-expanded` on the trigger, or add a shared `use_menu_keynav` hook with `use_focus_return`.
- **F438 · Both comboboxes place non-option children (group labels, "Searching…", capped footers, cap Callout) directly inside `role="listbox"`** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/global_search.rs:263` · confirmed. Inside the listbox, `global_search.rs:274-289` renders the Searching/empty/error divs and :690 the "Go to" group label with no role, while the footers at :730 are correctly `role="presentation"`; `permission_tester_view.rs:326-338` has the same shape. Wrap each group in `<div role="group" aria-labelledby=…>`, move the status text into a sibling `role="status"` live region (or mark it presentational), and mirror in the tester picker.
- **F361 · ModalShell hardcodes one `id="modal-shell-title"`; stacked modals get duplicate ids and both close on one Escape** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/modal_shell.rs:51` · confirmed. Eight components mount ModalShell, `use_shortcuts.rs:163-166` toggles the shortcuts help on `?` with no dialog gate (a focused button is not "typing"), and `use_escape` registers an independent window listener per dialog, so one Escape closes both. Mint a per-instance id and either gate the bare `?` on no open `[role=dialog]` or make `use_escape` act only for the topmost `.modal-backdrop`.
- **F360 · Two 'is the user typing?' predicates disagree: `?`/`/` are dead while a row checkbox has focus** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/hooks/use_shortcuts.rs:34` · confirmed. `use_shortcuts::is_typing` treats every `<input>` as typing while `use_grid_keynav::in_text_field` correctly exempts checkbox/radio/button types, so after Space-toggling one of the 19 bulk-select checkboxes, `?` and `/` no-op; the grid hook's doc forbids importing the window module but not a shared leaf helper. Move the predicate to `hooks/mod.rs` as `is_text_entry(ev)`, use it from both hooks, and add a checkbox probe to `tests/gui/shortcuts.rs`.

---

## Second-pass findings (gap slices)

These 52 findings come from a completeness critic that named lenses the 26 first-pass slices had not applied. Six gap finders then swept those lenses: shared cancel and throttle state across long-running runs, error codes as the operator actually sees them, the launch cold path, updater and install formats per OS, the support log, and the desktop crate's inline test oracles. Every finding was verified against the source; 14 were narrowed by the verifier (marked "partially") and the two high findings were re-verified by a second pass. Counts by severity: 2 high, 28 medium, 22 low.

### Concurrency and shared cancel/throttle state (gap-cancel-flag-sharing)

#### F440 · medium · effort M · audit_cancel is shared by the read-only audit, every bulk write and the AAP migration, and the UI can run them concurrently, so any Cancel button stops all of them
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/bulk.rs:127` · confirmed
**Problem.** The docstring at bulk.rs:125-134 says the audit and bulk loops "never run at once", so one flag covers both. That premise is false: views are keep-alive, `BulkActionBar` gates only on its own `busy`, and the audit controller only on `scanning`. All ten bulk commands and the AAP migration claim `audit_cancel`, and `cancel()` stamps the current generation, so either Cancel button stops every run on the flag. The verifier lowered it from high to medium: a stopped bulk run names its unattempted remainder and a cancelled audit is never cached, so the harm is a wrong-target cancel plus a misleading coverage caveat.
```rust
pub fn cancel_bulk(state: State<'_, AppState>) {
    state.audit_cancel.cancel();
}
```
**Proposal.** Minimal: add `bulk_cancel` and `migration_cancel` flags, point `cancel_bulk` and the `bulk_*` claims at them, and pin the command-to-flag mapping in `repo_invariants/cancel.rs`. Robust: return the generation from `claim()`, emit it as `run_id` in the first progress event, and add `cancel_run(run_id)` so each Cancel stops exactly its own run. The verifier notes only the run_id variant also stops two bulk bars cross-cancelling and fixes the interleaved `bulk-progress` rows every mounted bar receives.

#### F442 · medium · effort M · sweep_cancel is shared by the site sweep, Key Vault sweep, mailbox probe and per-app site panel, all of which can run concurrently from always-mounted panels; one panel's Cancel aborts the others
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:443` · confirmed
**Problem.** The docstring claims the mailbox probe and site sweep "never run concurrently from the UI", yet resource_access/mod.rs:19-20 says all panels stay mounted with independent busy signals. The site sweep, Key Vault sweep, mailbox probe and per-app site panel all claim `sweep_cancel`, and all four Cancel handlers call `cancel_resource_sweep`. Cancelling a 20-second mailbox probe therefore aborts a multi-minute site or vault sweep, which is deliberately not cached, so the whole scan is lost with a coverage caveat the operator never aimed at it. Two site sweeps (Sites tab plus app panel) can also run at once with no single-flight, so the tenant walk is paid twice and their progress events interleave.
**Proposal.** Split into `site_sweep_cancel`, `keyvault_sweep_cancel` and `mailbox_probe_cancel` with their own commands, or adopt the run_id design from F440, and fix the docstring plus resource-access-and-permission-tester.md:14-15. Wrap `sweep_site_permissions` in `state.single_flight(&sweep_cache_key(..))` with a post-acquire cache re-check; the verifier notes single_flight is documented for read-through caches, and joining is safe here only because a cancelled first run is never cached.

#### F441 · medium · effort S · GraphClient has a single throttle_observer slot, so concurrent fan-outs on one tenant overwrite each other's tracker and ThrottleGuard::drop clears whichever run's observer is installed
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/throttle.rs:141` · confirmed
**Problem.** `GraphClient` holds one observer slot; `set_throttle_observer` overwrites it and `clear_throttle_observer` clears it unconditionally, which is what `ThrottleGuard::drop` calls. Every fan-out (audit, bulk, site sweep, DR backup) attaches to the shared per-tenant `Arc<GraphClient>`, so a second run silently replaces the first run's tracker and its 429s halve the wrong cap. When either guard drops first, it removes the other run's observer, leaving a live fan-out at a fixed cap with no back-off, the pre-adaptive behaviour the throttle was added to fix. The verifier notes the transport still honours Retry-After, so this degrades back-off rather than correctness.
```rust
throttle_observer: parking_lot::RwLock<Option<Arc<dyn ThrottleObserver>>>,
```
**Proposal.** Make the slot multi-observer (`RwLock<Vec<Weak<dyn ThrottleObserver>>>`, notify every live Weak, remove only your own tracker on drop via `Weak::ptr_eq`), or at minimum make `clear_throttle_observer` a compare-and-clear with `Arc::ptr_eq` and log a displaced observer. Add a transport test that attaches two observers, asserts both see a 429, and checks that dropping one guard leaves the other attached.

- **F445 · sign_out prunes only graph_clients and exchange_clients; kv_clients, arm_clients and la_clients are never removed anywhere** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/auth.rs:54` · partially (verifier: the "cancel all three flags on sign_out" part is wrong, since flags are process-wide and would abort another tenant's run; only the client-map asymmetry stands). state.rs:205-214 declares three per-tenant client maps that `sign_out` never touches, and the `(tenant, vault)`-keyed KV map only grows; it is not a credential leak because every client re-asks the auth singleton per request. Remove the tenant's entries from all five maps and add a repo_invariants check that every client map on `AppState` is referenced in `sign_out`.
- **F444 · AAP migration claims a cancel token 'so the operator can stop it' but no UI surface exposes a Cancel; the only things that stop it are the audit and bulk Cancel buttons** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/exchange/aap_migration.rs:52` · confirmed. Both frontend callers pass `Some(&app_id)` and wire no cancel binding, so the token's cancel half is reachable only through the audit strip's or a bulk bar's Cancel, and today it mostly serves the SessionDead latch. Give the migration its own flag plus a Cancel button in the scoping section and the MigrateLegacyScope dialog, or rewrite the comment to say cancellation exists only for the dead-session break.
- **F446 · AppState cancel-flag field docs describe the retired AtomicBool reset model and name a command that does not exist** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/state.rs:214` · confirmed. The three field docs still say "Reset to `false` at the top of every run" although `reset()` was removed, and cite `cancel_site_sweep`, which does not exist (the command is `cancel_resource_sweep`). Rewrite them to name every claiming and cancelling command and reference `CancelFlag::claim`; the verifier adds that backup-and-restore.md:126,173 and resource-access-and-permission-tester.md:18-19 carry the same stale narrative.
- **F443 · Backup and restore share dr_cancel and are gated by independent signals in the same view, so cancelling a read-only backup aborts an in-flight restore mid-pass** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/dr.rs:150` · confirmed. `run_backup` gates on `busy`, `do_restore` never checks it, and both Cancel handlers call `cancel_dr`, so cancelling a backup stops a restore between passes and leaves unwired shells; the verifier lowered it to low because the sequence is unusual and the report flags the run cancelled. Disable Restore while `busy` and Back up while `restoring`; a separate `restore_cancel` flag is optional.

### Error codes to screen and degraded network (gap-error-code-to-screen)

#### F465 · medium · effort M · `unauthorized` (client 401, including the CAE "re-auth needed" path) renders as bare "unauthorized (401)" with no action; the three ui_hints tell the operator to sign out, contradicting the in-place recovery design
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/state/errors.rs:157` · confirmed
**Problem.** A Graph 401, including a CAE claims challenge the silent re-mint could not satisfy, reaches `report_command_error_for` and falls through to a plain toast reading `unauthorized (401)`. The remedy the transport itself names ("surfaces to the caller to re-auth") already exists as the top-bar Refresh token handler, which falls back to `reauthenticate` on a dead session. Meanwhile the Key Vault, ARM and Exchange `Unauthorized` hints say "Sign out and back in", contradicting auth-and-consent.md:101 ("Force re-auth in place, never make the user sign out"). `unauthorized` is deliberately excluded from `REAUTH_FATAL_CODES` so fan-outs keep going; that rationale is about loops, not the screen.
**Proposal.** Extract the shell's refresh handler into `Session::spawn_refresh_token()`, and in the sink add a branch for `code == "unauthorized"` that toasts "Your access token was rejected" with a Refresh token action. Reword the three hints and `premium_feature_err` to "use Refresh token (next to Sign out), then retry", the wording exchange/error.rs already uses for its 403 hint, and add a GUI test beside tests/gui/reauth.rs. Do not add the code to `REAUTH_FATAL_CODES`.

#### F467 · medium · effort S · ARM, Key Vault and Exchange reads burn minutes on a firewalled host before a cause-less network error; only Graph has a connect budget
`crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/client.rs:38` · partially (verifier: Log Analytics `query` is a POST and ARM/KV writes are non-idempotent, so they get one attempt of about 2 min; the ~247 s figure holds only for ARM/KV GETs and every Exchange cmdlet)
**Problem.** The Graph client sets `.connect_timeout(10 s)` with a comment explaining that a blackholed host otherwise burns the whole 60 s ceiling once per attempt. The ARM, Log Analytics, Exchange and Key Vault builders set only `.timeout(60 s)` (120 s for Log Analytics), and the SSO metadata probe uses `unwrap_or_default()`, which falls back to a client with no timeout at all. On an egress allow-list that admits only graph.microsoft.com, an ARM or KV read retries three times for roughly four 60 s attempts plus back-off, with no `CancelToken`, before the cause-less `network` error of F463.
```rust
let http = reqwest::Client::builder()
    .user_agent(concat!("azapptoolkit/", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")))
    .timeout(Duration::from_secs(60))
```
**Proposal.** Add `.connect_timeout(Duration::from_secs(10))` to the four builders and the SSO probe, replacing `unwrap_or_default()` with the `expect` the others use. Pin it with a repo_invariants source scan asserting every `reqwest::Client::builder()` outside azapptoolkit-auth is followed by `.connect_timeout(`; the verifier notes core has no reqwest dependency, so a shared builder there would add a dependency edge.

#### F462 · medium · effort S · `throttled` error message leaks Rust `Option` Debug formatting ("retry after Some(30)s" / "retry after Nones") and never says to wait
`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/http_error.rs:76` · confirmed
**Problem.** Both the `http_error_enum!` macro and the hand-written `ExchangeError` format the arm with `{retry_after_secs:?}`, the Debug formatter for `Option<u64>`. `ui_error_from!` copies `err.to_string()` into `UiError.message`, and `DetailLoadError` and the toast render it verbatim, so after three exhausted Retry-After waits the operator reads `throttled (429); retry after Some(30)s`. No frontend site branches on `throttled`, and the two GUI fixtures use "Too many requests", a string the backend never produces, so no test can see the leak.
```rust
#[error("throttled (429); retry after {retry_after_secs:?}s")]
Throttled { retry_after_secs: Option<u64> },
```
**Proposal.** Format the arm with thiserror's extra-args form, e.g. `.retry_after_secs.map_or("a moment".to_string(), |s| format!("{s} s"))`, so no manual Display is needed for the macro or `ExchangeError`; the verifier suggested this refinement. Add an error-conformance test asserting the message contains neither "Some(" nor "None", and switch the two GUI fixtures to the real backend string.

#### F463 · medium · effort M · Network errors drop the cause: `err.to_string()` on reqwest 0.13 yields only "error sending request for url (...)", hiding DNS/timeout/TLS/proxy at all six sites and in the retry log
`crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/transport.rs:571` · partially (verifier: README has no proxy/TLS guidance to match a symptom against, and core has no reqwest dependency, so `is_timeout()`/`is_connect()` cannot live there)
**Problem.** reqwest 0.13.5's Display writes only the kind plus ` for url (...)` and never the `source()` chain. All six `Network(err.to_string())` sites (graph transport.rs:571 and 585, arm/transport.rs:60, keyvault/client.rs:196, exchange transport.rs:188 and 196) and `AuthError::Http`'s `#[error("http: {0}")]` discard the cause, so DNS failure, connect timeout, TLS interception and proxy 407 all read identically on screen. Worse, `with_retries` logs only label, attempt and retry_after, so the cause is not in the log either; only Exchange's hand loop logs `?err`.
**Proposal.** Add a dependency-free `describe_error_chain(e: &dyn std::error::Error) -> String` in core that joins Display of `e` and each `source()` (hyper already says "dns error", "connection refused", "invalid peer certificate"), use it at the six sites and in a manual Display for `AuthError::Http`, and log it in the retry warn line. Classify timeout versus connect only at the call sites that hold the concrete `reqwest::Error`. Unit-test with a refused localhost port.

- **F468 · Offline at launch shows the plain sign-in card: `restore_session` folds a `network` failure into `Ok(None)` and the silent restore cannot be retried without relaunching** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/auth.rs:39` · partially (verifier: the command doc at auth.rs:20-30 documents `Ok(None)` for every failure as deliberate, the auth client times out in 30 s, and the interactive hints already say to check the network). The offline case is the one the enumerated rationale omits: the refresh token stays valid, but the operator is told to sign in and cannot retry the silent restore without relaunching. Return `Err` only for code `network`, render a warn `Callout` with a Retry button on the sign-in screen, and budget for a wire-shape change touching the binding, demo fixtures and Root.
- **F469 · A mid-session token-refresh network failure surfaces as non-retryable `token_error`, not `network_error`: `token_adapter::token_error` flattens `AuthError::Http`** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/token_adapter.rs:26` · partially (verifier: `post_token` returns `TokenExchange` for every unrecognised non-2xx including permanent 400s, so mapping it to `server_error` would make them retryable; only the `Http` half stands). `token_error` maps `AuthError::Http` to `token_error`, which `is_retryable_code` excludes, so the same outage is retryable during the Graph call and non-retryable during the lazy refresh one line earlier. Map `AuthError::Http` to `network_error`, add a `TRANSIENT_PASSTHROUGH` slice beside `passthrough_code`, and pin adapter/dto agreement in the dto tests.
- **F464 · `UiError.retryable` drives a Retry action at only one toast site; `run_toast_err` discards the op it already holds** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/src/state/errors.rs:160` · partially (verifier: `DetailLoadError` always offers Retry by design and the sink-only contract is documented at errors.rs:70-74, so the real gap is the 16 `run_toast_err` and 9 direct `report_command_error` sites). Those sites turn a 429/5xx/network failure into a 10-second auto-dismissing toast although `run_toast_err` holds the op, typed `FnOnce`. Take `op: impl Fn + Clone` in `run_toast_err` only and offer a Retry action when `e.retryable`, or trim the "retry button" sentence from the DTO doc so the flag's contract matches its one consumer.
- **F466 · `.toast__message` lacks `white-space: pre-wrap`, so the one hint-carrying toast today (admin-consent 403 remediation) collapses its blank-line guidance** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/styles.css:3513` · partially (verifier: the Exchange scoping section, scope wizard and bulk bar all render in `.form-error`, which is already pre-wrap; only the admin-consent 403 toast at permissions_tab.rs:133 reaches this path today). `.form-error`, `.alert` and `.signin-error` honour the documented `\n\n` hint contract, but `.toast__message`, `.dr-view__error` and `.app-detail__error` do not. Add `white-space: pre-wrap` to `.toast__message` and pin it with a GUI assertion on the admin-consent failure toast.

### Launch and cold path (gap-launch-sequence-perf)

#### F470 · medium · effort M · Launch runs three concurrent full /applications paginations that one $select superset already covers
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/credentials.rs:63` · confirmed
**Problem.** Home's resources fire together on a cold tenant, so `list_applications_with_pairing` (`list_row_select`), `list_enterprise_applications` via `app_name_index_cached` (id, appId, displayName) and `list_credential_expirations` (five fields) each page the whole `/applications` collection concurrently: 3 x ceil(N/999) serial round trips, 18 for 5,000 apps. `list_row_select` is a strict superset of both other projections, and both roll-ups are busted by exactly the same two invalidation tiers, so deriving them from one scan changes no freshness contract. The pairing core seeds the SP index from its scan but never `app_name_index`, so the Enterprise card immediately re-enumerates the apps just fetched. CHANGELOG-archive.md:2991-2994 records the duplicate scan as known and only cached, not shared.
**Proposal.** In `list_applications_with_pairing`, take an `app_name_index` watch before the scan and afterwards store the lean index via `app_name_index_store_if_current` and the credential roll-up via `put_index_if_current`; make `list_credential_expirations` re-check the cache behind a single-flight gate instead of scanning. The verifier cautions to strip rows to id/appId/displayName before storing them as the typed index and to take every watch before the fetch. Expected effect: 18 to 6 round trips at launch on a 5,000-app tenant.

#### F471 · medium · effort M · Restored dock mounts every parked detail pane hidden at launch, each firing its full Graph detail fetch before Home paints
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/components/open_items_workspace.rs:138` · confirmed
**Problem.** `restore_open_items` repopulates `open_items` inside `set_active_tenant`, the keyed `<For>` mounts a window per item, and each pane's `detail` LocalResource runs on mount regardless of `shown` (the verifier places the pane at views/application_detail_pane.rs:39-51, not components/). An operator with eight parked items pays eight times four Graph waves plus permission resolution the instant the shell appears, none of it visible, competing with Home's tenant-wide scans on the same throttle. frontend-workspace.md:124-126 justifies mounting all windows for chip switches and says nothing about restore. Mitigations keep it medium: `MAX_OPEN_ITEMS=8` bounds the set and the backend caches detail for 60 min.
**Proposal.** Gate each restored window on first show using the existing `util::keep_alive` visited-latch pattern from security_view.rs:58-95, seeded from `shown_items`, so items opened interactively keep today's eager behaviour. Add a GUI test in the open_items_dock shard asserting a restored, un-shown item records no `get_application_detail` invoke via `ts::call_count`.

#### F472 · medium · effort M · Home pulls the entire cached AuditRunResult (and four other full collections) over IPC to render counts, duplicating the Security controller's copy
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/home_dashboard.rs:84` · confirmed
**Problem.** The Security Posture tile shows three counts, a timestamp and a few group counts, yet `get_cached_audit` ships every `AuditItem` (21 fields, five Vecs, up to 10,000 items) through the IPC bridge, and the audit controller holds a second copy. On the backend the run is read via the untyped `cache.get`, which walks the whole `Value` tree per call, and every `audit_reload` bump re-ships it. The four inventory cards do the same with `list_applications_with_pairing` (also refetched by the Apps list on the same bump), enterprise apps, managed identities and credential expirations.
**Proposal.** Add a `get_cached_audit_summary` command returning `{ completed_at, posture, groups }`, moving `posture_counts` into core or dto so Home and the Security strip share one count source, and store the run via `put_typed` for a refcount-clone read. The verifier cautions that `put_typed_index_if_current` pins and is restricted by PINNABLE_KEYS, that the new cache-only command must call `tenant_context` to satisfy the repo_invariants session check, and that `ranked_actionable_findings` should stay frontend-side.

- **F473 · Credential-expiry roll-up is stored with plain `put` although the `put_index` doc names it as a pinned tenant-wide index** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/credentials.rs:97` · partially (verifier: the proposed repo_invariants pin has no target because `_if_current` writes are already covered, and eviction needs over 10k per-object writes within the 60-minute TTL). core cache.rs:711-720 names the roll-up as pinned, but credentials.rs:97 uses plain `put`, unlike the three sibling list stores. Take a `generation_for` watch before `list_applications_all` and store with `put_index_if_current`, which also closes the store-after-invalidate race F006 recorded.
- **F474 · trunk build --release ships unminified CSS and JS: Trunk's `minify` defaults to `never` and nothing enables it** — `apps/desktop/web-rs/Trunk.toml:1` · confirmed. The wasm side is tuned (`wasm-opt=z`, lto, strip) but Trunk's separate `minify` defaults to `Never`, so the 120,706-byte stylesheet and the wasm-bindgen glue ship verbatim. Add `minify = "on_release"` under `[build]`, eyeball the Pages demo once, and pin the Trunk version CI installs (`tool: just,trunk` is unpinned in three workflows).
- **F475 · Shipped frontend bundle size is never measured or recorded; only the debug GUI-test shards are gated** — `justfile:149` · partially (verifier: do not wire it into `verify-full`, which builds the frontend in debug to mirror CI; the measurement belongs where release builds already happen). `web-itest-size` gates only `gui_*.wasm` shards at 52 MB, `web-build-release` prints nothing, and release.yml uploads `bundle/` blind. Add a `web-size` recipe printing raw and gzip sizes of `dist/*.{wasm,js,css}`, call it from release.yml and pages.yml into `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, and record a baseline in release-updater-demo.md.

### Updater, install formats and OS paths (gap-updater-install-format-platforms)

#### F488 · high · effort S · Linux release leg builds on floating `ubuntu-latest`, so the AppImage/.deb require glibc >= 2.38 and cannot start on Ubuntu 22.04 / Debian 12 / RHEL 9 despite 'runs on most distributions'
`.github/workflows/release.yml:105` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** The only shipped Linux leg runs on `ubuntu-latest` (24.04, glibc 2.39, and it will float again), and no step checks a glibc floor. The verifier reproduced the evidence: workspace binaries built here import strong `GLIBC_2.38` symbols (`__isoc23_sscanf`, `__isoc23_strtol`) originating in aws-lc-sys C code, which the desktop binary inherits through rustls. glibc is never bundled by the AppImage and the generated .deb `Depends` carries no versioned libc6, so Ubuntu 22.04 (2.35), Debian 12 (2.36) and RHEL 9 (2.34) install cleanly and fail at load with "version GLIBC_2.38 not found". README.md:225-229 and the release body promise "runs on most distributions", while Tauri's own docs say to build on the oldest base you support.
```yaml
- os: ubuntu-latest
  rust_target: x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu
  recipe: build-linux-updater
```
**Proposal.** Pin the Linux leg to `ubuntu-22.04` (or a `debian:bookworm` container as the durable form, since GitHub retires the 22.04 runner around April 2027) and add a post-build `objdump -T` guard asserting the highest `GLIBC_x.y` import is at most 2.35. The verifier corrects the binary name: the guard path is `target/x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu/release/desktop`, not `.../azapptoolkit`. Document the actual floor in README, the release-body row and release-updater-demo.md.

#### F487 · high · effort M · MSI and .deb installs are offered the NSIS/AppImage payload: no install-format gate in check_for_update although bundle_type() is available
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/updater.rs:28` · confirmed, re-verified
**Problem.** `check_for_update` and `perform_update` call `updater.check()` with no install-format gate, and the plugin falls back from `{os}-{arch}-{installer}` to the bare `{os}-{arch}` key that release.yml:213-215 alone publishes. An MSI or .deb install therefore always resolves to the NSIS or AppImage payload, the shell toasts "Update available" on every launch, and the documented `auto_update` opt-out has no consumer anywhere (F036/F139). On an MSI install one click runs the NSIS setup with `/P /UPDATE` and installs a second per-user copy beside the per-machine MSI, exactly what README:255-258 warns about. On .deb, `install_deb` fails `is_deb` on AppImage bytes after the full download and the raw "invalid updater binary format" lands in the splash.
**Proposal.** Read `tauri::utils::platform::bundle_type()` in both commands and short-circuit for `Msi | Deb | Rpm`, preferably via a tri-state DTO (`UpToDate` / `Available` / `ManagedExternally { format }`) so the manual check can say the install is managed by packaging tooling. Pull the decision into a pure `updatable_format(BundleType) -> bool` with a unit test; the verifier stresses that `bundle_type()` is `None` for dev builds, so the gate must fall through to "updatable", never block. Publishing `windows-x86_64-msi` and `linux-x86_64-deb` keys instead is a policy change to weigh separately.

#### F489 · medium · effort S · macOS: unsigned .app plus legacy-keychain default ACL means every auto-update triggers a keychain prompt (or a silent 'no session'); README says it 'updates in place like the Windows NSIS build'
`README.md:217` · confirmed
**Problem.** token_cache.rs:55-57 uses `apple_native_keyring_store::keychain::Store`, the legacy keychain whose items carry an ACL naming the creating application. The macOS build is unsigned and not notarized (DEVELOPMENT.md:161-164), so there is no stable designated requirement; after the updater swaps the .app, the next `find_generic_password` raises the "wants to use your confidential information" dialog, and "Deny" becomes `AuthError::Keyring`, which `restore_session` folds into `Ok(None)` with an info log. The operator lands on the sign-in card with no hint the update caused it. This is reasoned from Apple's ACL model and the vendored store code rather than observed on hardware; the verifier confirms the mechanism is standard behaviour for unsigned apps.
**Proposal.** Document in README's macOS and Updates sections that an unsigned build re-prompts for keychain access after every update (choose Always Allow) and that a post-update sign-in card is this, not a revoked session. Name Developer-ID signing, not only notarization, as the real fix in DEVELOPMENT.md. The optional warn log when the stored version differs overlaps F477/F484 and should be deduplicated there.

- **F490 · Daily debug-level logs (14 files) are written to Roaming AppData on Windows instead of LOCALAPPDATA** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:397` · confirmed. `log_directory()` hangs off `config_directory()`, which resolves `%APPDATA%` (Roaming), so two weeks of `azapptoolkit=debug` logs roam with the profile; Tauri's own `app_log_dir` uses `data_local_dir` on Windows and Linux and `~/Library/Logs` on macOS. Split only the log dir per platform (keep settings.json in Roaming), and move README Logs, the lib.rs comment and a CHANGELOG line together.
- **F491 · DEVELOPMENT.md says the NSIS installer 'asks once about per-user vs per-machine'; the config uses Tauri's default currentUser mode, so nothing asks** — `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md:180` · confirmed. tauri.conf.json sets no `nsis.installMode`, `CurrentUser` is the default and only `both` shows the chooser; README and the release body already say per-user. Rewrite the sentence to say it installs per-user with no prompt and that `currentUser` keeps the passive `/P /UPDATE` relaunch UAC-free (the verifier notes `both` would add a UAC prompt rather than break it); optionally pin `installMode` in repo_invariants/release.rs.

### Observability and the support log (gap-observability-support-log)

#### F478 · medium · effort S · Startup panics with zero diagnostics if the log file cannot be created: install_tracing `.expect()`s the appender before any subscriber or panic hook exists
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:353` · confirmed
**Problem.** `run()` calls `install_tracing()` before `install_panic_hook()`; inside, the `create_dir_all` error is discarded and the rolling appender's `build()` is unwrapped with `.expect(...)`. tracing-appender 0.2.5 returns `InitError` when the log directory or file cannot be created, so on a read-only or redirected app-data dir (or the install directory when APPDATA/HOME is unset, per the `PathBuf::from(".")` fallback at lib.rs:418) the process panics before any subscriber or hook exists. A double-clicked GUI app then "just doesn't open", the advertised log directory holds nothing, and stderr is invisible; the panic hook's own doc at lib.rs:374-378 explains why an unlogged crash is unacceptable.
```rust
.max_log_files(14)
.build(&log_dir)
.expect("failed to initialize rolling log file appender");
```
**Proposal.** Make the file appender optional: match on `build()`, `eprintln!` on `Err`, register the file layer as `Option<Layer>` via `.with(...)`, then `tracing::warn!` after init that file logging is disabled. The verifier notes installing the hook first alone would not help (no subscriber yet), so the optional appender is the load-bearing change. Consider surfacing the fact in About or the config screen.

#### F480 · medium · effort M · Webview panics and surfaced IPC errors are never persisted to the backend log; no frontend-to-backend log command exists
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/lib.rs:69` · partially (verifier: a `spawn_local` inside the panic hook cannot work because wasm32 is panic=abort and the module traps when the hook returns; the hook must issue the IPC synchronously)
**Problem.** web-rs `run()` installs only `console_error_panic_hook::set_once()`; the backend has no tauri-plugin-log, tauri.conf.json enables no devtools, and no console or log bridge exists anywhere in web-rs. F425 established that any non-UiError IPC rejection panics the WASM app (`swb::from_value(err).unwrap()` in tauri-sys), so in a release build the operator sees a frozen window and the panic text exists only in a devtools console they cannot open. The backend log, the one artifact support asks for, stays silent.
**Proposal.** Add `#[tauri::command] fn log_frontend(level, message)` (command, `generate_handler!`, `bindings/diagnostics.rs` stub, capped length, re-emitted at `tracing::warn!`/`error!` with `target: "webview"`). In `run()` install a hook that calls `console_error_panic_hook::hook(info)` and then synchronously invokes it through a `#[wasm_bindgen] extern` to `window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke`, dropping the Promise; guard with `is_tauri()` and register a no-op fixture in `demo::register_fixtures`. Have the sink's plain-toast branch also call the async binding with code and message.

#### F481 · medium · effort M · No diagnostics affordance beyond cache stats: no 'Open log folder' / 'Copy diagnostics', no bundle command; operators are told to hand-locate and hand-redact log files
`apps/desktop/web-rs/src/views/shell.rs:404` · confirmed
**Problem.** The account menu (shell.rs:396-470) offers Access Readiness, Settings, Cache diagnostics, Check for updates, Sign Out and the version; commands/diagnostics.rs exposes only cache tuning, and `log_directory()` is referenced nowhere outside `install_tracing`. bug_report.yml:46-51 asks reporters to find the right file among 14 daily logs and redact tenant and object ids by hand, while its version field points at a Help menu that does not exist off macOS (F486). The app already holds version, `cache_stats`, tenant id, cloud and log path but exposes none of them together.
**Proposal.** Add `get_diagnostics_bundle(state) -> DiagnosticsBundleDto` (version, OS/arch, cloud, tenant id, log_dir, `CacheStatsDto`, the last N UiError codes from F479's ring buffer, and the tail of today's log), plus an account-menu "Copy diagnostics" item using the existing `util::copy_text` and a read-only "Log folder" path with a copy button. The verifier asks that the bundle carry or apply the same redaction caveat the template gives. Update the bug template to "Account menu, Copy diagnostics".

#### F482 · medium · effort S · Retry/throttle log lines carry no endpoint, status or reason; a 429 storm and a 5xx outage look identical, and inner $batch 429 re-batches are silent
`crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/http_retry.rs:207` · confirmed
**Problem.** The only retry-path log line records attempt, a constant label ("graph", "key vault") and retry_after; `RetryReason` and the HTTP status are in scope but dropped, and budget exhaustion returns `Err(err)` with no line at all. batch.rs has zero `tracing::` calls, so the inner-429 re-batch loop, on "the throttle-happiest endpoint in the API" per its own comment, is invisible. A 10k-app audit under a tenant-quota 429 storm and a Graph 503 incident therefore produce identical files, with no way to tell which fan-out is throttled.
**Proposal.** Log `reason = ?reason` and a `status: Option<u16>` carried on `Attempt::Retry`; the verifier prefers this over `%err`, whose Display for `Server`/`Forbidden` embeds the response body that DEVELOPMENT.md:348-351 says never to log. Pass a label naming method and `Url::path()` with the query stripped, add an `info!` in `batch_chunk` for re-batched sub-requests, and `warn!` when the budget is spent.

#### F479 · medium · effort M · No failure or mutation logging at the command boundary: write-command files have zero tracing and the UiError conversion is a pure map
`crates/azapptoolkit-dto/src/lib.rs:139` · partially (verifier: logging `%err` in the `From` impl would write raw response bodies against DEVELOPMENT.md:348-351, and a `warn!` there would fire for probe errors callers recover from)
**Problem.** `ui_error_from!` is a pure map (dto has no tracing dependency), the Graph transport logs no terminal failure, and the frontend sink only toasts. The per-file counts are exact: remediation.rs, restore.rs, expose_api.rs, app_roles.rs, credentials.rs, owners.rs, federated.rs, authentication.rs, exchange/grants.rs, scope_group.rs and aap_migration.rs have zero `tracing::` calls across 47 commands, and the whole backend has one `error!` and 65 `warn!`s, none on the common failure path. Even successful destructive writes (bulk delete, disable sign-in, AAP migration, DR restore) leave no local record of which object was touched when.
**Proposal.** Log at the terminal-failure site in each transport where status and request-id are known (see F476), keep the `From` impl pure, and reserve `warn!` for the command boundary such as `dispatch` and `run_bulk_seq`. Add a ring buffer of the last N (code, status, timestamp) for the diagnostics bundle, and `info!(command, %tenant_id, %object_id)` in the single-sourced write cores for destructive writes; pin with a capturing-subscriber test that no body text appears.

#### F476 · medium · effort M · Graph/ARM/Key Vault transports send no client-request-id and never capture request-id/Date; Microsoft-support correlation is impossible for every non-Exchange failure
`crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/transport.rs:586` · confirmed
**Problem.** The Graph transport builds only Authorization, ConsistencyLevel, Prefer and Content-Type and reads back only Retry-After and WWW-Authenticate; ARM and Key Vault read only Retry-After, and batch.rs:52-58 reads only `retry-after` from sub-response headers. A repo-wide grep for client-request-id, request-id or x-ms-request-id hits only the Exchange client, which mints, captures and logs them. Microsoft's Graph guidance says these headers are required when reporting issues to support, and DEVELOPMENT.md:348-351 already states "log the ui_code/request id, never a raw body", a policy only Exchange meets.
**Proposal.** Mint a v4 GUID per logical request and send `client-request-id`; on non-success capture `request-id`/`client-request-id`/`Date` (ARM `x-ms-request-id` and `x-ms-correlation-request-id`, KV `x-ms-request-id`, batch sub-response maps) and append them to the error detail the way Exchange's `compose_error_detail` does. Emit one `tracing::warn!(method, path_without_query, status, request_id, ..)` at terminal failure and pin with a wiremock test. The verifier notes `rand` is a src-tauri-only dependency, so moving `new_v4_guid` into core adds it there.

- **F486 · Bug template tells reporters to read the version 'From Help → About', but the Help submenu is empty and exists only on macOS; the version actually lives in the account menu** — `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml:33` · confirmed. Windows and Linux have no native menu at all, macOS's Help submenu is deliberately empty and About sits under the app-name menu, while the version renders in the account menu (shell.rs:468-469). Point the description at the account menu (click the tenant name, top right), or at "Copy diagnostics" if F481 lands.
- **F477 · README says a failed update install is logged, but `perform_update` failures are never logged** — `README.md:253` · partially (verifier: check failures do reach the file because the plugin's `log::error!` lines are bridged by the `LogTracer` that `try_init` installs; only `app.updater()` construction and every `perform_update` failure are unlogged). updater.rs has zero `tracing::` calls and the plugin has no log call in download or install, so a signature or download failure leaves no line and README's "or install" half is unmet. Add `tracing::warn!(error = %e, "update install failed")` at the `map_err(updater_err)` sites in `perform_update` and for the `app.updater()` failure, without duplicating the plugin's check-failure line.
- **F485 · The security audit, the app's flagship long run, writes no start/finish summary line (duration, totals, coverage gaps, cancelled), unlike the site sweep** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/audit.rs:380` · confirmed. `run_audit` has no entry line and no `Instant::now()`, and its only completion-adjacent line is the conditional "audit completed with unscored principals" warn, so a clean, cancelled or degraded run leaves zero lines; sharepoint.rs:964-970 shows the one-line idiom. Add `info!(%tenant_id, "audit started")` and a final `info!(total, scored, unscored, degraded, cancelled, elapsed_secs, "audit complete")`, and the same pair for `backup_tenant`, `restore_tenant` and `run_bulk_seq`.
- **F484 · Startup banner records only version and log_dir; OS/arch, cloud, configured tenant id and build profile are missing, so the first thing support needs is not in the log** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:368` · confirmed. state.rs:247-256 warns only when the tenant or client id is unset and never records the resolved value or whether env, settings.json or the baked value won, so a log tail cannot self-describe. Extend the banner with `os`, `arch` and `debug = cfg!(debug_assertions)`, and after `AppState::new` log cloud, tenant id and the source of each id; the auth crate already logs the tenant id at service/mod.rs:534, so there is no new exposure.
- **F483 · `target = "azapptoolkit::graph"` / `"azapptoolkit::cache"` are recorded as a field named `target`, not as the event target, while both layers hide the real target with `with_target(false)`** — `crates/azapptoolkit-graph/src/client/service_principals.rs:189` · confirmed. In tracing 0.1.44 the override is spelled `target: expr`; the `target = "..."` form at 12 sites (the verifier counts 12, not 13) falls into the field arm, so the intended `azapptoolkit::cache=debug` filter matches nothing, and both fmt layers set `with_target(false)` so the file carries no module attribution at all. Fix the spelling at every site (the auth crate already uses `target: "auth"`), turn `with_target(true)` on for the file layer only, and add a grep-based repo invariant.

### Desktop inline test oracles (gap-desktop-inline-test-oracles)

#### F459 · medium · effort M · progress.rs's runtime generic is the wrong seam; the ProgressSink trait bulk.rs proved works is private and re-invented as a closure in backup.rs, leaving 18 AppHandle-bound emit sites that keep audit/backup/restore/sharepoint/tester untestable
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/progress.rs:21` · confirmed
**Problem.** `emit_progress<R: tauri::Runtime, P>` is generic over the runtime and its doc promises `MockRuntime`, but `tauri = { version = "2", features = [] }` and bulk.rs:40-45 record that the `test` feature was deliberately dropped, so the generic is unusable in tests. bulk.rs solved this with a private `trait ProgressSink` that made `run_bulk_seq` testable; backup.rs re-invented the seam as `report: &(dyn Fn(usize) + Sync)`. `emit_progress(` has 18 call sites across 7 files, and concrete `&AppHandle` parameters sit on backup.rs:512, 679, 941 and restore.rs:921, which is the structural root of the untestable bodies in F450, F451 and F454. Nothing is silently excluded today; there are no feature-gated tests.
```rust
pub(crate) fn emit_progress<R: tauri::Runtime, P: Serialize + Clone>(
    app_handle: &AppHandle<R>, event: &'static str, payload: P,
```
**Proposal.** Lift `ProgressSink` into progress.rs as `pub(crate) trait ProgressSink { fn emit<P: Serialize + Clone>(&self, event: &'static str, payload: P); }` with the `AppHandle<R>` impl and a `#[cfg(test)] Recorder`. Change the backup, restore, audit, sweep and tester helpers to `&impl ProgressSink` (not `dyn`, since the method is generic), delete bulk's local trait and backup's closure, and reword the progress.rs doc per F015.

#### F450 · medium · effort M · audit.rs tests the cache guard exhaustively but nothing tests what feeds `degraded`; score_one's swallowed SP lookup (F023) returns a clean Ok(AuditItem) the guard cannot see
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/audit.rs:1664` · confirmed
**Problem.** `only_a_complete_undegraded_run_is_cacheable` proves the predicate for all eight combinations, but the only `degraded` producers are the tenant-wide gaps and the two run-level pushes at audit.rs:381 and 389, none inside `score_one`. At audit.rs:1490-1497 a failed lean SP lookup is logged and becomes `None`, so the app is scored as holding nothing, `degraded` stays empty and the guard is fed a lie. The verifier confirms reachability: `seed_lean_sps_from_index` seeds only positive matches and is capped, so apps outside the index hit a live `GET /servicePrincipals?$filter=appId eq` that can fail on its own. `ScoreCtx` is plain Arcs and maps, so a wiremock producer test is feasible.
```rust
Err(err) => {
    tracing::warn!(app = %app.display_name, ?err, "audit: SP lookup failed");
    None
```
**Proposal.** Make `score_one` report coverage, returning `(AuditItem, Option<AuditCoverageGap>)` or pushing to a shared `Mutex<Vec<AuditCoverageGap>>` on the Err arm. Add `a_failed_sp_lookup_degrades_the_run_and_blocks_the_cache` with wiremock returning 503 for the SP filter, and assert the gap is reported and `run_is_cacheable(false, false, &degraded)` is false.

#### F451 · medium · effort M · backup.rs's only wiremock test constructs a SessionDead it never asserts and fixtures a 500 that can never latch it; Pass 3 and the restore passes are AppHandle-bound so F039/F038/F037 had no test that could exist
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/backup.rs:1104` · confirmed
**Problem.** The test at backup.rs:1097-1104 passes `&SessionDead::new()` and never reads `is_dead()`; its failing fixture is a 500 (Transient), so `note_graph_failures` is never observed latching anywhere in the suite. `backup_managed_identities` (675-683) takes `&AppHandle`, has no `SessionDead`, and `unwrap_or_default`s its per-item errors with no `skipped` push, so the documented `kind == "managedIdentity"` (dto/backup.rs:78) is emitted by no code path. On the restore side `list_managed_identities().await.unwrap_or_default()` turns a failed destination read into "every MI not yet recreated", and `restore_enterprise_app` folds `Err` and `Ok(None)` into one message; restore.rs has seven pure tests and no wiremock.
**Proposal.** Add `a_reauth_fatal_read_latches_the_session` (an auth-failure shape whose `ui_code()` is `refresh_missing`, assert `is_dead()`; a 500 must not latch). Give `backup_managed_identities` the `report` closure and `&SessionDead` seam (or F459's sink) and test that a failed appRoleAssignments read yields `SkippedObject{kind:"managedIdentity"}`. Test `restore_managed_identities` and `restore_enterprise_app` over wiremock so a 503 destination read produces a reason distinguishing "could not be read" from "not found".

#### F455 · medium · effort S · grant_single_permission_core, grant_admin_consent_core and create_application_core already take &GraphClient, so F001/F004 partial-write regression tests need no AppState refactor; they were simply never written
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permissions.rs:618` · confirmed
**Problem.** `grant_single_permission_core(client: &GraphClient, ..)` PATCHes the manifest at 643 and then `?`s on `ensure_service_principal` and `resolve_resource_sp`, returning `Err` after a landed write despite the comment at 646-647; `grant_admin_consent_core` creates the SP and then `try_join(..).await?`, losing `sp_created`. `create_application_core` creates the app then `?`s on SP creation and `add_password`, and its wrapper does not augment the error with the new object id as sso/mod.rs:441-447 does. permissions.rs has 17 `#[test]`s and zero `MockServer`; applications/mod.rs tests are export-only. The seam exists, and the same file already fixed this shape elsewhere with `(outcome, Option<UiError>)` at 887-890.
**Proposal.** Add wiremock tests copying credentials.rs:456-597: manifest PATCH 204 then `POST /servicePrincipals` 503 must yield `(GrantResult{declared:true,..}, Some(err))` with the PATCH in `received_requests()`; SP POST 201 then appRoleAssignments 503 for admin consent; `POST /applications` 201 then SP 503 must name `obj-1` in the error. The verifier notes graph's `make_client` is `pub(crate)` inside the graph crate, so the tests need a local constructor (see F457).

#### F448 · medium · effort S · Every plan_redundant_removals test passes `|_, _| false`, so the fail-closed veto branch that F022 breaks is dead in the suite; a for_test handler round trip would hit F022 by construction
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/remediation.rs:577` · confirmed
**Problem.** All six `plan_redundant_removals` call sites in the tests (577, 593, 605, 624, 637, 648) pass a constant-false veto, so the branch at 256-264 that filters vetoed broaders and then `continue`s with no `skipped.push` is never exercised. That contradicts the in-code contract at 373-377 and audit-findings-and-remediation.md:238-240, both of which say a vetoed value "shows up in `skipped`". `AppState::for_test` has no tenant context, so `exchange_client` returns `not_signed_in`, `mail_scopes` is empty and `broader_is_confined` is true for every Graph mail permission; a handler-level test would report `removed: [], skipped: []` for Mail.ReadWrite plus Mail.Read and expose F022 at once.
```rust
let (plan, skipped) = plan_redundant_removals(&required, &idx, &granted, |_, _| false);
```
**Proposal.** Add `plan_reports_a_narrower_whose_only_cover_is_vetoed_as_skipped` (veto `|_, b| b == "Mail.ReadWrite"`, expect an empty plan and `skipped == ["Mail.Read"]`; it fails today) and `a_veto_never_plans_a_removal` with `|_, _| true`. Split `remediate_remove_redundant_permissions` into a `_core(&AppState, ..)` and drive it with `for_test` plus wiremock, asserting no PATCH or DELETE is sent.

#### F449 · medium · effort M · EntraReach and the Option-returning grant readers have no 'read failed' representation, so no fixture can express the F055/F056/F057 inputs; the 15 synthesize tests are exhaustive over a type that already lost the information
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/permission_tester.rs:177` · confirmed
**Problem.** `orgwide_mailbox_grant` returns `Option<Vec<String>>` via `.ok()??`, so a Graph error and "no SP" are indistinguishable, and `EntraReach` (177-192) has no unreadable variant; at 404-411 `None` becomes `NotHeld`, a definitive `no_access`. `sharepoint_grants_held -> Option<HeldSharePointGrants>` has the same shape, and its own doc says the caller "must then report unknown", yet `test_site_access` (1091-1101) returns a definitive "holds no organization-wide SharePoint grant" whenever no entry is found, regardless of `held`. Every `synthesize` test uses one of the five variants, so F056 and F055 are unreachable from any test by construction, and the module doc's promise that a missing right "surfaces as an unknown verdict" is a contract the type cannot express.
**Proposal.** Return `Result<Option<Vec<String>>, GraphError>` from `orgwide_mailbox_grant` (or add `EntraReach::Unreadable`) and test that `synthesize(.., &EntraReach::Unreadable, ..)` yields "unknown" with `has_access == false`. Extract `site_verdict(held, hit, label)` per F068 and test `(None, None)` yields "unknown"; extract `discover_mailbox_candidates(graph_assigned, ews_assigned)` for F057 and assert an EWS-only principal appears with `held == [EWS_FULL_ACCESS_AS_APP]`.

#### F452 · medium · effort S · SiteSweepResult fixture and DTO have no truncation dimension, so the F059 regression test ('a capped sweep is not complete and not cached') cannot be written until the type carries it
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sharepoint.rs:1234` · confirmed
**Problem.** `list_all_sites(MAX_SITES_PER_SWEEP)` silently `truncate(max)`s at 5,000 and returns `Ok`, but neither `SiteSweepResult`, the DTO's `is_complete()` (`!cancelled && sites_failed == 0`) nor the cache guard at sharepoint.rs:988 has a truncation field. At over 5,000 sites an empty per-app list would read as "no grants" under `is_complete()`, and the fixture at 1234-1241 cannot express the case. The audit's sibling `list_applications_all` already returns `(Vec, truncated)` and `AuditExportCoverage.truncated` is table-tested at audit.rs:1664.
**Proposal.** Add `#[serde(default)] truncated: bool` to `SiteSweepResult` and `AppSiteAccessDto` (carried by `from_sweep`), fold it into `is_complete`, make `list_all_sites` return `(Vec<Site>, bool)`, extract `sweep_is_cacheable(cancelled, sites_failed, truncated)` beside the cache write and table-test all eight combinations.

#### F456 · medium · effort S · F054's two halves are each tested but never joined: targets_from_declared is proven to include EWS while the only resolver-level test feeds a non-mail permission, so the hardcoded Graph resource in resolve_mail_scopes was never exercised with full_access_as_app
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/exchange/tests.rs:200` · confirmed
**Problem.** `declared_targets_span_graph_and_the_legacy_ews_scope` proves the declared targets include EWS `full_access_as_app`, and the only `resolve_mail_scopes*` test (213-219) feeds `User.Read.All` against an unreachable base and asserts `Ok(empty)`. Nobody wrote the inverse, so the hardcoded `MICROSOFT_GRAPH_APP_ID` filter at mail_scopes.rs:108-116, which drops the EWS value and short-circuits an EWS-only set to `Ok(empty)`, was never exercised. The verifier notes the target must be the enriched `resolve_mail_scopes`, since `resolve_mail_scopes_audit_cached` legitimately sees Graph-only values.
**Proposal.** Add `the_ews_scope_is_resolved_not_short_circuited`: same unreachable-base client, `&[EWS_FULL_ACCESS_AS_APP.to_string()]`, assert `Err` (a probe was attempted) rather than `Ok(empty)`, with `["Mail.Read"]` as the positive control. After the F054 fix, a wiremock EXO base returning `Scoped` asserts the map contains the `full_access_as_app` key.

#### F447 · medium · effort S · claims.rs round-trip test pins the F070 wire-format bug: it asserts TransformationID is emitted and ID is absent, with no Microsoft-documented fixture
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/claims.rs:442` · confirmed
**Problem.** claims.rs:440-443 asserts a transformation-sourced entry emits `TransformationID` and `entry.get("ID").is_none()`, and 453-455 round-trips it through the same codec, a tautology for a wire format. Microsoft's documented sample is `{"Source":"transformation","ID":"DataJoin","TransformationId":"JoinTheData",...}` with `OutputClaims[].ClaimTypeReferenceId == "DataJoin"`, so both keys are required and the current shape can never resolve the output claim; the parser also accepts only the `TransformationID` casing. sso/mod.rs:1907-1912 records the same lesson for `customKeyIdentifier`: tests "asserted our assumption, not Graph's behaviour". The F070 fix will fail this test and look like a regression.
```rust
assert_eq!(entry["TransformationID"], "JoinUpnDomain");
assert!(entry.get("ID").is_none());
```
**Proposal.** Replace both assertions with `a_transformation_entry_matches_microsofts_documented_shape` using Microsoft's definition string as the fixture: parse yields `id == Some("DataJoin")` and a new `transformation_id == Some("JoinTheData")`, build emits both keys (read case-tolerantly), and the OutputClaims reference resolves. The verifier adds that the PowerShell sample spells `ClaimsTransformations` while the Graph doc uses `ClaimsTransformation`, so read that key tolerantly too. Land with F070's DTO change.

#### F454 · medium · effort M · sso/mod.rs: the cert-lifetime gate is table-tested but the OIDC secret lifetime has no gate and no twin test (F074); F071/F075 sit in State-bound bodies with zero wiremock coverage although configure_saml/configure_oidc already take &GraphClient
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:356` · confirmed
**Problem.** `resolve_cert_lifetime_days` is table-tested including `u32::MAX`, but `input.secret_lifetime_days.unwrap_or(180)` at 356-357 has no gate; the wizard passes any parsed u32, and graph's `add_password` silently turns an absurd value into 180 days while a merely large one (36500) goes to Graph as-is. sso/mod.rs has 21 `#[test]`s, zero `tokio::test` and zero `MockServer`, although `configure_saml` and `configure_oidc` take `&GraphClient` and are wiremock-drivable today, so F071 (the `if let Ok(existing)` swallow at 1642) and F075 (`Err => warn; None` at 261-266) have no oracle. The verifier adds that `create_application_core` has the identical ungated `initial_secret_lifetime_days.unwrap_or(180)`.
**Proposal.** Add `resolve_secret_lifetime_days(Option<u32>) -> Result<u32, UiError>` (reject above 730 like the cert gate, or clamp like keyvault.rs:85), mirror the cert table test, and use it at both sites. Wiremock `configure_oidc` for `endDateTime <= now+730d`; make `configure_saml` return `warnings: Vec<String>` and test a 400 on the notification-email PATCH and a 403 on the claims policy POST; add `set_claims_mapping_core` and test that a 503 list yields `Err` and a found policy yields both DELETEs.

#### F460 · medium · effort S · set_saml_urls, set_oidc_redirect_uris and set_claims_mapping bust both tenant-wide indexes (invalidate_app_lists) for detail-only writes; the F026 mis-tier in sso, unnoticed because no SSO handler has a test
`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/sso/mod.rs:1728` · confirmed
**Problem.** All three commands follow an in-place application PATCH or an SP policy assignment with `invalidate_app_lists`, which drops `sp_index`, `app_name_index`, the search corpus, the MI list and the credential-expiry list and forces the multi-second re-enumeration cache.rs:134-147 documents. Nothing those writes change lives in the list tier: the list row DTO carries no identifierUris or redirect fields, the enterprise DTO no SSO fields, and no audit rule reads them, while the cached `ApplicationDetail` does embed them. app_roles.rs:336-339 uses `invalidate_app_details` for the same kind of PATCH, and no comment justifies the list tier at any of the three sites.
```rust
client.patch_application_web(&object_id, &body).await?;
invalidate_app_lists(&state.cache, &tenant_id);
```
**Proposal.** Replace the three calls with `invalidate_app_details(&state.cache, &tenant_id)`. Once the SSO bodies gain `_core` seams, add the credentials.rs-shaped test that after a successful PATCH the detail row is gone while `sp_index_hit` and `app_name_index_hit` still return `Some`; consider a repo_invariants rule that `invalidate_app_lists` may follow only a create, delete, `ensure_service_principal` or rename in the same function.

- **F453 · sp_index_tests is a weaker copy of its app_name_index twin: the untyped-get test asserts only `.is_none()` and the invalidate_app_lists case is missing** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/applications/cache.rs:502` · confirmed. The SP twin's untyped-get test asserts only `.is_none()`, which the app_name twin's own comment (400-406) says cannot tell a miss from a poison-path eviction, and `invalidate_app_lists_drops_the_index` exists only for app_name; `invalidate_app_lists` does drop `sp_index_key` today, so this is a ratchet. Copy the `expect` plus `Arc::ptr_eq` assertions and the drop test into `sp_index_tests`, or fold both modules into one macro-instantiated table.
- **F457 · Handler-test fixtures diverge on the mock base-URL convention and duplicate builders/clocks; a `commands/test_support.rs` would give the proposed handler tests one home** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/backup.rs:1080` · partially (verifier: wiremock client construction exists in only two command files, the calc-payload file list is off by one each way, and "~20 copies" overstates today's duplication). `AppState::for_test` roots the client at `{base}/v1.0` while backup.rs uses `with_base_url(.., server.uri())`, so a path copied between the two handler tests 404s, and `app_json` is byte-identical to graph's `sample_apps_json`. Add `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate) mod test_support` exporting `mock_state()`, `FIXED_NOW`, the `sp`/`app`/`manifest` builders and one copied `sample_app_json()`, and leave the per-domain CSV tests alone.
- **F458 · `resolve_password_window` and `generate_self_signed` return bare `String` errors, forcing substring oracles; the CA `ca_unavailable` collapse is a documented shared design and should stay** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/conditional_access.rs:249` · partially (verifier: the CA collapse is documented at conditional_access.rs:138-142, the frontend branches only on the code, and graph_err already tests the license-vs-consent split, so that half stays). credentials.rs:375/394 and cert.rs:425 discriminate causes only by copy text, so a wording edit breaks the suite while a swapped classification need not. Give the two helpers small error enums (`EndNotAfterStart | LifetimeOverCap | EmptyCommonName | ValidityOutOfRange`) with a `code()` mapped into `UiError`, match on variants, and delete the duplicate prose test in conditional_access.rs rather than adding codes.
- **F461 · managed_identity.rs tests stop at ARM scope-string classification; the per-subscription flatten that duplicates management-group assignments (F072) has no test and no seam** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/managed_identity.rs:251` · confirmed. The inline `flat_map` at 251-258 emits one row per (subscription, assignment), and ARM's per-subscription `roleAssignments` query returns management-group-inherited assignments in every subscription, so one MG assignment inflates the table N times while `scope_level` is tested to label exactly such rows. Extract `flatten_assignments(per_sub)` that dedupes on `RoleAssignment.id` (a full resource id embedding its own scope) and test that two subscriptions returning the same `ra-1` plus one `ra-2` yield exactly two rows.

---

## Appendix A: refuted and downgraded claims

What was checked and cleared, so the reader knows the verification was adversarial and not a rubber stamp.

### Refuted (excluded from every section)

- **F205 · `Subscription` drops the `state` field, so Disabled/Warned/Deleted subscriptions are fanned out to and their failures counted as coverage gaps** (`crates/azapptoolkit-arm/src/models.rs:14`). The premise that reads against a Disabled or Deleted subscription fail is contradicted by Microsoft Learn: Disabled, Warned, Expired and Past Due subscriptions all allow GET (only PUT, PATCH and POST are blocked), and Deleted is not a persistent listing state. The fan-outs therefore pay no failed round trips for non-Enabled subscriptions and `skipped` is not inflated. Worse, the proposed partition on `state != "Enabled"` would hide genuine role assignments and vaults on disabled subscriptions, grants that become live again on reactivation, which is a regression for a security-posture tool.

### Downgraded or narrowed by the judges

Each id keeps its entry in the sections; the clause says how the judges weighed it.

- **F140**: the retry-loop mechanism is real, but the headline harm (a duplicate role assignment surviving removal) was refuted by the verifier, since Exchange rejects the duplicate and removal sweeps every assignment; folded into F164's false-failure framing.
- **F251**: the `graph_mail_role` half is a documented deliberate gap (AAP parity); only the advisory-membership half is a defect, reported under F151.
- **F393**: degraded-with-findings is disclosed on the default pane and the unmarked case needs more than 10,000 app registrations; kept as a one-line S fix inside the coverage theme.
- **F266**: the single-app delete dialog already says soft-delete; only the bulk bar copy is wrong, and the "Recently deleted" view is a feature, not a defect.
- **F001**: narrowed by the verifier; the partial-write shape is real but smaller than first reported.
- **F043**: partially confirmed low; regenerating a secret for an expired credential is arguably intended restore semantics and the report already lists them.
- **F216**: several proposed pedantic lints are nursery or fight Leptos's by-value style; keep a non-gating `just clippy-pedantic` recipe and the tiny low-false-positive subset.
- **F232**: HTTP/2 being off is confirmed but unmeasured; an experiment to run, not a finding.
- **F214 and F226**: the `$crate` re-export idiom for thiserror is refuted (thiserror hard-codes `::thiserror::__private` paths); reduced to a manifest comment plus a cargo-machete ignore, folded into F212 and F213.
- **F220**: rests on an open, unconfirmed upstream issue; a watch item next to F219.
- **F130**: the finder itself calls the `deduped()` clone cost negligible at current throughput.
- **F257**: the Key Vault api-version retires on 2027-02-27; a dated maintenance ticket, nothing is wrong today.
- **F272**: single-tenant-per-instance is a documented invariant; a feature request rather than a defect.
- **F305**: stale `.gitignore` template entries are harmless noise.
- **F241**: AGENTS.md sitting 2 bytes under its budget is a hint to dedupe when a rule is next added, not a defect.
- **F109**: text-only message injection on an abortable sign-in; kept as a low hardening line under auth.
- **F003**: sign-out sweeps the cache, so the exposure is the mid-flight switch and dead-session window only; low.
- **F286**: CSP over-allowlisting is harmless for a webview that never contacts those hosts; duplicate of F044 and F156.
- **F295**: high severity but developer-only (`just setup` on a fresh clone); no operator impact, so it belongs in tooling, not the headline.
- **F420**: an enhancement to demo variety, not a violated rule.
- **Feature requests moved to the product backlog rather than the headline**: F259 (appCredentialSignInActivities), F260 (app management policies), F264 (servicePrincipalRiskDetections), F265 (requestSignatureVerification), F267 (#109 posture snapshot; large and product-owned), F274 (tenant consent settings tile), F275 (SCIM job control), F277 (headless mode), F283 (bulk create from CSV), F392 (operator-supplied PFX).
- **Duplicates collapsed into one entry each**: F284 into F105; F139 into F036; F245 into F014; F091 into F019; F143 into F047; F211 into F119; F210 into F118 and F121; F286 and F156 into F044; F362 into F313; F358 into F331; F445 into F046; F351 into F335; F294 into F204; F224 into F209; F212 absorbs F173; F226 into F214; F225 into F213; F428 into F042; F276 into F264; F255 into F163; F287 into F252; F431 and F346 into F093; F379 into F319; F289 into F109; F401 partially into F127.
- **Doc-drift and a11y groups**: the roughly forty documentation-drift items (F014, F015, F030, F051, F067, F082, F099 to F101, F120, F131, F132, F137, F144, F145, F169, F175, F191, F237 to F246, F249, F282, F302 to F304, F344, F356, F409, F421, F446) are worth one sweep PR, with F239 and F169 the two that could mislead a maintainer into reintroducing a bug; the accessibility group (F312 to F328, F349, F360 to F363, F386, F438) is real and shippable but sits below correctness in the ranking and is presented as its own sub-section.

## Appendix B: minor nits

Every minor nit the slices recorded, grouped by slice, verbatim apart from punctuation (each carries its own `file:line`). These are below the finding bar: wording, comments, tiny inconsistencies and one-line tidy-ups. 302 items across 33 slices.

### backend-crates/core-audit (7)

- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/scoring.rs:160: "Mixed credential status: {names} are expired but {n} credentials are active" reads as "expired are expired but 1 credentials are active" in the snapshot (tests.rs:1300); pluralize and rephrase.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/permissions.rs:162-165: HIGH_RISK_DELEGATED_PERMISSIONS cites `Constants.ps1:104-130`, the union of the two app-permission spans (104-115, 123-130); looks copy-pasted rather than a delegated-list citation.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/permissions.rs:83-92: "Both scored ZERO." past-tense change narrative inside a constant table; move to the CHANGELOG/commit and keep the table comment declarative.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/scoring.rs:1009-1012: orphaned refactor note "(No resource-stripped value list any more: ...)" in `score_application`; it documents a removed line, not the code that remains.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/types.rs:562: `issue` module doc says `emitted_issue_markers_are_stable` "asserts the scorer still emits each"; the test covers 10 of the 17 markers.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/scoring.rs:1112-1118: `join_refs<S: AsRef<str>>` is now used by exactly one caller (`rule_high_risk_delegated`); could be inlined or `join_values`/`join_names` could share it.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/audit/types.rs:275-282: `mail_scopes` doc says "every read goes through `AppPermissions::is_scoped`" while the single read is actually `scope_mechanism` (line 385); point the doc at the real gate.

### backend-crates/core-infra-a (10)

- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/cache.rs:1046-1051: the doc comment for `release_watch` ("Drops one reference to a watch...") sits directly above `peek_watch`, which then has its own one-line doc; the two blocks are merged and `release_watch` (line 1058) is undocumented.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/cache.rs:793-800: `store_if_current` returns `true` when `store` returned `None` (caching disabled / serialize failure) although the doc says it returns `false` when the store was skipped; return `stamp.is_some()` on the success arm (callers currently ignore the bool).
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/cache.rs:697: the poisoned-entry eviction in `get` uses `remove(key)` after the bucket lock was dropped in `lookup`, so it can evict an entry a concurrent writer just replaced; `lookup` could hand back the stamp and use the existing `remove_if_stamp` for consistency with `store_if_current`.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/cache.rs:189: `Bucket::touch` allocates `key.to_string()` on every cache hit under the bucket lock; storing LRU keys as `Arc<str>` (shared with `entries`) would make the hot-path row a refcount clone.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/http_retry.rs:61-72: `parse_retry_after_seconds(Some("0"))` → `retry_after_millis(0)` = 0 ms, so a `Retry-After: 0` yields MAX_RETRIES back-to-back attempts with no backoff or jitter; consider flooring the explicit header at e.g. 500 ms.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/token.rs:63-73: `From<String>` and `From<&str>` for `TokenError` have no users (every site uses `TokenError::new`/`opaque` explicitly) and exist only to let a `?`/`.into()` silently flatten to `token_error`, the anti-pattern the type's doc warns about; remove them.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/cache/tests.rs:53 and crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/defaults.rs:217: `use super::*;` appears mid-module after tests that already rely on it; move to the top of each test module.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/settings.rs:404-429 and crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/private_file.rs:95-112: two identical hand-rolled `TempDir` helpers (the no-`tempfile` decision is documented); a `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate) mod test_support` would hold one copy.
- crates/azapptoolkit-core/src/cache.rs:42: comment "matches enum declaration order" is the only thing tying `ALL` to `idx()`; see the CacheKind::idx finding.
